## [Tactus: A 3D-Knit Sensing Glove for Natural Dexterous Manipulation Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39076) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers present Tactus, a thin, conformal 3D-knit glove that integrates capacitive fingertip tactile sensors with IMU-fused pose tracking to collect naturalistic hand manipulation data. The glove achieves a minimum detectable force of 0.59 N (1.6–2.7x lower than commercial baselines) and maintains 11.0 mm fingertip keypoint error during heavy occlusion, with the authors also introducing standardized benchmarking protocols for tactile force regression and hand pose estimation. Collecting high-fidelity human demonstrations with both contact force and hand pose is a key bottleneck for training dexterous robot manipulation policies and world models, and Tactus offers a wearable platform that preserves natural behavior while providing calibrated tactile and occlusion-resilient pose data. Its cross-platform benchmarking also enables objective comparison of tactile gloves, which could accelerate progress in embodied AI and wearable sensing. Tactus features a 1.5 mm fingertip stackup, capacitive sensors placed at manipulation-relevant regions informed by empirical contact studies, and a documented calibration pipeline for cross-glove consistency (0.87 cross-participant consistency) plus per-session subject-dependent correction. The authors demonstrate that the combined signals support object property estimation, slip detection, and representative recording of hand-object interaction, though the work is a preprint without community discussion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Dexterous robot manipulation policies and world models are often trained via imitation learning from human demonstrations, requiring accurate hand pose and contact force data. Capacitive tactile sensors detect force through changes in capacitance and are commonly used in robotics and wearables, while IMUs (inertial measurement units) provide motion data that can complement vision-based tracking when the hand occludes the object. However, existing wearable systems struggle to combine calibrated fingertip force sensing with occlusion-resilient tracking in a form factor that does not interfere with natural manipulation.

## [FoldScape predicts protein structures at any folding stage from sequence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04287) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FoldScape is a sequence-conditioned deep learning framework that generates protein conformational ensembles at specified folding fractions Q, trained on approximately one million conformations from 10,000 protein sequences. It reconstructs 3D Cα chains via hierarchical sliding-window regressions, chain-level assembly, and a rectified-flow decoder, achieving median conformational-space coverage of 0.989, 0.722, and 0.877 along radius of gyration, fraction of native contacts Q, and secondary-structure fraction, versus 0.05, 0.05, and 0.10 for seed-varied AlphaFold 3 ensembles. This work extends sequence-based structure prediction beyond a single folded endpoint to controllable conformational-ensemble generation across the entire folding landscape, which could significantly impact structural biology and drug discovery by making unfolded and intermediate states accessible without expensive molecular dynamics simulations. FoldScape combines coarse-grained folding and all-atom mechanical-unfolding trajectories, and its reconstruction accuracy remains approximately stable across folding stages; stride-one crop-and-superpose inference extends predictions to chains several times longer than the single-pass limit. Analyses of mechanical-unfolding trajectories reveal residue-resolved pathways and reproducible extension-conditioned observables across pulling velocities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Protein structure prediction has largely focused on native conformations, leaving the continuum of unfolded states and folding intermediates difficult to access. The fraction of native contacts Q is a common metric for folding progress, and coarse-grained models are often used to simulate folding at biologically relevant timescales. Rectified flow is a generative modeling framework that learns straight flows between distributions, enabling efficient sampling.

A technical blog post demonstrates that ULC, an obscure transform audio codec originally built for the 16 MHz Game Boy Advance, is a near-perfect fit for the Nintendo 64's Reality Signal Processor (RSP). The author ported the decoder to the RSP in about 1,400 lines of MIPS assembly, achieving 44.1 kHz stereo playback at near-Opus quality and bitrate with only about four times the complexity of ADPCM. This matters because the N64 lacks a dedicated audio chip and its cartridge space and 93 MHz CPU made recorded audio nearly impossible, forcing most games into tracker-style sample playback. A codec that fits the RSP's tight 4 KB instruction memory could enable high-quality streaming audio in homebrew games and retro-development projects, and the write-up offers a rare, detailed case study in embedded codec optimization. The decoder fills almost the entire 4 KB RSP instruction memory (IMEM), which raises a practical concern about whether games can still use the RSP for graphics work. The codec's quality and bitrate are described as near-Opus, but at roughly four times the complexity of ADPCM, and the demo's seemingly poor vocal quality is actually present in the original source track.

**Background**: The Nintendo 64 uses a software-driven audio model: instead of a dedicated audio chip, its Reality Signal Processor (RSP) executes microcode to process audio commands, which are then converted to analog signals by the Audio Interface. The RSP has only 4 KB of instruction memory, so audio microcode must be extremely compact. ULC is a transform codec originally designed for the resource-constrained Game Boy Advance, making it an intriguing candidate for the similarly constrained N64.

## [Blog Post Argues for Work-or-Time Based Flushing Over Line Buffering](https://nullprogram.com/blog/2026/11/05/) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A blog post on nullprogram.com argues that line-buffered output should be replaced by a work-or-time based flushing strategy, which flushes the buffer either after a certain amount of work or after a short idle period. The post sparked an 84-point Hacker News discussion with 84 comments adding practical caveats. This matters because line buffering is a common default in terminal applications and can cause performance bottlenecks or unbounded delays, so a better flushing strategy could improve responsiveness and efficiency for many CLI tools and systems programs. The proposed strategy combines work-based flushing (to batch work and amortize overhead) with time-based flushing (to bound worst-case latency in low-throughput environments). Commenters noted caveats such as progress-report overhead, the need for human-speed output, and the importance of keeping write chunks below PIPE_BUF to preserve atomicity when multiple processes share a pipe.

hackernews · zdw · Nov 5, 15:25 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50380268)

**Background**: Buffering is a technique where output is accumulated in memory and written to the underlying file or pipe in larger chunks, reducing the number of system calls. Line buffering flushes whenever a newline is encountered, which is common for interactive terminals but can be inefficient for high-volume output. Work-or-time based flushing is an alternative that flushes after a certain amount of data or after a timeout, balancing throughput and latency.

A key technical caveat raised was that aggregating multiple lines per write can break atomicity when output is interleaved to the same pipe unless chunks stay below PIPE_BUF.

## [Evidence-Gated Override Authority for LLM Reviewers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05390) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.05390) by Fuad Ali proposes an evidence-gated override authority for LLM reviewers, requiring a mechanically valid violation record and compliant repair before a reviewer can replace an upstream agent's decision. Across two open-weight models (Qwen and Mistral) and 192 tasks, the gate sharply reduced corruption of correct proposals while retaining most repairs of violations. This work reframes reviewer authority in multi-agent LLM systems as an institutional jurisdiction problem, showing that interface design can reduce harmful overrides but is not a model-independent safeguard. It has implications for AI governance and the design of reliable multi-agent review pipelines. The study uses 96 spatial-policy tasks and a 96-task portfolio-transfer benchmark; a prospective validation with an explicit retain/replace/escalate choice eliminated correct-proposal corruption for Qwen, but Mistral still replaced all 48 correct proposals, and Qwen declined every welfare-only repair that Mistral frequently attempted. The authors conclude that reviewer competence, action interface, and binding jurisdiction jointly determine both the correction domain and the corruption surface.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Multi-agent LLM systems often add a reviewer agent to check and improve an upstream agent's decisions, but reviewers can both fix errors and wrongly override correct decisions. This paper treats the question of when a reviewer is authorized to make its recommendation binding as a problem of institutional jurisdiction, analogous to legal authority. The proposed evidence gate requires the reviewer to produce a mechanically valid record of a binding-constraint violation and a compliant repair before its override takes effect.

## [Agent Harness Awareness lets tool-using LLMs see verified tool health before acting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05391) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.05391) introduces Agent Harness Awareness, a trust-separated design in which the execution harness supplies a small, fresh, verified state snapshot of tool and network health to the model before a consequential action. In a 216-decision ablation across GPT-5.6 Sol, Claude Sonnet 5, and Gemini 3.7 Flash, this verified tool-health state reduced mean observable policy regret by 96.9% (from 4,252 ms to 131 ms) and raised single-dispatch completion from 69.4% to 95.8%. The paper names and quantifies 'operational blindness' — the gap between what the harness knows about tool health and what the model sees — and shows that a small, verified telemetry snapshot can dramatically improve pre-dispatch decisions. This matters for anyone building or evaluating tool-using agents, since it suggests a lightweight architectural fix that improves reliability without changing the model itself. The design keeps measurement, enforcement, evaluation, and final dispatch authority in the host, so the model receives only a bounded, freshness-labeled view rather than raw control. A separate 27-decision live replication using local TCP/HTTP execution, OpenTelemetry client spans, and Toxiproxy-injected faults reduced mean regret from 8,956 ms to 0 ms and completed the live action in 9/9 model-by-fault cases versus 5/9 for the neutral control; runtime state, hardware pressure, economics, retry-aware recovery, and heterogeneous selection remain open extensions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: An agent harness is the runtime scaffolding that turns a language model into an agent: it drives model and tool calls, manages context and conversation state, applies approval policies, and dispatches actions. Tool descriptions tell the model what an action can do, but they typically do not reveal whether that action is currently healthy or suitable, even though the harness may know this through monitors, traces, and recent failures. Agent Harness Awareness addresses this information gap by giving the model a bounded, freshness-labeled, host-verified view of relevant execution state before a consequential action.

## [Conformal Prediction Enables Uncertainty-Aware Resource Orchestration for Agentic AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05392) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.05392) proposes Conformal Reservation for Orchestration (CRO), an online algorithm that uses conformal prediction to translate past execution traces into dispatch-time compute and bandwidth reservations for agentic LLM pipelines. Simulations show CRO is the only method that meets target probabilistic coverage across all risk levels while roughly halving resource waste compared to a Chebyshev-bound baseline. Agentic AI pipelines have unpredictable, heavy-tailed resource demands that existing schedulers cannot handle, causing either throttling or wasted capacity. CRO's distribution-free coverage guarantees offer a practical way to provision edge and cloud resources with probabilistic confidence, which could improve reliability and efficiency for LLM-based agent deployments. CRO provides finite-sample, distribution-free coverage guarantees via conformal prediction, and the paper formalizes the uncertainty-aware agentic orchestration problem as reserving resources and verifying deadline feasibility each with a prescribed probability. Evaluation is simulation-based over heterogeneous task mixes with multi-modal and heavy-tailed profiles, and the abstract does not report real-world deployment results.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Agentic AI systems built on large language models run as autonomous multi-step pipelines that call tools, update memory, and iterate until a task completes or a budget runs out. Unlike fixed workflows, their total compute, bandwidth, and latency needs are unknown at dispatch time and emerge stochastically from intermediate observations and token sampling, producing heavy-tailed, multi-modal distributions. Conformal prediction is a statistical framework that turns past observations into prediction sets with guaranteed coverage under minimal assumptions, making it suitable for online resource reservation without parametric demand models.

## [Challenge-Aware Recruitment Boosts Medical Multi-Agent Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05393) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint proposes a challenge-aware adaptive recruitment framework in which an external CHALLENGER agent inspects the evolving reasoning trace to detect factual or expertise gaps, triggering targeted retrieval or mid-discussion specialist recruitment. On the MEDAGENTSBENCH hard subset (862 questions across nine biomedical datasets), this approach improved accuracy on 8 of 9 datasets and raised GPT-4.1-mini MedQA accuracy from 46% to 54% when used for specialist recruitment. This work shows that the timing and rationale for invoking external resources—rather than just the resources themselves—are critical design choices in multi-agent medical reasoning, where multi-agent debate often fails to outperform strong single-model prompting. It offers a concrete mechanism that could inform the design of more reliable LLM-based clinical decision support systems. Ablations confirm that both retrieval timing and gap-specific query signals contribute to gains, and a live web-search experiment shows the timing advantage persists across retrieval backends. However, naively combining retrieval and specialist recruitment degrades performance, and self-triggered adaptation is unreliable compared to Challenger-triggered recruitment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Large language models perform well on standard medical question answering but struggle with complex reasoning tasks that require multi-step clinical reasoning. Multi-agent debate (MAD) is a paradigm where multiple LLM agents discuss and critique each other's reasoning to reach a better answer, while retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances LLMs by fetching relevant external knowledge. MEDAGENTSBENCH is a benchmark specifically designed to evaluate thinking models and agent frameworks on challenging medical questions.

## [ReScopeBench: Benchmarking Experience Lifecycles for Long-Lived AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05450) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ReScopeBench, a benchmark that evaluates how long-lived agents acquire, reuse, and revise structural and rule-based experience across 18 interactive environments. It uses an experience-first construction approach with silent environmental changes and tracks changes in saved experience across 20 models. This benchmark addresses a gap in evaluating how agents retain or revise experience in evolving environments, which is critical for developing robust long-lived agents that assist users across successive goals. It could influence future research on agent memory and adaptation, impacting AI agent developers and researchers. The benchmark distinguishes structural experience (how an environment is organized) from rule experience (reusable principles and constraints), and finds that while retained experience improves task performance, agents struggle to keep it current as environments change. Even when experience is correctly recorded, agents do not always complete tasks successfully, highlighting challenges in selective maintenance and effective use.

## [LLMs Can Find Scientific Articles but Struggle with Precision and Synthesis, Benchmark Shows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05459) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv study benchmarked six AI tools across four literature review stages using a published review of the Spanish plume weather pattern and its 102-article corpus. The best individual tool found 42 articles initially, but institutional library access boosted discovery to 82 for Codex and 79 for Claude Code, while three tools misclassified 40% to 79% of passing mentions as substantial. This study provides a realistic, end-to-end evaluation showing that while LLMs can assist with discovery and interpretation, they cannot replace expert judgment in evidence evaluation or synthesis. These findings are actionable for researchers adopting AI tools and for developers building literature review assistants, highlighting the critical role of institutional access. Given the complete corpus, three tools correctly identified all 18 articles discussing the Spanish plume substantially but incorrectly classified 40% to 79% of the 84 passing mentions as substantial. Expert blind evaluation of syntheses found limited insight, unsupported claims, quotation errors, and exaggerated consensus, and no single tool performed best across all tasks.

## [SlackBench: New Benchmark Tests AI Agents on Realistic Collaborative Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05512) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced SlackBench, a benchmark of 140 queries built from privacy-preserving reenactments of real academic research projects, requiring agents to jointly reason over Slack-style conversations, GitHub repositories, and Google Docs. Across 33 frontier models and agent harnesses, the best system scored around 80% while most agents scored below 66%. Existing agent benchmarks often rely on synthetic data or isolated tasks, missing the multi-party dynamics of real knowledge work where information is scattered across conversations, code, and evolving decisions. SlackBench addresses this gap and reveals substantial headroom for improvement, which could guide the development of more capable digital coworkers. The benchmark includes tasks such as heterogeneous-source agentic search, abstention on unanswerable or false-premise questions, goal-directed summarization, and circumstance inference over long conversations. The dataset is publicly available on Hugging Face, and the tasks are not reducible to static corpus question answering because agents must determine which conversations matter, traverse threads, inspect repository state, and synthesize evidence across artifacts.

SlackBench provides a reproducible environment that mimics these conditions using reenactments of real research projects, ensuring privacy while maintaining realism.

## [MA-SafeDiffuser extends SafeDiffuser to multi-agent safe planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05524) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MA-SafeDiffuser is a multi-agent extension of SafeDiffuser that equips diffusion-based trajectory planners with finite-time diffusion invariance guarantees for joint-agent safety specifications. It formalizes joint safe sets as intersections of per-agent and pairwise barrier sets, derives centralized and decentralized constrained denoising procedures, and addresses deadlocks via time-varying specifications and liveness CBFs. This work extends formal safety guarantees from single-agent to multi-agent diffusion planning, which is critical for deploying autonomous systems such as robot swarms and traffic systems where inter-agent safety is essential. The decentralized variant with communication awareness also addresses practical scalability concerns. The method builds on the single-agent SafeDiffuser construction that embeds control barrier function (CBF) constraints into reverse-diffusion updates, and provides algorithms, proofs, and reference implementations including a multi-agent Maze2D benchmarking domain. Empirical results show reduced violation counts relative to unconstrained diffusion baselines while retaining planning quality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Diffusion probabilistic models have recently been used for trajectory planning by iteratively denoising random trajectories into feasible plans. SafeDiffuser introduced finite-time diffusion invariance by adding a quadratic programming (QP) step with control barrier function constraints to ensure safety in single-agent settings. Control barrier functions are mathematical tools that enforce safety by keeping system states within a defined safe set. MA-SafeDiffuser extends these ideas to multiple agents that must coordinate and avoid collisions.

## [AGORA: Reliability-Aware Aggregation of LLM Judges via Item-Response Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05556) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AGORA (Aggregation Of Reliability-weighted Assessments) is a new item-response-theory-based aggregator for LLM judges that learns each judge's reliability from all items jointly without human labels. It matches or exceeds Dawid-Skene on large evaluation sets and its advantage widens as the set shrinks toward a few hundred items. This work addresses a key challenge in LLM evaluation: aggregating judges of varying reliability when human-labeled validation sets are small. By providing a principled, label-free method that outperforms existing approaches in small-data regimes, AGORA could improve the trustworthiness of LLM-as-a-judge systems for practitioners. AGORA places every item on a latent quality scale and models each judge with a sensitivity parameter and ordered rating thresholds, learning reliability from all items jointly. A label-free dispersion statistic can indicate in advance when trust weighting helps, and the learned trust weights track per-judge human validity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: LLM-as-a-judge uses large language models to evaluate text outputs as a scalable alternative to human annotation, but individual judges have biases, so aggregating multiple judges is common. Item response theory (IRT) is a psychometric framework that models the probability of a response based on a person's latent ability and item characteristics, and it is widely used in high-stakes tests like the GRE. The Dawid-Skene model is a latent variable approach that infers true labels from crowd-sourced annotations by estimating each annotator's error patterns, but it requires sufficient data per judge.

## [Anduin: Sybil-resistant reputation for agentic marketplaces via onchain payment graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05586) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Anduin, a reputation system that models onchain payment flows as a bipartite graph between buyers and providers and derives provider ratings from paths linked to a buyer's trusted network. It proposes three Sybil-resistant rating algorithms—a max-flow rating and two relaxed variants—and evaluates them on a 157-day dataset of 66M agentic transactions worth $6.1M across 16 blockchains, showing that rating manipulation costs 20% of buyers' total spend and is about 23x harder than the standard collaborative filtering similarity rule. As autonomous AI agents increasingly transact in permissionless onchain markets, selecting reliable new service providers without human curation or identity credentials becomes critical, and naive volume rankings are easily gamed by Sybil attackers. This work shows that existing payment histories can provide Sybil-resistant ratings, which could enable safer decentralized AI agent economies and inform the design of trust mechanisms for agentic commerce. The evaluation uses simulated adversaries across 813 buyers, and the manipulation cost is measured as 20% of those buyers' total spend, making it around 23x harder to manipulate than the standard similarity rule (collaborative filtering). The approach relies on a max-flow algorithm and two relaxed variants optimized for stronger rating performance, and it assumes the availability of onchain payment histories as a scarce resource for trust.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: A Sybil attack occurs when an attacker creates many pseudonymous identities to subvert a reputation system, a key threat in permissionless networks. Bipartite graphs model relationships between two disjoint sets—here, buyers and providers—and max-flow algorithms compute the maximum amount of flow that can be pushed from a source to a sink through a network with edge capacities, which is used here to quantify trust paths. Agentic marketplaces are emerging platforms where autonomous AI agents offer and consume services, often settling payments on blockchains.

## [Hybrid Spark: Adaptive Speculative Decoding That Lets the Content Choose the Drafter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05611) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Hybrid Spark is a new framework that runs a model-based drafter and a model-free n-gram drafter simultaneously on every decoding step, then selects the better draft chain per request within adaptive verification, all on GPU. Instantiated with DSpark and an n-gram drafter in vLLM, it achieves 1.10–1.62× the output throughput of DSpark on code editing at batch size 4 and 1.04–1.27× at batch size 128, while staying at 0.97–1.01× on chat at temperature 0. Speculative decoding gains depend heavily on workload shape and content, and existing adaptive verification supports only one model-based drafter, forcing deployments to fix a drafter or split GPUs. Hybrid Spark lets a single deployment serve both copy-heavy workloads like code editing and general chat efficiently, which could simplify and improve LLM serving infrastructure. The key idea is a price per verified draft token—the goodput that slot would earn elsewhere in the batch—which puts both drafters' chains on one scale once the model-free drafter's tokens carry calibrated acceptance estimates fit once on general text. The approach keeps asynchronous scheduling intact by running entirely on GPU, though results are from an arXiv preprint without community validation yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Speculative decoding speeds up LLM inference by having a small draft model propose several tokens ahead, which a larger target model then verifies in parallel, accepting matches without changing output quality. Model-based drafters propose short blocks on any content, while model-free drafters like n-gram lookup cheaply propose long copies when output repeats input, as in code editing. Adaptive verification allocates a batch-wide verification budget to the draft tokens most likely to be accepted, but previously supported only one model-based drafter because model-free drafters provide no per-token confidence.

<a id="item-68"></a>
## [ACCA: Continuous Authorization Framework for Autonomous AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05632) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Assurance-Conditioned Continuous Authorization (ACCA), a harness-independent framework that maintains and re-evaluates the operational authority of autonomous AI agents based on currently justified assurance. It uses typed dependency graphs for selective re-evaluation and authority epochs to track changes in effective authority. As autonomous agents increasingly act through tools, delegate work, and change models or environments, traditional static authorization becomes inadequate. ACCA provides a formal approach to continuously align an agent's authority with evolving evidence and policy, which is crucial for AI safety and systems security. The framework distinguishes evidence validity from contextual applicability, derives assurance claims from eligible evidence, and resolves a multidimensional effective-authority envelope within a principal-declared ceiling and applicable policy. It includes protected effect-time enforcement to reject stale actions before consequential resource changes, and a compositional Domain Governance Profile for domain-specific semantics without granting authority.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Autonomous AI agents are software systems that can perform tasks, use tools, and make decisions with minimal human intervention. Authorization is the process of determining what actions an agent is allowed to perform. Continuous authorization means re-evaluating permissions as conditions change, rather than granting static access. ACCA builds on concepts like dependency graphs, which represent dependencies between objects, and authority epochs, which are versioned states of an agent's authority.

<a id="item-69"></a>
## [On-Policy Self-Distillation Makes Weight Memory Competitive for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05636) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.05636) proposes using on-policy self-distillation (OPSD) to train a context-free student model from a teacher model conditioned on user memory, enabling weight-based memory to store long-term factual knowledge. On the HaluMem benchmark, OPSD matches or surpasses strong text-memory baselines, including full-context prompting and retrieval systems, while reading fewer tokens per query. This work addresses a key bottleneck in production LLM agents: text-based memory incurs growing retrieval costs as history accumulates. By making weight memory a viable low-cost complement, it could enable more efficient persistent, multi-session agent deployments and reduce reliance on retrieval at query time. The method combines OPSD with a verify-then-regenerate router that lets weight and text memory each handle the queries they best serve. The paper also includes an extensive study characterizing weight memory behavior and analyzing how different training recipes affect performance, though the abstract is truncated and full details are not yet available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: LLM agents typically rely on text-based memory: key information is extracted from context and stored as text, then retrieved and inserted back into the prompt at query time. This retrieval-augmented approach grows more expensive as memory accumulates. Weight memory instead internalizes knowledge directly into model parameters, answering without any context, and has shown promise for skills and behaviors but remains underexplored for episodic and semantic facts. On-policy self-distillation (OPSD) is a technique where a single model acts as both student and teacher, with the teacher having access to privileged context, and the student learning to match the teacher's outputs on its own generated trajectories.

## [Paper Proposes Reporting Standard for Attributing Agent Failures to History](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05650) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.05650) argues that claims attributing a long-horizon AI agent's failure to its history must be backed by intervention-verified evidence, such as executed counterfactuals with replay certificates, rather than taxonomy labels inferred from transcripts. It proposes a reporting standard requiring a frozen executable mid-trajectory state, alteration of only the history the agent sees, and a measured replay certificate matched to the estimand. This work addresses a methodological gap in AI agent evaluation, where failure explanations are often subjective taxonomies; a rigorous standard could improve reproducibility and causal validity of failure attribution, influencing how researchers and practitioners report and trust agent diagnostics. The paper identifies four manipulation classes with precedents (e.g., DoVer, REFLECT, Who&When Pro, Causal Agent Replay) but notes none reports a measured replay certificate. Two cautionary measurements show a carrier taxonomy's concentration could not be separated from benchmark construction (adjusted odds ratio 1.15, interval crossing 1), and hash-identical snapshots restored into identically seeded processes agreed in 24 of 24 states but differed from live continuation in 9 and 20 of 24; restoring the random stream's captured position made all 24 agree.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Long-horizon AI agents perform multi-step tasks, and when they fail, researchers often publish taxonomies labeling the failure from the transcript. Counterfactual reasoning involves asking what would have happened under a different history, and executing such interventions requires restoring an agent's mid-trajectory state exactly. Replay certificates are evidence that a restored state faithfully reproduces the original execution, which is necessary for valid counterfactual claims.

## [Petri Nets with Individual Tokens Enable Individualized Causal Effects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05703) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes representing causal structures as Petri Nets under the Individual Token Philosophy, providing a formal translation from Bayesian Networks and adding a conformal prediction layer to quantify individualized causal effects with finite-sample coverage guarantees. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of type-level causal estimands like ATE and CATE, which collapse under unbounded heterogeneity, by offering a token-level structural account that could impact causal modeling research and applications requiring individual-level inference. The approach combines a Petri Net layer that structurally represents causal mechanisms without distributional assumptions and a conformal prediction layer that provides distribution-free uncertainty quantification with finite-sample coverage guarantees, though practical scalability and translation complexity remain open questions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Structural Causal Models and Bayesian Networks are the dominant frameworks for causal inference, but their estimands such as Average Treatment Effects (ATE) and Conditional Average Treatment Effects (CATE) are population-level properties. When treatment effects vary widely across individuals (unbounded heterogeneity), these type-level estimands become ill-defined. Petri Nets are a mathematical modeling language for distributed systems, and under the Individual Token Philosophy, each token represents a distinct individual entity, making them suitable for token-level causal representation. Conformal prediction is a distribution-free method that produces prediction sets with guaranteed coverage.

## [Thought Supervision in LLM Agent Distillation Often Unnecessary, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05709) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.05709) disentangles the impact of teacher thoughts and actions during LLM agent distillation, finding that altering or discarding thoughts has little effect on student action performance across model scales and benchmarks. Thought supervision only becomes important when the information in the thought is not disclosed in the corresponding action. This challenges the standard practice of distilling entire teacher trajectories, suggesting that thought supervision can be selectively omitted for efficiency without sacrificing action performance. It has practical implications for reducing training data and compute costs in building smaller, efficient agent models. The study independently perturbs thoughts and actions during distillation on widely adopted agent benchmarks, and identifies 'discrepant cases' where thought information is not present in the action. In those cases, thought supervision remains critical, so the decision to distill thoughts should depend on whether the action discloses the thought's information.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Knowledge distillation trains a smaller student model to mimic a larger teacher model. In LLM agents, distillation often uses complete teacher trajectories that interleave intermediate reasoning (thoughts) and tool calls or environment actions. This paper questions whether supervising the student on the teacher's thoughts is necessary for good action performance.

## [PersonaCodeBench: Benchmarking Coding Agents on Persona-Driven Multi-Turn Requirements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05739) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PersonaCodeBench introduces a benchmark that converts user personas—spanning roles, technical abilities, interaction preferences, and practical constraints—into verifiable obligations for coding agents across multi-turn, human-interactive sessions. A controller governs user-simulator responses and requirement disclosure, while separate completion and personalization verifiers jointly determine pass/fail, and the authors evaluate all major frontier agents to build a leaderboard. Existing coding-agent benchmarks increasingly include interaction but largely ignore whether agents fulfill personalized user requirements, so this benchmark fills a gap that matters as agents are deployed to assist users with diverse expertise and constraints. It could push agent developers to optimize not just code correctness but also adherence to permissions, testing, explanation, and delivery expectations. Requirements emerge progressively during the session, but their content, disclosure conditions, and acceptance criteria are fixed before evaluation to keep results reproducible; joint pass requires both staying within a declared budget and complying with validity conditions. The benchmark is a controlled evaluation paper rather than a new model or tool, and its persona pool and user simulator design are central to its validity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Coding agents are LLM-based systems that can write, debug, and refactor code, and benchmarks like SWE-bench have become standard for measuring their coding ability. However, real-world use often involves multi-turn conversation in which users reveal requirements gradually and expect the agent to respect their expertise, preferences, and constraints. Persona-driven evaluation turns those user characteristics into explicit, checkable obligations, and user simulators are used to reproducibly emulate human interaction.

## [PAYWORTHY benchmark tests if LLMs can predict AI agent success on freelance jobs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05759) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PAYWORTHY introduces a benchmark of 1,494 real freelance job postings to test whether frontier LLMs can forecast an AI agent's success before attempting the job. The study finds that forecasts are accurate (AUC 0.66–0.86) only when the agent's limitations are explicitly described, but drop to near-random (AUC 0.45–0.56) when the agent is described generically. This benchmark highlights that LLMs' ability to predict agent performance is highly sensitive to how the agent's capabilities are communicated, which has implications for task routing, agent deployment, and client decision-making in freelance platforms. It also provides a realistic evaluation framework using real-world job postings. The benchmark samples from 18.8 million postings (2020–2026) and uses an LLM judge as the client to accept or reject work; results hold across judges from Claude, GLM, Kimi, and Qwen families, and informed forecasts also rank human rater verdicts. The code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: AUC (Area Under the ROC Curve) is a metric for binary classification that measures how well a model ranks positive examples above negative ones, with 0.5 indicating random ranking and 1.0 perfect ranking. LLM-as-a-judge is an evaluation approach where a language model assesses the quality of another model's outputs. AI agent benchmarks like PAYWORTHY test agents on realistic tasks such as freelance jobs to gauge their practical capabilities.

## [FlyHash validated for LLM agent memory retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05791) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper empirically validates FlyHash, a fruit-fly-inspired locality-sensitive hashing method, for LLM agent memory retrieval, showing robust quality advantages over SimHash under a correctly matched-bit protocol across 10 seeds and four datasets. The study also introduces a sparse-set comparison and finds that the quality advantage does not translate into memory or latency savings against dense-vector indexing (HNSW) at ordinary scale. This work provides practitioners with a rigorous, nuanced assessment of when bio-inspired hashing is worth using for agent memory, correcting a previously undocumented comparison pitfall and showing that quality gains alone do not justify replacing HNSW. It helps the LLM agent community avoid over-optimistic adoption of hashing methods and highlights the need for end-to-end evaluation including latency and memory. At equal bits, FlyHash's codes are larger and slower to compare than SimHash's, and HNSW dominates both hash methods at ordinary scale; a sparse-set comparison recovers most but not all of a naive latency gap. The quality advantage also holds when using genuine, non-circular agent-memory relevance labels rather than the nearest-neighbor-by-embedding proxy commonly used in the literature.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Locality-sensitive hashing (LSH) compresses high-dimensional vectors into compact codes while preserving similarity, enabling fast approximate nearest-neighbor search. FlyHash mimics the fruit fly's olfactory circuit by using sparse random projection followed by winner-take-all competition to produce sparse binary codes, and prior work showed it preserves neighbor structure better per bit than conventional random-hyperplane hashing (SimHash). LLM agents often store conversation history in external memory and retrieve relevant items via dense embeddings searched with methods like HNSW, so alternative hashing schemes are of practical interest.

## [Orbit Audits Test Whether Neural Field Weight Readers Capture Function, Not Coordinates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05798) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.05798) introduces an evaluation protocol that audits neural field weight readers by applying hidden-neuron permutations and positive ReLU rescalings—transformations that preserve the represented function—to 480 shape fields and 1,000 MNIST fields. It finds that a statistics-based reader's accuracy drops sharply under joint transformations, while a normalized set reader retains its accuracy with unchanged predictions. This work provides an inspectable, large-scale protocol for testing whether weight-space models learn functional representations rather than memorizing arbitrary parameter coordinates, which is critical for interpretability and for building reliable weight-space learning systems. It also extends the audit to language models and reasoning sessions, showing that consistency under canonicalization does not guarantee correct recognition or identical generated responses. The study includes four small frozen language models achieving a raw accuracy range with next-token scoring, and a separate four-configuration reasoning experiment with 1,920 scored sessions where fresh identical raw prompts disagree on a range of origins. The authors use joint correctness/agreement metrics, semantic-cue controls, fresh repeats, and exact-rational rounding bounds to separate failure modes, and they restrict claims to the tested fields, prompts, and execution configurations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Neural fields are networks that represent continuous signals such as shapes or images, and weight-space learning aims to recognize content directly from network parameters. However, neural networks have symmetries: permuting hidden neurons or positively rescaling ReLU weights leaves the represented function unchanged, so a reader that relies on raw parameter coordinates may fail under these transformations. Prior work on permutation-equivariant neural functionals and rescaling invariance provides the theoretical basis for such audits.

## [Vision Paper Argues for Distributed LLM Post-Training and Decentralized Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05841) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv vision paper (2611.05841) argues that distributed learning, including federated and decentralized approaches, should become a first-class paradigm for LLM post-training rather than a privacy add-on. It challenges the implicit assumption in RLHF and DPO that preference data should be centralized, and formalizes challenges like reference drift, preference heterogeneity, and the DP-utility trade-off for contrastive losses. This perspective is significant because current alignment methods assume centralized preference data, which is increasingly impractical as LLMs deploy in healthcare, law, finance, and multilingual communities where data cannot or will not be centralized. If adopted, distributed post-training could enable more privacy-preserving, pluralistic, and scalable AI alignment, affecting researchers, policymakers, and communities whose preferences are currently underrepresented. The paper builds a three-pillar argument around privacy and data sovereignty, pluralism and representational equity, and robustness and scalability, and proposes concrete algorithmic directions and a research agenda. As a vision paper, it lacks empirical results, so the tractability claims remain to be validated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: RLHF (Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback) and DPO (Direct Preference Optimization) are widely used methods for aligning LLMs with human preferences, and both typically rely on a centralized dataset of pairwise preference comparisons. Federated learning allows multiple parties to train a shared model without pooling raw data, while decentralized alignment aims to produce systems whose values reflect distributed preferences rather than a single authority. This paper argues that combining these ideas with post-training is necessary and tractable.

## [Risk Control Certifies Error Rates, Not Magnitudes, in Fetal Biometry Audit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05851) ⭐️ 7.0/10

An audit of a selective fetal biometry pipeline on the HC18 dataset shows that Clopper-Pearson risk control certifies gross-error rates but not error magnitudes, allowing a 271.3 mm head circumference error to pass certification. The error came from a small, geometrically complete ring fitted to an image with a true HC of 324.6 mm, and a second split reproduced the mechanism with a 233.3 mm error. This exposes a critical limitation of selective prediction in safety-critical medical ML: finite-sample risk certificates bound the rate of gross errors, not their magnitude, so a single catastrophic outlier can still be certified as safe. The finding is relevant to any deployment of selective prediction where the cost of an error is not binary but depends on how wrong the prediction is. The accepted candidate scored 0.94 on contour completeness, so the score could not detect the failure, and one outlier among approximately 150 answers left the certified risk bound (α = 0.10) intact. Constraining the anatomical admissibility region by raising the lower bound from 50 to 55 mm cost only 0.4 coverage points and cut worst-case error to 11.4 and 11.1 mm on the two splits, whereas suppressing it via the completeness threshold cost 19.0 and 12.1 points.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Selective prediction allows a model to abstain from answering when it is uncertain, trading coverage (the fraction of cases answered) for lower error rates. Clopper-Pearson is an exact method for constructing binomial confidence intervals, used here to calibrate a threshold that certifies the rate of gross errors at a chosen risk level α. Fetal biometry involves measuring head circumference from prenatal ultrasound to assess fetal growth, and the HC18 dataset is a public benchmark of 999 labeled 2D ultrasound images for automated head circumference measurement.

## [Auditable Unlearning for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05866) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces auditability as a fourth evaluation axis for LLM unlearning and proposes GapWiden, a two-stage method that can be added to any unlearning technique to provide evidence of deletion. It shows that across eleven unlearning methods on TOFU, MUSE, and RWKU, no method satisfies all four axes, and that GapWiden plus GapVeil achieves oracle parity for auditability and leakage. This work highlights that current unlearning methods lack a way to prove deletion to users, which is crucial for privacy compliance and user trust. By making auditability an explicit requirement, it could reshape how unlearning methods are designed and evaluated, impacting providers and regulators. GapWiden uses a hinge loss to increase the difference between audit statistics of forget and retain sets, while GapVeil is an inference-time rejection sampler that uses the base model as a drafter and re-samples based on audit statistics. Results show auditability and leakage reach oracle parity for every method, forget performance converges to oracle, but retain performance improves for only nine of eleven methods and remains below parity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Machine unlearning aims to remove a user's data from a trained model after a deletion request. Existing methods are typically evaluated on three axes: how well they forget, how much capability they retain, and how robust they are to membership inference attacks (which try to determine if a data point was in the training set). Benchmarks like TOFU, MUSE, and RWKU provide standardized forget/retain splits to measure these aspects.

## [RECAP: Context-Aware Prediction Recycling for Multimodal Speculative Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05894) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RECAP introduces a training-free prediction recycling method for speculative decoding in vision-language models that stores target model predictions under one-, two-, and three-token keys, using request-local and persistent memories to avoid aliasing. It achieves mean speedups of 1.66x and 1.56x over autoregressive decoding on Qwen2.5-VL-7B and LLaVA-1.6-7B across seven single-turn benchmarks, outperforming the strongest training-free baseline, Token Recycling (1.51x and 1.37x). This work addresses a key inefficiency in applying speculative decoding to vision-language models, where predictions from different images and prefixes get conflated, and could enable faster multimodal inference without requiring an auxiliary draft model. It is a valuable contribution to efficient multimodal inference, likely of interest to researchers working on accelerating vision-language models. RECAP uses entropy-confidence weighting to fuse retrieved predictions according to key specificity and concentration, and employs best-first search to compose a candidate tree with suffix-matched continuations, verified by one target pass under greedy decoding. The method requires no training or auxiliary draft model, and the implementation is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Speculative decoding is an inference-time optimization for autoregressive large language models that generates multiple tokens per decoding step by having a smaller draft model propose candidate tokens, which the larger target model verifies in a single forward pass, preserving the output distribution while cutting latency. Prediction recycling is a training-free alternative that reuses the target model's own past predictions as proposals, but in vision-language models, indexing by the last token alone conflates predictions made under different images and prefixes, causing reuse to fail at image-sensitive decoding states.

## [CHARM+ Boosts Distributed Attention Speed on Cross-Hardware Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05906) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CHARM+ introduces a re-merge multistream mechanism that reorganizes sequential communications into levels to enable intra-level parallelism and computation overlap, while decoupling cross-hardware from intra-hardware communication to eliminate synchronization overhead. It achieves average speedups of 1.3x and 1.7x for forward and backward passes on dual-NUMA PCIe, and 2.8x and 2.9x on dual-node NVLink compared to Ring-Attention. As sequence lengths grow, distributed attention methods like Ring-Attention become critical for training LLMs, but communication dependencies limit GPU utilization. CHARM+ addresses this by improving computation-communication overlap and handling bandwidth imbalance in cross-hardware architectures, potentially enabling more efficient large-scale training. The method reorganizes sequential communications into multiple levels and achieves intra-level parallelism, while decoupling cross-hardware communication from intra-hardware communication to avoid synchronization overhead caused by bandwidth imbalance. Evaluations on multiple hardware architectures show consistent outperformance over state-of-the-art methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Sequence parallelism distributes a long input sequence across multiple GPUs, with Ring-Attention being a popular approach where each GPU holds a shard of the KV cache and communicates in a ring. NUMA (Non-Uniform Memory Access) is a memory design where access time depends on the memory location relative to the processor, common in multi-socket servers. Cross-hardware communication (e.g., between NUMA nodes or separate nodes) often has lower and imbalanced bandwidth compared to intra-hardware communication, making synchronization costly.

## [LLM agents autonomously plan electricity systems under carbon constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05911) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2611.05911) presents an autonomous multi-agent planning framework that iteratively proposes, numerically evaluates, and revises electricity-system investment and dispatch designs. It was validated on forty carbon-constrained trajectories across ten system-year scenarios in five electricity systems and four countries, using two model deployments and both single- and multi-agent architectures. This demonstrates that LLM-based agents can close the loop on a real-world engineering planning task, potentially reducing the manual effort and expertise needed for capacity expansion studies under emissions constraints. If validated further, such frameworks could help utilities and planners explore more scenarios faster and with less human intervention. All forty trajectories retained candidates satisfying physical and fixed annual operational carbon-budget checks, and twenty-eight of forty matched reference designs exceeded the new scenario ceilings. A separate archive of ninety-six trajectories under a common 1,000 Mt annual ceiling provides matched references and broader regional coverage, using public regional data and published institutional demand outlooks.

## [MoE Router Logits Enable Training-Free Control of Reasoning Behaviors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05919) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.05919) shows that Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) router logits can serve as a training-free interface to classify and reshape reasoning behaviors such as planning, execution, and verification. By selectively editing expert router scores during inference, the method reduces generated tokens by a median of 30.4% (up to 63.3%) across four models and four benchmarks while keeping accuracy within 0.5 percentage points. This work addresses the costly problem of 'overthinking' in reasoning models, where models waste test-time compute on redundant verification after finding a correct answer. It offers a lightweight, interpretable mechanism to optimize inference efficiency without retraining, which could significantly reduce latency and cost for deployed reasoning models. The method splits reasoning traces into sentence-level segments labeled with six behaviors (planning, execution, restatement, fact, reflection, conclusion), and linear probes on router logits predict these labels nearly as well as probes on the residual stream while using about half as many features. The signals generalize across six datasets in five domains, and the intervention encourages models to stop once solved rather than skipping essential steps.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models use a learned router to select a few expert networks per token, and the router's logits (scores for each expert) are normally discarded after selection. Test-time compute refers to the additional computation a model spends during inference, such as generating longer reasoning chains, which can improve accuracy but also lead to inefficiency. Linear probes are simple classifiers trained on internal model representations to predict properties like reasoning behavior.

## [Split-KV: Exact Context Parallelism for Training Diffusion Drafters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05926) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Split-KV is a new exact context parallelism (CP) method for training diffusion drafters that keeps key/value shards local and limits per-layer attention communication to query-sized tensors, making communication independent of context length. At a CP size of 64, it retains 85–91% weak-scaling efficiency and achieves up to 3.67× speedup over the fastest available CP baseline, with code available at https://github.com/zxgx/verl. This work addresses a specific but important bottleneck in training diffusion drafters for speculative decoding, where context lengths grow much larger than query lengths. By making communication independent of context length, Split-KV enables efficient distributed training of these models and could accelerate research and deployment of faster speculative decoding systems. The method exploits the empirical observation that acceptance length remains stable as context scales with a fixed query budget, while increasing the query budget yields diminishing quality gains. Its global query view enables a cost-aware longest-processing-time-first (LPT) strategy that balances context key/value chunks by realized attention cost, achieving <1% imbalance and planning three orders of magnitude faster than the best load-balancing baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Speculative decoding is a draft-then-verify technique where a cheap drafter model proposes multiple next tokens and the target model verifies them in parallel, speeding up inference. Diffusion drafters accelerate this by denoising multiple draft tokens in parallel, and their training uses anchor sampling to construct compact draft queries and cross-attention with a block-sparse mask over a much longer context. Context parallelism (CP) is a distributed training strategy that partitions the input sequence dimension across accelerators, but existing CP methods do not exploit the query–context asymmetry or meet the nonlinear, sample-dependent load-balancing requirement created by anchor sampling.

## [Survey Proposes Taxonomy for Graph-Mediated LLM Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05953) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv survey (2611.05953) introduces an operation-level role–locus taxonomy that categorizes how graph operations function across reasoning, knowledge and evidence, retrieval and memory, execution, and coordination in agentic LLM systems. It also organizes benchmarks, comparison designs, process diagnostics, and system costs into an evaluation framework, and identifies open challenges in adaptive computation and stopping, memory maintenance, and dependency-aware recovery. As LLM systems increasingly adopt graphs for structural reasoning, knowledge grounding, retrieval, memory, and multi-agent coordination, this taxonomy offers researchers and practitioners a shared vocabulary to assess when graph mechanisms actually help rather than merely improving local metrics. It could guide more rigorous evaluation and design of agentic architectures. The survey distinguishes graph observations and external computations that guide decisions, memory representations and updates that support later tasks, and dependencies that constrain execution and information delivery, while separating local metric improvements from downstream task performance and retaining the conditions under which results were reported. It is a synthesis of existing work rather than a new empirical breakthrough.

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) lets LLMs pull in external documents before answering, and agentic LLM systems extend this by coupling a foundation model with an execution loop for planning, memory, tool use, and multi-step action. Graphs—networks of nodes and edges—are increasingly used in these systems to represent relationships among knowledge, memories, or task dependencies, but their varied roles are often conflated. This survey aims to disentangle those roles so that graph-based components can be evaluated more precisely.

## [URED recycles high-scored unexposed items to boost discovery in Taobao feed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05997) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Alibaba researchers present URED (Unexposed Recycling for Efficient Discovery), a training-free recall-stage system deployed on Taobao feed recommendation for over six months to tens of millions of daily active users. URED aggregates items that were scored highly but never exposed over a 30-day window and re-injects them at recall time, yielding +3.47% discovered categories and +8.47% content richness in online A/B tests while preserving engagement. This work offers a production-validated solution to the exploration-exploitation dilemma in industrial recommender systems, showing that recycling discarded high-scored candidates can improve discovery without hurting engagement. It provides a practical alternative to naive boosting of cold content, which often backfires, and could influence how platforms design recall-stage discovery mechanisms. URED adds under 1% latency via a single O(1) lookup per request with no model retraining, and ablations confirm both design pillars are necessary: a one-day window collapses the discovery gain to +0.55% (not significant), and random injection without score filtering yields no gain while trending negative on engagement (-0.17%). Across production recall channels, URED reaches 13.9x the platform-average discovery rate (6.3x the strongest nearline baseline) at engagement quality on par with the platform average.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Industrial recommender systems typically use a multi-stage cascade: a recall (retrieval) stage filters a huge candidate pool down to a manageable set, and a ranking stage orders them for presentation. Discovery—helping users find new interests—is often addressed by boosting under-explored or cold-start items, but such items have little interaction data and rankers may overestimate them, leading to poor engagement. URED instead recycles items that received high scores from ranking models but were never exposed due to pipeline constraints, forming a pool that is both relevant and reliably predictable.

## [Reglos: Self-Evolving Memory Agent with Cross-Model Critic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06049) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Reglos is a training-free personal memory agent that uses recursive self-improvement to patch its harness, with a critic from a second model family screening each patch for benchmark gaming. It outperforms Mem0, MemOS, and EverOS on PersonaMem-v2 and LongMemEval-S, reaching 61.3% and 84.0% with Qwen3.8-27B, and 57.5% and 87.2% with GPT-5.6 Luna. This work addresses a key risk in self-improving agents: patches that fix targeted failures may overfit and fail to generalize. The cross-model critic mechanism offers a practical safeguard against benchmark gaming, which is increasingly important as agents autonomously modify their own infrastructure. Reglos maintains an append-only per-user memory record where new preferences supersede old ones and facts can be forgotten, and its harness evolves via a coding agent that proposes patches from failure traces. After the harness is frozen, a fixed evaluator decides which further patches to accept, ensuring changes are tested beyond the failures they target.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: An agent harness (or scaffolding) is the software infrastructure around an LLM that enables it to operate as an agent, defining tools, context, and control flow. Recursive self-improvement refers to an AI system iteratively improving its own capabilities, which raises safety concerns about unforeseen evolution. Benchmark gaming occurs when a system exploits evaluation gaps rather than demonstrating the intended capability, which NIST calls evaluation cheating.

## [UniCompress: From Multi-LoRA Experts to One Compact Model for Edge Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06075) ⭐️ 7.0/10

UniCompress is a deployment-oriented framework that converts multi-task LoRA expertise into a single adapter-free INT2 vision-language model, using GPTQ initialization and teacher-guided quantization recovery. Starting from a consolidated BF16 Qwen3-VL-4B teacher, it reduces weight storage from 8.9 GB to 2.1 GB (about 76% reduction) while improving the INT2 macro task score by 34.3 percentage points over GPTQ-INT2 and staying within 0.9 points of the BF16 teacher. This work addresses the memory and management overhead of deploying separate task adapters with a high-precision backbone in embodied and edge systems, showing that aggressive 2-bit quantization can preserve multi-task behavior. It could enable a single multi-skill VLM to run on resource-constrained platforms, impacting edge AI and embodied AI deployment. The method jointly quantizes and recovers projection weights in both the vision encoder and language backbone, and is evaluated on four heterogeneous embodied tasks: posture recognition, household-safety understanding, multimodal dialogue routing, and mobile-GUI action prediction. As a single arXiv preprint, it lacks peer review and community discussion, and INT2 quantization is generally known to cause significant accuracy degradation, making the reported recovery gains notable but in need of independent validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that freezes pre-trained weights and injects small trainable matrices, enabling lightweight task-specific adapters. GPTQ is a post-training quantization method that uses approximate second-order information to compress weights with minimal error. INT2 quantization reduces weights to 2 bits, offering extreme compression but typically at a large accuracy cost, which this work aims to mitigate via teacher-guided recovery.

## [Reasoning-Trace Overflow Plagues Code Generation in Explicit Reasoning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06095) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new empirical study (arXiv:2611.06095) identifies 'reasoning-trace overflow' in explicit reasoning models, where models spend so much of a fixed 32k-token output budget on reasoning that they fail to produce usable code. Across seven open-weight models and six code benchmark settings, the study finds that overflow is rare on simple function-level tasks but common on harder benchmarks, with some model-benchmark pairs exhausting the full budget in over half of generations. This matters for practitioners deploying reasoning models for code generation, as it shows that explicit reasoning is not always beneficial and can waste tokens without improving pass@1. The findings suggest that reasoning should be treated as a controllable resource, with inference-time controls like budgeted transitions to mitigate overflow and improve both performance and token efficiency. Most complete overflow is associated with repetitive reasoning loops, though substantial long reasoning also occurs without looping. Disabling reasoning can be competitive for overflow-prone models because it ensures an answer is produced, while prompt steering reduces overflow inconsistently; budgeted transitions provide more direct control and can improve both performance and token efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Explicit reasoning models, such as those in the DeepSeek-R1 and Qwen3-Thinking families, generate a chain of thought before answering, which can improve performance on complex tasks. Pass@1 is the standard metric for code generation, measuring the fraction of problems where the first sampled solution passes all unit tests. Output budgets cap the total number of tokens a model can generate, so excessive reasoning can leave no room for the final code.

Researchers introduced MechScam, a benchmark of 6,614 scam and non-scam videos with evidence-based explanations, scam-element annotations, and misleading arguments built from genuine evidence, and proposed ScamCourt, a debate framework where trained prosecution and defense debaters argue before a judge that decides based on a transcript with verified citations. Existing scam video detection work focuses on binary classification and overlooks the difficulty of separating scams from superficially similar content like advertisements, so a mechanism-level benchmark and a verifiable debate approach could make detection more interpretable and robust for platforms and users exposed to online fraud. Experiments show state-of-the-art multimodal large language models struggle to distinguish scam videos from similar non-scam content, while ScamCourt outperforms larger MLLMs and same-backbone fine-tuned baselines, reducing false positives and resisting misleading arguments; the dataset and code are released on GitHub.

## [Paper Proposes Formal Definition of Catastrophic AI Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06219) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2611.06219) introduces a formal definition of catastrophic performance for AI misalignment, using two reference values: a baseline value (no agent deployed) and an orthogonal value (a trajectory unrelated to human preferences). Catastrophic performance is defined as an agent undoing the benefits afforded by past human activity when deployed to accelerate further progress. This definition addresses a gap in AI alignment theory by distinguishing merely suboptimal outcomes from truly catastrophic ones, enabling more rigorous analysis of catastrophic risk. It could influence how researchers formalize and evaluate severe misalignment scenarios in AI safety. The definition relies on two reference values: baseline value (no agent deployed) and orthogonal value (a trajectory unrelated to human preferences, typically far worse than baseline). The authors illustrate these values through examples where past human activity has altered the environment to human benefit, and catastrophic performance represents the agent's undoing of those benefits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: AI alignment research aims to ensure AI systems pursue intended goals and preferences. Misalignment can lead to outcomes ranging from suboptimal to catastrophic, but existing theoretical work often does not formally distinguish between these. The orthogonality thesis, which holds that intelligence and final goals are independent, underscores the risk that highly capable systems may pursue goals misaligned with human values.

## [HarnessFlow uses MCTS to automatically optimize LLM agent harnesses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06254) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HarnessFlow introduces a Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS)-guided refinement tree combined with a coding agent to automatically optimize complete executable LLM agent harnesses under a limited evaluation budget. With only 15 refinement attempts per benchmark, it achieves the highest mean test scores on six agentic benchmarks, averaging 69.48% and outperforming initial harnesses by 10.89 points and Meta-Harness by 3.95 points. Harness design is currently a manual, labor-intensive process that significantly impacts LLM agent performance, so automating it could lower engineering costs and enable more capable, efficient agents. This work demonstrates that structured search over harness configurations is a viable path, potentially shifting how agent systems are developed and benchmarked. The method holds the underlying model and task environment fixed, using archived code, execution feedback, and a library of reusable harness mechanisms to guide refinement. The optimized harnesses also incur lower average per-task execution costs than hand-engineered baselines, though the abstract is truncated and full details on the MCTS policy and coding agent are not yet available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: An LLM agent harness is the engineered execution layer around a model that supplies context, memory, constraints, orchestration, tooling, and feedback to turn model intelligence into reliable, goal-directed behavior. Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) is a heuristic search algorithm that balances exploration and exploitation by building a search tree through random simulations, commonly used in decision processes with large search spaces like board games. Agentic benchmarks evaluate whether AI models can complete multi-step workflows using tools, browsers, or software interfaces, rather than just answering in a single shot.

## [EnzymeRSI: Dual-loop recursive self-improvement system for enzyme design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06321) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EnzymeRSI introduces a dual-loop recursive self-improvement system where the outer loop evolves harness assets without changing model weights and the inner loop develops prediction and selection algorithms, achieving a mean Spearman correlation improvement of 0.044 on ProteinGym across five assays and reaching 0.849 PCC in ThermoMPNN self-improvement. This work demonstrates that recursively improving the research workflow itself, not just candidate selection within a fixed model, can overcome bottlenecks in enzyme engineering and offers a path toward autonomous dry-to-wet experimentation. The system achieved 0.335 Spearman on CD19 and 0.683 on OPSD (rhodopsin), reached 0.307 Spearman on TadA-Bench in 1.13 hours, and in prospective wet-lab tests 10 of 32 non-pre-specified RLuc candidates showed at least twice wild-type activity, while an A3A variant raised 5mC conversion to 90.2% and KOD-E catalytic efficiency reached 31.1 times the parent.

## [GIST: Gibbs-Targeted Hierarchical Data Selection for Offline RLVR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06395) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces GIST, a two-level data selection method for offline reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) that selects which responses and tokens to supervise based on an exact factorization of the bidirectional KL-divergence mismatch at the reference policy. GIST uses sampling temperature to select correct responses on-policy and incorrect responses near the policy's mode, and uses the initial policy's token probabilities to supervise only tokens where the mismatch lies. This work provides a principled theoretical analysis of offline RLVR using the Gibbs-optimal policy and bidirectional KL divergence, leading to a data selection method that improves accuracy while supervising 66.6% fewer tokens than training on all candidates. It addresses the inefficiency of uniformly supervising all tokens in RLVR, which is relevant for reducing compute costs in LLM post-training. GIST is evaluated on two offline training objectives across three models, raising average accuracy on GSM8K, MATH-500, and MBPP; in a controlled comparison on Qwen3-0.6B/GSM8K, it outperforms seven selection baselines while supervising 66.6% fewer tokens. The method relies on signals available before training, such as sampling temperature and initial policy token probabilities, and is grounded in an exact factorization of the KL-divergence mismatch at the reference policy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) is a post-training method that fine-tunes language models using rule-based verifiers as rewards, commonly applied to math and coding tasks. Offline RLVR trains on a fixed pool of sampled responses, and typically every token of every response is supervised, even though responses and tokens are not equally informative. KL-regularized RL aims to find a policy close to a reference policy while maximizing reward, and the optimal policy has a Gibbs form; bidirectional KL divergence measures the mismatch between a policy and this target.

## [D3D: Decoupled Diffusion Drafting for Asynchronous Speculative Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06425) ⭐️ 7.0/10

D3D introduces a standalone diffusion drafter that removes the need for target-model hidden states, enabling asynchronous speculative decoding where successor blocks are drafted during verification in a single packed pass. It achieves average decoding speedups of 5.00x and 5.71x with Qwen3-8B and Qwen3-32B targets, respectively, and up to 7.48x on GSM8K with Qwen3-32B. This work breaks the sequential dependency between drafting and verification in speculative decoding, potentially enabling more efficient LLM inference in production systems. The significant speedups, especially with larger models, could reduce latency and cost for deploying large language models. D3D combines blockwise denoising with auxiliary autoregressive supervision to achieve long accepted prefixes without target hidden states, and it progressively prunes layers based on their effect on acceptance length; removing 5 of 36 layers retains over 99% of mean acceptance length across three benchmarks. The code is publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Speculative decoding accelerates autoregressive LLM inference by using a smaller draft model to propose multiple tokens, which the target model then verifies in a single forward pass, preserving output quality. Diffusion drafters generate entire blocks of candidate tokens in parallel via iterative denoising, but target-conditioned versions require updated target hidden states for each block, forcing sequential drafting and verification. D3D removes this dependency, enabling asynchronous operation.
