EviSeek introduces a training-free, evidence-centric agentic framework that makes question-derived evidence demands explicit acquisition objectives for embodied active question answering. On 3,436 ESI-Bench questions, it improves accuracy over the Single-model agent by 7.1 percentage points with Qwen3-VL-32B and 3.7 points with Gemini 3.1 Pro, while also improving visual-support-weighted accuracy and reducing mean token usage. This work addresses a core inefficiency in embodied agents—undirected interaction—by explicitly linking actions and retained observations to the information needed to answer a question. The training-free design and consistent gains across two different vision-language models suggest the approach could be broadly adopted to make embodied question answering more accurate and token-efficient. EviSeek organizes action selection and evidence retention around explicit evidence demands derived from the question, rather than relying solely on action plans or accumulated history. The framework is evaluated on 3,436 questions from ESI-Bench, a benchmark for embodied spatial intelligence built on OmniGibson, and shows gains with both Qwen3-VL-32B and Gemini 3.1 Pro.

## [SumOne: A Task-Agnostic Summarizer for Plug-and-Play Agent Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06445) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SumOne introduces a standalone summarizer trained via masked reconstruction and GRPO to compress long-horizon agent histories without relying on downstream task outcomes. It reduces estimated inference cost by approximately 78% on DeepSearchQA and 75% on SWE-bench Verified, with only a 0.04 absolute decrease in performance. This approach enables plug-and-play context compaction for LLM agents without coupling to specific tasks or models, potentially making long-horizon agent deployments more efficient and cost-effective. The same checkpoint transfers across downstream models and agent harnesses without additional fine-tuning. The summarizer is optimized with GRPO using a task-agnostic reward that combines reconstruction quality from a frozen reconstructor and a length penalty, eliminating the need for reference summaries or downstream task execution. At inference, only the lightweight summarizer is retained, and it is evaluated on long-horizon search and software-engineering tasks.

Researchers introduced AmbiHTMLBench, a benchmark of 1,115 annotated pages that evaluates models on recovering executable HTML from visually rich documents, preserving content, layout, and rendering semantics. The benchmark uses two evaluation protocols: an objective one measuring table fidelity, text error, and layout consistency, and a reference-anchored one aligning LLM judges with human preferences. Traditional document parsing often outputs Markdown, which loses fine-grained spatial structure and table geometry; HTML preserves these, making this benchmark valuable for the document AI and OCR community. It provides a diagnostic testbed for building smarter document parsing systems that need editable, inspectable, and reusable representations. Across ten primary multimodal systems, the best objective score is 0.8092, but the subjective winner differs, showing that automatic decomposition and human-perceived fidelity capture complementary properties. The authors also fine-tune a small-parameter expert model, finding that task-specific adaptation yields good performance on document-to-HTML reconstruction.

## [Coca-Cola compresses tool observations into latent tokens for LLM agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06500) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Coca-Cola, a continuous latent context compression model that compresses tool observations into latent tokens embedded directly into the agent's input embedding space, trained with Action-Preserving Training to align next-action distributions under full and compressed observations. Experiments with web-search agents on four QA benchmarks and two model families show 70.01%-89.24% token reduction and 24.45%-55.97% runtime reduction while improving task performance. This challenges the prevailing assumption that agents must consume tool outputs as explicit text tokens, offering a path to drastically reduce context overhead and attention dilution in multi-step agent interactions. If validated broadly, such latent compression could make long-horizon LLM agents significantly cheaper and faster to run in production. The method uses continuous latent tokens rather than discrete text, enabling higher compression ratios than token pruning or summarization, and the Action-Preserving Training objective explicitly trains the compressor to retain decision-critical information by matching next-action distributions. Code is available at https://github.com/THU-Agent/Coca-cola, though evaluation is limited to web-search agents and QA benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: LLM agents typically append raw tool outputs to their context, and over multi-step interactions these long, noisy observations consume substantial context and distract the model from task-relevant information. Existing compression methods like token pruning and summarization still operate on discrete text, which caps the achievable compression ratio. Latent context compression instead represents information as continuous embeddings, and prior work such as Latent Context Language Models has shown high compression ratios with minimal accuracy loss.

## [ORSD: Self-Distilled Process Rubrics for Token-Level Credit Assignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06543) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Online Rubric Self-Distillation (ORSD), a method that generates natural-language process rubrics from on-policy trajectories and uses them as privileged information to compute token-level credit, which is added to the trajectory-level GRPO advantage. Experiments on ALFWorld and WebShop show that ORSD accelerates policy improvement and boosts final task performance for Qwen3-1.7B and Qwen3-4B under the same interaction budget. This work addresses the credit assignment problem in reinforcement learning for LLM agents by bridging sparse outcome rewards and dense process supervision without requiring external annotations. It offers a promising direction for improving sample efficiency and training effectiveness of agentic LLMs, which could benefit practitioners working on multi-step reasoning and decision-making tasks. ORSD uses a frozen verifier to evaluate rubric items against trajectories and selects a rubric bundle based on alignment between rubric satisfaction and task success; the likelihood gap between token log-probabilities with and without the rubric provides token-specific process credit. The method is evaluated on two benchmarks with small Qwen3 models, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: In reinforcement learning, the credit assignment problem refers to determining which actions in a sequence are responsible for a final reward, which is especially challenging when rewards are sparse and delayed. For LLM agents that interact with environments over multiple steps, trajectory-level rewards provide little guidance on which intermediate decisions to reinforce or suppress. Process supervision offers denser feedback by evaluating intermediate steps, but typically requires costly human or model annotations. Self-distillation methods leverage a model's own outputs to generate learning signals without external teachers.

A new arXiv paper proposes 'Addition by Subtraction,' a finetuning method that gradually removes identified safety-critical neurons and uses preference optimization to re-establish safety-aligned behavior through other model components. Experiments across four models from three vendors show the resulting models are substantially more robust to pruning-based attacks, reducing attack success rates by up to 90% for some attacks. This work addresses a structural weakness in LLM safety alignment—its reliance on sparse, easily removable components—and could lead to more resilient safety mechanisms that withstand both white-box and black-box jailbreak attempts. It has implications for AI safety researchers and anyone deploying LLMs in adversarial environments. The method generalizes beyond the white-box pruning attacks it was designed for, also improving robustness against refusal-direction ablation and a broad range of black-box jailbreaks, while improving truthfulness and largely preserving general capabilities. However, the approach requires identifying safety-critical neurons in advance and involves a finetuning procedure that may add computational overhead.

## [GraphRail: Graph-Based Step-Level Verification for Search Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06662) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GraphRail introduces a graph-based method that represents requirements, evidence, and reasoning as structured claims and compares them to verify intermediate steps in search-augmented reasoning, without using a generative judge or gold answer. Across three multi-hop QA benchmarks and three reasoning frameworks, it improves error recall over generative verifiers and remains stable under stochastic extraction. This work addresses a critical gap in AI reliability: search-augmented systems can produce correct final answers through flawed intermediate reasoning, and GraphRail provides a traceable, judge-free way to detect and correct such errors. It could improve auditing and trust in AI systems that rely on external evidence, affecting developers and users of multi-hop QA and retrieval-augmented reasoning. GraphRail compares structured claims of requirements, evidence, and reasoning to check whether evidence covers the question, reasoning addresses the question, and reasoning is grounded in evidence. Its diagnoses can also guide online reasoning, enabling traceable detection and correction of intermediate errors.

Generative verifiers use another model to judge reasoning steps, but they can be costly and inconsistent; GraphRail avoids this by using graph-based comparison of structured claims.

The paper introduces ConceptARC at Scale, a generator that produces thousands of ARC-style tasks targeting specific spatial and semantic concepts, and uses it to build ConceptARC-100, a dataset of 1,600 manually reviewed tasks (100 per concept across the 16 ConceptARC concepts), ten times larger than the original benchmark. The authors evaluate recent large reasoning models and from-scratch models on this dataset and analyze their internal representations, finding that task representations cluster by transformation operation (e.g., recoloring, removal, translation) even across different rules and scenarios. This work addresses a real gap: the original ConceptARC benchmark has only a handful of manually constructed tasks per concept, limiting statistical power and making it hard to test whether models form generalizable abstractions rather than memorizing specific tasks. The generator targets the 16 concepts defined by ConceptARC, and the resulting 1,600 tasks were manually reviewed to ensure quality. The analysis of internal representations shows semantic structure clustering by operation type, suggesting models form stable abstractions of transformation operations that generalize across diverse tasks, though the paper is an arXiv preprint and the evaluation focuses on characterizing which concepts models solve reliably versus where they struggle.

**Background**: Best-of-N sampling is an inference-time scaling technique where multiple candidate outputs are generated and the best is selected, trading extra compute for higher quality. LLM agents are models that take sequences of actions in an environment to accomplish tasks, and long-horizon tasks require many steps where errors can compound. The generator-selector framework separates action generation from action selection, allowing off-the-shelf models to be used without fine-tuning.

## [BEAKER: Expert-Curated Benchmark for Embodied AI in Self-Driving Labs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06911) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced BEAKER, a benchmark of 1,000 expert-curated question-answer samples across 11 image and video task types to evaluate multimodal large language models' execution-oriented embodied capabilities in self-driving chemical laboratories. The benchmark was manually created and reviewed by chemistry and embodied AI experts without AI-assisted generation, and 28 MLLMs were evaluated in an offline, single-turn setting. BEAKER addresses an underexplored gap by focusing on execution-oriented embodied capabilities—such as affordance grounding and trajectory planning—rather than general scientific understanding or safety alone. It provides a diagnostic tool that could guide the development of MLLMs as embodied brains for laboratory automation, impacting AI, robotics, and chemistry research. The benchmark covers 11 task types including affordance grounding, trajectory planning, equipment/state/safety recognition, apparatus setup, operation and step reasoning, and temporal procedure reasoning. Evaluation results show that current models can follow coarse experimental procedures in videos but struggle with grounding actions into operable regions and feasible trajectories, recognizing fine-grained apparatus and safety norms, and ordering steps in mid-length procedures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Self-Driving Chemical Laboratories (SDCLs) aim to automate chemical experimentation by integrating AI, robotics, and automation to design, perform, and analyze experiments autonomously. Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) are increasingly considered as 'embodied brains' for such systems, needing to perceive scenes, reason about states, and act under physical and safety constraints. Affordance grounding—identifying where and how to interact with objects—is a key challenge in embodied AI, as models must translate language into precise actions.

<a id="item-108"></a>
## [Hallucination Linearly Decodable from Mid-Layer States in Quantized LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02628) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2606.02628) shows that a linear probe on a single mid-network layer of 4-bit NF4 quantized 7B–8B instruction-tuned models (Llama-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B, Qwen2.5-7B) achieves 0.904–1.000 AUROC for hallucination detection on held-out splits, while sampling-based detectors such as self-consistency and INSIDE EigenScore do not exceed 0.541 AUROC under the same protocol. This finding suggests that truthfulness information in quantized LLMs is approximately linearly separable and concentrated in a consistent mid-layer band, enabling cheap, single-forward-pass hallucination detection that could be integrated into deployment pipelines on modest hardware (the authors release code and data runnable on a single 8 GB GPU). MLP probes rarely surpass linear probes by more than 0.01 AUROC, confirming the signal is approximately linear; peak probing layers fall in blocks 13–18 of 32 for Llama and Mistral and blocks 19–25 of 28 for Qwen on natural-language benchmarks, and first-block attention entropy provides a complementary signal (0.866–0.941 AUROC on HaluEval-QA) at no extra inference cost. The authors note that the low discriminability of sampling methods reflects a structural mismatch between paired-label evaluation and the information those methods access, rather than an inherent limitation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Hallucination detection aims to flag factually incorrect LLM outputs; white-box approaches such as INSIDE use internal hidden states and metrics like EigenScore, while black-box approaches rely on sampling consistency. NF4 (4-bit NormalFloat) is a quantization format used by QLoRA and bitsandbytes that stores weights in a 4-bit normal-quantile codebook, enabling large models to run in low memory. Linear probing is a standard interpretability technique that trains a simple linear classifier on frozen representations to test whether a property is linearly encoded.

## [Behavior Graphs: Unsupervised Graph Structures for Interpreting Robot Deployment Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00405) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces behavior graphs, an unsupervised method that compresses robot policy rollouts into a single interpretable directed graph by identifying similar behavior modes, connecting them temporally, and linking them to outcomes. In a user study on three real-world tasks, 16 of 18 participants preferred behavior graphs over rollout videos, achieving comparable conclusions with 40.3% lower perceived temporal demand and 37.6% lower effort. This work addresses the challenge of making sense of large volumes of high-dimensional robot deployment data, which is critical for data flywheels that aim to continuously improve policies by targeting data collection at failures and successes. By providing a more interpretable and less effortful alternative to video analysis, behavior graphs could help robotics teams make faster and more confident data collection decisions, potentially accelerating the deployment and improvement of robot learning systems. Beyond interpretability, behavior graphs serve as progress predictors: across 43 datasets, they improve the rank correlation between predicted progress and true temporal order by 22.9% and reduce mean absolute error by 53.5% relative to the strongest baseline. The method is unsupervised, relying on learning to identify similar behavior modes without manual labels.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: In robot learning, a data flywheel refers to the continuous loop of deploying a policy, observing its successes and failures, collecting targeted data, and retraining to improve performance. However, robot deployments generate massive amounts of high-dimensional rollout data (e.g., video and sensor streams), which is difficult to analyze and act upon. Behavior graphs aim to structure this data into a compact, interpretable form to support data collection decisions.

## [Do agents need to sleep? Paper maps sleep theory to LLM memory consolidation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02216) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv position paper (2611.02216v2) argues that the three phases of the selectionist 'ringing, resonance and renormalization' (RRR) account of sleep each map onto operations feasible in digital memory architectures for LLM-based agents. It derives a falsifiable prediction that selectionist consolidation will outperform instructionist alternatives as memory stores grow large. Persistent LLM agents already suffer from semantic drift, degraded retrieval precision, error propagation, and memory saturation, so a principled framework for what to forget and when could shape how stateful agent memory systems are designed. The work bridges neuroscience and AI engineering, potentially influencing continual-learning and memory-management practices. The paper is a theoretical position piece without empirical results, offering a falsifiable prediction rather than demonstrated benchmarks. Its core claim is that plasticity has a price, and for any system that learns continuously under finite capacity while coupled to its environment, that price is sleep.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Selectionist theories hold that learning works by selecting among pre-existing variations, whereas instructionist views treat learning as direct instruction. The RRR account of sleep is a selectionist explanation in which sleep phases support memory consolidation, and 'renormalization' echoes the synaptic renormalization (downscaling) hypothesis from sleep neuroscience. LLM agents that persist across sessions accumulate memory and face catastrophic forgetting, the abrupt loss of previously learned information when new information is learned.

## [VenusGenome: Genome-Aware Masked Autoencoding for Microbial Genomes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05405) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VenusGenome introduces a gene-resolution masked autoencoder that uses genome-aware attention incorporating contig boundaries, relative strand orientations, and gene positions, pretrained on over 1.7 million microbial genome assemblies. It achieves the highest mean performance on nine of eleven primary metrics across genome-level phenotype prediction and gene-level tasks. This work shows that biologically informed attention and high-ratio masking can significantly improve microbial function prediction from genome context, which is valuable for computational biology and machine learning. The large-scale pretraining and strong transfer performance suggest a promising direction for genome representation learning. The model uses frozen protein embeddings as gene-level inputs and employs visible-only encoding with high masking ratios, outperforming BERT-style masked modeling. Ablations confirm the contribution of genome-aware attention, and an exploratory perturbation analysis links representation sensitivity to experimentally measured fitness defects.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Microbial genomes are often assembled into contigs, which are contiguous DNA sequences that do not necessarily reflect true adjacency in the genome. Masked autoencoders are self-supervised models that learn representations by reconstructing masked parts of the input, and protein language models provide embeddings that capture biochemical properties of protein sequences. VenusGenome combines these ideas to model genomic context at gene resolution.

## [Omni-Encoder: One Shared SigLIP-2 Transformer for Image, Video, Audio, and Audio-Visual](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05406) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Omni-Encoder is a new multimodal encoder that trains a single SigLIP-2 Vision Transformer with fully shared parameters on image, video, audio, and audio-visual inputs, using a 1D-convolutional audio tokenizer and timestamp-parameterized multimodal rotary position embeddings. It achieves competitive results on 16 benchmarks across all four modalities, and joint training improves each modality over its separately trained counterpart. This work challenges the prevailing practice of training separate image, video, and audio encoders and aligning them post-hoc to a frozen anchor space, suggesting that a single shared parameter set can serve all modalities without per-modality adapters. If validated, it could simplify multimodal representation learning and reduce the engineering overhead of maintaining many modality-specific encoders. The framework uses a modality-native input stage that maps each signal into a shared embedding space, a multimodal rotary position embedding based on absolute timestamps to place visual frames and audio segments in a unified spatiotemporal coordinate system, and alternating-modality training that interleaves batches and accumulates gradients into one update. The paper is an arXiv preprint, and code and models are promised to be publicly released.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: SigLIP-2 is a family of multilingual vision-language encoders from Google that builds on the original SigLIP's sigmoid contrastive loss, adding decoder-based pretraining, self-distillation, and masked prediction to improve dense tasks like segmentation. Rotary position embeddings (RoPE) encode position by rotating query and key vectors, and multimodal variants such as TMRoPE extend this to align positions with real-world timestamps across modalities. Audio tokenization converts audio, often mel-spectrograms, into discrete or embedding tokens so that transformer architectures can process audio alongside visual patches.

## [Systematic mapping study quantifies LLM-based NPC architectures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05427) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A systematic mapping study of 377 papers, with 42 analyzed in depth, provides the first quantitative characterization of how LLM-driven non-player characters (NPCs) are built, covering component adoption, latency, safety coverage, and reproducibility. This study offers a valuable reference for game AI researchers and practitioners by identifying which techniques are common versus rare and highlighting six structural barriers to commercial adoption, helping guide future development and standardization. Short-term memory and world-state grounding are near-universal (60% and 69%), while long-term memory (31%), reflection (38%), and retrieval-augmented generation (14%) remain rare; response times range from 2.6 to 15 seconds, and the study identifies six structural barriers to commercial adoption.

A systematic mapping study is a research method that systematically collects and categorizes existing literature to provide an overview of a field and identify trends and gaps.

## [LIBRA builds executable tool libraries via LLM-proposed contracts and sandbox validation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05445) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced LIBRA (Library Induction by Runtime Assembly), a framework that uses an LLM to propose per-tool contracts—candidate invocations, reusable outputs, state changes, and success conditions—and then validates tool compositions in a sandbox to retain only runtime-supported relations. Evaluated on AppWorld, BFCL, and SciToolEval, LIBRA improves one-shot Recall and NDCG by 7.9 and 3.7 percentage points on average over the same dense retriever, and within ToolReAGt it yields gains of 4.5 and 2.9 points after ten retrieval iterations. Most tool retrievers rank each tool independently by request similarity, which for multi-tool tasks recovers the goal-matching tool but omits prerequisite tools needed by the workflow. LIBRA addresses this gap by constructing an executable tool library before user queries, without requiring dependency annotations, tool-use trajectories, or task-specific training, and it supports both one-shot and agentic retrieval—potentially improving reliability of LLM agents in complex workflows. The library is built before user queries by having an LLM propose tool contracts and then testing compositions in a sandbox, retaining only relations supported at runtime. Gains are measured against the same dense retriever on three benchmarks, but the approach depends on the quality of LLM-proposed contracts and sandbox fidelity, and the abstract does not report absolute performance or computational overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: LLM agents often need to call multiple tools in sequence to complete a task, and retrieving the right set of tools from a large catalog is challenging because tools may have prerequisites. Existing retrievers typically rank tools independently by similarity to the user request, ignoring dependencies between tools. Benchmarks like AppWorld, BFCL, and SciToolEval evaluate tool retrieval and function-calling capabilities in realistic scenarios. LIBRA introduces the idea of an executable tool library where tool relations are validated at runtime rather than annotated manually.

## [OGC-DPO: Active Preference Learning for Multi-Objective LLM Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05506) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced OGC-DPO (Objective-conditioned Gradient Coverage DPO), an active preference learning framework that treats each (response pair, objective) combination as a separate query and selects the most informative ones based on the policy's implicit gradient coverage, without needing an external reward model. Experiments across varying numbers of objectives show it outperforms existing active preference learning methods under the same annotation cost. This work addresses the linear growth of annotation cost with the number of alignment objectives, making multi-objective alignment more scalable and cost-effective. It also challenges the common practice of discarding conflicting preferences, showing they can provide useful training signal. OGC-DPO measures query informativeness via the policy's own implicit gradient, avoiding an external reward model, and selects queries that most expand each objective's gradient coverage. The analysis indicates that informative conflicts, rather than being uniformly harmful, are beneficial for policy training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Aligning LLMs with human preferences often uses Direct Preference Optimization (DPO), which optimizes pairwise preferences without a separate reward model. When aligning to multiple objectives like helpfulness, harmlessness, and honesty, preference judgments are typically collected for each objective, causing annotation costs to grow linearly. Active preference learning reduces this cost by selecting comparisons whose labels are expected to most improve the policy.

## [CoCo Framework Uses LLM to Guide Time Series Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05515) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes Context as Covariates (CoCo), a framework where an LLM distills textual reasoning into forecast and confidence covariates that guide a time series foundation model (TSFM) backbone. It also introduces a scalable method for generating multimodal time series datasets by annotating real-world series with LLMs, and shows that CoCo with Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507 and Chronos2 achieves up to 12% MAE improvement over state-of-the-art TSFMs and LLM forecasters like GPT-4o. This work bridges the gap between LLMs' textual reasoning and TSFMs' numerical precision, enabling more accurate forecasts that incorporate textual context such as news and reports. It also addresses the scarcity of large-scale paired multimodal datasets, which could accelerate research in text-guided time series forecasting across domains like finance, economics, weather, traffic, and security. The framework aligns the two models in stages: first fine-tuning the LLM via Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) with a reward tied to covariate-induced improvement in forecasting performance, then fine-tuning the TSFM on covariates generated by the aligned LLM. The synthetic multimodal corpus generalizes to real benchmarks, and code, checkpoints, and evaluation datasets are publicly available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Time series foundation models (TSFMs) are pre-trained on vast amounts of diverse time series data to enable generalized forecasting with minimal additional training, but they cannot reason about textual information. Large Language Models (LLMs) can interpret text but lack numerical precision for forecasting. Multimodal time series analysis, which combines text and numerical series, is an emerging area, but progress has been limited by the lack of large-scale paired datasets.

The paper introduces BACE (Belief-Aware Critic with Epistemic tracking), a lightweight action chooser built on a frozen language model backbone, and shows it selects the optimal number of clarification questions more consistently than GRPO and ArCHer in a controlled reference game environment derived from SIMMC 2.1. This work addresses a known weakness of LLMs—premature answering—by providing a method that decides when to ask versus answer, which could improve reliability in interactive dialogue systems where ambiguity is common. A new arXiv paper presents the first evaluation of Decision Models (DMs) as human surrogates in economics, comparing 10 DMs with 8 LLMs across a household spending survey, classic economic games, and a labour-market field experiment. Frontier DMs reproduced human response variation more closely than LLMs in all three settings and ran roughly ten times faster than the fastest LLM. ## [Bio-VQA: Largest Biological VQA Benchmark Reveals VLM Gaps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05559) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Bio-VQA introduces the largest biological visual question answering benchmark, comprising 1.86 million questions over 217,000 images from 11 public datasets, covering hierarchical taxonomic reasoning, fine-grained attribute grounding, quantitative spatial reasoning, and individual re-identification. It evaluates 18 open-weight vision-language models (3.75B to 38.4B parameters) and finds that general visual capabilities do not transfer to biological organisms, with taxonomic accuracy dropping from 0.72 at phylum to 0.12 at scientific name and most models failing at trait measurement and re-identification. This benchmark exposes a critical gap in how vision-language models handle biological data, which is essential for biodiversity monitoring and ecological research. It provides a standardized test to drive improvements in domain-specific AI, affecting both the AI/ML community and biodiversity researchers who rely on automated image analysis. Bio-VQA extends right-or-wrong scoring to graded scoring, so near-miss answers count more than distant ones. The best model counts animals exactly on only 41.7% of images with 2 to 10 individuals, less than half its accuracy on everyday objects, and 15 of 18 models do no better than an image-blind baseline on trait size measurement, while 17 of 18 fall below chance on re-identification under adversarial conditions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are AI systems that jointly interpret images and text, extending large language models to multimodal tasks like visual question answering (VQA). VQA benchmarks test a model's ability to answer questions about images, but existing benchmarks in biology focus mostly on species identification within a few taxonomic groups. Individual re-identification—recognizing the same animal across different sightings—is a fundamental task in ecology for studying population dynamics, traditionally done via tagging or DNA sampling.

<a id="item-120"></a>
## [Diagnosing and Repairing Spatial Relation Failures in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05565) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.05565) identifies two distinct causes of spatial relation failures in contrastive vision-language models: access failures, where the relation is present in the pooled embedding but unreachable by native image-text similarity, and pooling failures, where pooling discards the relation while dense features retain it. The authors propose a zero-parameter, query-conditioned spatial moment readout that recomputes geometry from the patch grid and stores it as a vector, repairing both failure modes. This work matters because it provides a diagnostic framework that distinguishes between two failure modes requiring opposite repairs, and offers a lightweight, zero-parameter fix that significantly improves spatial reasoning accuracy without retraining. It could impact any application relying on contrastive vision-language models for spatial understanding, such as robotics, image retrieval, and multimodal reasoning. On a 118,287 image index, the proposed readout raises left/right pairwise accuracy against reversed geometry negatives from 0.51-0.53 to 0.81-0.96 and conditional R@1 among 21 category-matched candidates from 0.05 to 0.37-0.75, at under a millisecond per batched query, while exactly reproducing native ranking for queries that name no relation. The method is evaluated across ten encoders and uses controlled C4 orbits to isolate pooling behavior.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Contrastive vision-language models, such as CLIP, are trained to align images and text in a shared embedding space, enabling zero-shot tasks like image retrieval and classification. However, they are known to struggle with compositional reasoning, including spatial relations like left/right, often performing near chance. This paper diagnoses why such failures occur and proposes a targeted fix that operates downstream of dense features.

## [MESA: A New Benchmark for Generalization in Language-Conditioned Robot Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05567) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced MESA, an evaluation framework with five suites that isolate in-distribution performance and four axes of generalization (spatial, instance, category, composition) for language-conditioned robotic manipulation policies. They also released MESA-Gen, a MimicGen-based data generation pipeline, and BiMESA, the first large-scale benchmark for bimanual generalization, evaluating seven baseline methods with the best achieving only 52% on MESA and 49% on BiMESA. MESA addresses a critical gap in systematically evaluating foundation models for robotic manipulation, enabling researchers to diagnose where and why policies fail rather than just whether they fail. Its rankings are broadly consistent with RoboArena scores, suggesting it captures method-level differences relevant beyond simulation, which could guide future development of more robust embodied AI systems. The framework includes five evaluation suites and is instantiated on two platforms: the single-arm MESA suite and BiMESA for bimanual tasks. MESA-Gen improves upon MimicGen to generate tasks and demonstrations at scale, and the evaluation of seven baselines (diffusion policies, VLM-based approaches, and pretrained VLAs) shows significant room for improvement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Language-conditioned robotic manipulation policies are models that use natural language instructions to specify tasks for a robot to perform. MimicGen is a data generation system that synthesizes large-scale demonstration datasets from a small number of human demonstrations by adapting them to new contexts, enabling scalable training and evaluation. Benchmarks like MESA are essential for measuring progress in generalization, which is the ability of a policy to perform well on unseen spatial configurations, objects, categories, or task compositions.

## [Pre-registered tests challenge physical structure in learned fluid representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05577) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A pre-registered study on 2D decaying Navier-Stokes flow found that a JEPA world model does not organize its latents around physical quantities better than an untrained network, while plain autoencoders capture enstrophy in their slowest latent directions (best r 0.80-0.86 vs 0.38 untrained), and adding VICReg reduces this prominence but raises linear decodability. The negative results challenge the common assumption that self-supervised world models automatically learn physically meaningful latents, suggesting that what a latent contains and what can be controlled through it are separate issues, which impacts how the ML and physics communities evaluate representation learning. The study used three pre-registered experiments on one consumer GPU (~70 logged hours), with all pre-registrations, deviations, code, and outputs public; in Study 3, label-free rules found a loudness dial in all 8 trained models but a shape dial in only 3 of 5 fresh seeds, below the pre-registered bar of 4.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: JEPA (Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture) is a self-supervised framework that learns by predicting abstract representations of inputs rather than reconstructing raw data, often proposed as a world model. VICReg (Variance-Invariance-Covariance Regularization) is a self-supervised method that prevents representation collapse via regularization on embedding variance. Enstrophy is a fluid dynamics quantity related to kinetic energy dissipation, often used to characterize 2D turbulence.

## [Whiel: A Neurosymbolic Platform for Certified Relational Program Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05612) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Whiel is a new Lean-certified neurosymbolic system for verifying imperative relational programs, using Hoare-style reasoning with prophecy variables and leveled invariants, and LLM-discovered loop invariants checked by the Vampire theorem prover. It can encode containment and equivalence problems for Datalog, its extensions with negation, and core graph query languages, as well as optimized or incremental evaluation algorithms and first-order specifications of integrity constraints, schema mappings, and views. This work bridges formal methods, databases, and AI-assisted verification by combining LLM-driven invariant discovery with certified proof checking, potentially enabling high-trust verification of complex query and constraint languages that are typically undecidable. It could impact data integration, exchange, and sharing applications where correctness guarantees are critical. Whiel uses database instances as program states and supports compositional Hoare-style reasoning with first-order relational specifications. Counterexamples from Vampire are fed back into the neurosymbolic pipeline, and proposed solutions are translated into Lean proof certificates; the system is evaluated on a curated benchmark suite.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Hoare logic is a formal system for reasoning about program correctness using axioms and inference rules, where loop invariants are essential for verifying while-loops. Vampire is an automatic theorem prover for first-order classical logic that has won numerous CADE ATP System Competition trophies. Neurosymbolic approaches combine large language models with symbolic verification tools, and recent work such as NeuroInv has shown promise in LLM-based loop invariant generation with counterexample-guided refinement. Lean is an interactive theorem prover that can check proof certificates for high assurance.

## [VIPBench: Human-Aligned Benchmark for Voice Identity Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05638) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VIPBench introduces a benchmark of 124,876 same-or-different voice identity judgments from 1,290 listeners on 9,800 recording pairs from 100 public figures, including real, cloned, and morphed voices, and defines four tasks to measure alignment between speaker-representation models and human perception. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in speaker-recognition evaluation by focusing on human perception, which has direct implications for intellectual property rights, cybersecurity, and everyday life in the era of voice cloning. Listener judgments diverge most for voice clones: in 41% of responses, listeners judged a clone and a real recording of the same speaker as different people; no model reaches the noise ceiling, with the best model explaining 61% of explainable variance, and models trained with speaker labels align better than self-supervised models, though a model with 12% error rate aligns as well as models with error rates below 2%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Speaker-recognition benchmarks typically test how accurately a model recovers the speaker label of a recording, treating voice identity as a property of the person who produced it. However, they do not evaluate how humans perceive voice identity. VIPBench fills this gap by collecting human same-or-different judgments on real, cloned, and morphed speech, and evaluating speaker-representation models against these judgments.

## [LLM Predictions Cut Dantzig-Wolfe Search Complexity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05726) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper proves that predicting which constraint families belong together in Dantzig-Wolfe reformulation can reduce the search complexity from order k^w to Theta(k^e), where e is the number of missed members. The paper introduces JointDW, which verifies language model predictions with a staged search and interleaves them with a lookahead at a bounded loss. This work connects LLM predictions to combinatorial optimization search complexity, framing prediction quality as measurable evaluation savings. It could impact how mixed-integer programming solvers leverage language models to automate reformulation decisions, potentially reducing computational costs in operations research. The paper shows that a one-pass lookahead at an optimal LP dual is provably blind to groups whose families share no variable, while language models can read model names and text. Experiments on MIPLIB-NL models show that strongest LLMs identify joint groups about as often as the lookahead, and reasoning doubles accuracy, but on larger models with distracting families, the lookahead finds the group in one evaluation while LLMs given only text seldom name it.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Dantzig-Wolfe reformulation is a technique that strengthens the linear relaxation of a mixed-integer program by convexifying a chosen set of constraint families, with each candidate requiring a column generation run. Column generation is an iterative algorithm for solving large linear programs by dynamically adding variables. The choice of constraint families is hard when families strengthen the bound only together, as in classical inequalities for lot sizing, network design, and facility location.

## [OdorSpaces: Visual-Olfactory Embodied Navigation Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05738) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OdorSpaces is a new physics-based simulation platform and benchmark that augments Habitat-Sim with 3D odor transport and dispersion simulated by Fire Dynamics Simulator (FDS), enabling agents to navigate photorealistic indoor environments using both visual and olfactory observations. The benchmark includes two source-seeking scenarios—invisible gas-leak localization and early-stage fire localization—and the authors develop a multimodal reinforcement learning policy that outperforms diverse baselines. This work opens a relatively underexplored direction in embodied AI by combining machine olfaction with vision for navigation, which could enable robots to localize hazardous gas leaks or early fires in real-world indoor settings. It provides a standardized benchmark and a strong baseline, potentially accelerating research in multimodal embodied perception and disaster response robotics. The platform leverages FDS, a large-eddy simulation code for low-speed flows with emphasis on smoke and heat transport from fires, to simulate odor dispersion, and builds on Habitat-Sim's high-performance photorealistic rendering. The initial policy integrates visual observations and olfactory measurements over time via multimodal reinforcement learning, but the work is an arXiv preprint without community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Machine olfaction is the automated simulation of the sense of smell, often using electronic noses to detect and analyze airborne chemicals, but it remains challenging due to limited sensor selectivity and complex odor mixtures. Habitat-Sim is a flexible, high-performance 3D simulator for embodied AI research, providing photorealistic indoor environments. Fire Dynamics Simulator (FDS) is a computational fluid dynamics model developed by NIST for simulating smoke and heat transport from fires. OdorSpaces combines these tools to create a benchmark for visual-olfactory navigation, a task where agents must use both sight and smell to find odor sources.

## [SPLIT Learns Hierarchical Patch-Splitting Policies for Efficient Vision Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05742) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce SPLIT (Split Policy Learned Image Tokenization), a method that learns hierarchical patch-splitting policies to allocate token resolution based on model-relevant content rather than pixel-level heuristics. SPLIT matches full-resolution ViT accuracy while using 30% fewer tokens on both classification and dense prediction tasks like COCO and ADE20K. This work shifts adaptive patchification from noisy pixel statistics to task-relevant signals learned by the model, potentially enabling more efficient ViTs for high-resolution images and dense prediction without sacrificing accuracy. It could impact deployment of vision transformers in resource-constrained settings and inspire further research on content-aware tokenization. SPLIT learns a hierarchical policy that decides where to split patches into finer tokens, grounding compute allocation in the model's interpretation of task-relevant information. The method is evaluated on ImageNet classification and dense prediction benchmarks (COCO, ADE20K), showing 30% token reduction at matched accuracy, though the abstract does not specify the exact architecture or training overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Vision Transformers (ViTs) divide an image into a grid of fixed-size patches, each treated as a token; the token count grows quadratically as patch size shrinks, making attention costly for high-resolution inputs. Prior work on adaptive patch sizing, such as APT, uses local entropy heuristics to assign coarser patches to visually simple regions, but these pixel-level statistics may not align with what the model finds meaningful for the task.

## [SME-C: Expert Critique and LLM Refinement for Better AI Evaluation Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05749) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce SME-C (Subject Matter Expert Critique), a human-AI workflow that collects lightweight expert critiques of candidate LLM responses and uses LLM-driven analysis to iteratively refine response datasets and evaluation rubrics. Validated in the financial domain with 3,853 critiques from 411 finance professionals across 520 queries, the workflow showed significant quality improvements in a blind survey with 100 additional experts. This approach offers a scalable and cost-effective alternative to slow, expensive expert authoring for building domain-specific evaluation datasets, which are critical for benchmarking and improving LLMs in specialized fields like finance. It demonstrates how structured expert critique can be combined with LLM refinement to produce high-quality, reproducible evaluation resources. The study compared two LLM refinement strategies: one guided by experts' written critiques and one inferring critique solely from preference votes. Both improved quality over top-ranked baseline responses, with gains concentrated on weaker original responses; critique-based refinement produced more domain-grounded improvements, while vote-based refinement better removed irrelevant content.

Rubrics provide structured scoring criteria for LLM-as-judge evaluations, and human-AI collaboration workflows are increasingly explored to combine expert judgment with AI efficiency. SME-C addresses this by capturing expert knowledge through lightweight critiques rather than full authoring.

## [AMD GPU data pipelining speeds up LLM training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05751) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper presents kernel and framework techniques that orchestrate three AMD GPU data-movement features—Tensor Data Mover (TDM), transpose-load instructions, and System/Remote DMA (SDMA/RDMA)—to pipeline data to compute units during LLM training. The implementations achieve average kernel-level speedups of 1.88x for TDM and 1.19x for transpose-load, and 1.16x (SDMA) and 1.33x (RDMA) for computation overlapped with communication, and have been integrated into AMD's open-source Primus and Primus-Turbo frameworks. This is the first AMD LLM training system to utilize all GPU data pipelining features, directly attacking compute idle time caused by data movement—a bottleneck that becomes increasingly costly at scale. It strengthens AMD's software ecosystem for AI training and offers an alternative to NVIDIA's proprietary solutions. The speedups are measured at the kernel level and for overlapped compute-communication, with TDM moving tensor data from HBM to on-chip memory and transpose-load rearranging data into the required layout during load. The work focuses on explicit data-movement hardware units that do not consume compute capabilities of the Compute Units, and the code is available in AMD's Primus and Primus-Turbo repositories.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Large language model training requires massive computation, but GPUs often sit idle while waiting for data to arrive from slow memory or other GPUs. AMD GPUs include specialized hardware for explicit data movement—such as the Tensor Data Mover (TDM) for HBM-to-on-chip transfers, transpose-load instructions for on-the-fly layout changes, and DMA engines (SDMA/RDMA) for inter-GPU communication—that can be orchestrated to keep compute units busy. This paper builds a hierarchical pipeline using these features to overlap data movement with computation, reducing idle time and cost at scale.

## [LLMs Struggle to Impersonate Individual Authors; Gemini 3 Pro Copies Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05771) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A comparative evaluation of six LLMs (Gemini 3 Pro, Claude Opus, GPT 5.2, GPT 5.2 Pro, DeepSeek 3.2, and Grok 4.1 Fast) found that most models struggle to impersonate individual authors' writing styles. Gemini 3 Pro showed the strongest apparent mimicry, but overlap analysis revealed it extensively copied sentences and phrases from training texts, far exceeding human-level reuse. This study highlights the need to distinguish genuine stylistic imitation from direct textual reuse in LLM-generated content, which has important implications for forensic analysis of textual evidence and AI safety. It also suggests that apparent impersonation success may be an artifact of training data memorization rather than true style transfer. The evaluation used short product review texts with high within-author consistency and two types of prompts: a simple minimal-instruction prompt and a complex prompt with explicit stylometric guidance. Authorship mimicry was measured via cosine distance to training texts and visual analyses of embedding distributions; factor analyses showed training set consistency was the most robust predictor of mimicry success, while author distinctiveness did not generalize.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Stylometry is the quantitative study of writing style, often used for authorship attribution—determining who wrote a text based on linguistic features like sentence length and word frequency. Large language models can generate text in various styles, raising concerns about their potential to impersonate individuals. Cosine distance is a common metric for measuring similarity between text embeddings, which are vector representations of text.

## [Unsupervised Neural Operators Solve Rotating Relativistic Star Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05790) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces physics-informed neural operators that solve the axisymmetric general relativistic hydrostatic equilibrium equations for rotating neutron stars in a fully unsupervised setting, achieving a 10^3× speed-up with negligible accuracy loss. This enables high-fidelity equation of state inference and pulsar spin evolution modeling at scale. This work addresses a computationally prohibitive problem in nuclear astrophysics, allowing scalable equation of state inference that self-consistently accounts for rotation and repeated stellar-structure calculations during binary evolution. It could significantly impact the neutron star and gravitational wave communities by making population synthesis with realistic rotational structure and EoS uncertainties computationally accessible. The neural operators are trained solely on the partial differential equations without any labeled data, and they achieve a 10^3× speed-up compared to traditional numerical solvers. The approach enables repeated stellar-structure calculations during binary evolution, which were previously prohibitive, and makes spin predictions that account for rotational structure and EoS uncertainties feasible for population synthesis.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Neutron stars are extremely dense remnants of massive stars, and their mass-radius measurements help constrain the equation of state of degenerate nuclear matter. Neural operators are deep learning models that learn mappings between function spaces, serving as fast surrogates for solving partial differential equations. Physics-informed neural networks embed physical laws into the training process, allowing them to learn solutions even with limited data. The axisymmetric general relativistic hydrostatic equilibrium equations describe rotating neutron stars but are computationally expensive to solve repeatedly, which has limited previous studies to non-rotating approximations or simplified rotational corrections.

## [Consultation Value Distillation Helps LLM Agents Decide When to Ask Humans](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05814) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Consultation Value Distillation (CVD), a method that estimates executor-relative consultation values offline using Monte Carlo executor rollouts and distills them into lightweight predictors for budgeted allocation of human consultations. Across five batch and three sequential settings with two frontier executors, CVD improves allocation through joint and continuation-aware value learning, outperforming direct prompting and Value of Information where applicable. This work addresses the underexplored problem of allocating limited human decision capacity for LLM agents, which is crucial as agents automate more tasks but human oversight remains essential for private context, authority, or judgment. The method enables efficient human-AI collaboration without expensive deployment-time rollouts, potentially impacting how agent systems are designed for real-world applications. CVD requires no rollout-based value estimation at deployment and remains robust with substantially less rollout supervision, enabling nearly lossless approximate allocation at low search cost. However, independent consultation values remain competitive in several settings, and the abstract is truncated, limiting full assessment of the method's scope.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Large language model agents increasingly automate complex tasks, but human decision capacity does not scale accordingly. In many applications, humans provide private context, authority, or judgments that agents lack. The problem of human decision capacity allocation asks: given multiple consultation opportunities and a limited budget, at which decisions should an LLM agent consult a human? The central challenge is that consultation value is executor-dependent, as the same human input may benefit different downstream agents differently.

## [SecPatRec: Hybrid LLM and Static Analysis for Security Pattern Recovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05823) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present SecPatRec, a hybrid approach that uses a ReAct-style LLM agent for role detection and grounding, combined with static code property graph analysis for interaction recovery, to automatically recover security pattern implementations from source code. They also curate a human-validated dataset of 100 pattern instances across five security patterns in 93 open-source Java and Python repositories, and introduce a three-level evaluation framework. Automated recovery of security pattern implementations can support security-by-design by enabling auditors and developers to verify that prescribed security architectures are actually implemented in code. The hybrid approach achieves higher aggregate recovery scores and lower estimated LLM API costs than a one-shot LLM baseline, while handling repositories that exceed the baseline's input limit. Averaged over four LLMs (both proprietary and open-source), SecPatRec achieves role-detection accuracy of 0.84, role-grounding similarity of 0.84, and an interaction-recovery score of 0.66. The recovered role mappings and statically derived interaction sequences provide structural and behavioral evidence to support inspection of security-pattern implementations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Security patterns are reusable architectural solutions to recurring security problems, and security-by-design advocates integrating them into software development. Recovering their implementations is hard because patterns are abstract, span multiple components, and allow many valid implementations. A code property graph (CPG) is a program representation that combines syntactic structure, control flow, and data dependencies, often used for vulnerability discovery. ReAct-style LLM agents interleave reasoning and actions, such as code search, to solve multi-step tasks.

<a id="item-134"></a>
## [PEARL: Adaptive Regularization for Selective Forgetting in Non-Stationary Bandits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05842) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PEARL introduces a prediction error-adaptive regularization method that reverses the Gram matrix update only along feature directions where predictions are surprisingly wrong, enabling selective forgetting under partial preference drift. The authors prove that PEARL's cumulative regret scales with the number of shifted users N_s rather than the total population N, and on the PRISM benchmark (1,500 users, 21 LLMs) it outperforms greedy by +4.0 points and the best neural bandit baseline by +2.5 points. This work addresses a key limitation of existing non-stationary bandit methods that forget uniformly across all dimensions, which is inefficient when only some preference dimensions drift. It has direct implications for personalized recommendation, LLM response selection, and RLHF reward models, where preferences evolve partially over time. The selection policy is a ~100K-parameter MLP that runs in milliseconds on CPU, leaves LLM weights frozen, and works with any API-based model. The paper also proves that any non-adaptive policy suffers linear regret after a preference shift and that no fixed exploration rate achieves low regret in both stationary and non-stationary regimes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Non-stationary bandits are a variant of the multi-armed bandit problem where reward distributions change over time, requiring algorithms to adapt rather than assume fixed preferences. Neural contextual bandits, such as NeuralUCB, use neural networks to approximate reward functions and maintain exploration via a Gram matrix that accumulates confidence monotonically. In personalized settings, user preferences often drift partially—some features become stale while others remain valid—but standard methods forget uniformly, discarding useful information.

## [DualControlNet: Generative AI for Urban Land-Use Maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05859) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced DualControlNet, a generative model that jointly synthesizes high-resolution land-use maps and satellite imagery from text prompts, trained on over 40,000 paired tiles from Orlando and Chicago. It improves land-use diversity consistency by 62.8% and building coverage ratio consistency by 16.3% over unimodal baselines, more than doubles cross-modal spatial consistency, and triples visual quality. This work addresses key gaps in urban generative AI—multimodality, controllability, and domain-specific verification—by enabling intuitive text-driven exploration of urban futures, which could lower technical barriers for planners and support sustainable development scenario planning. The model is evaluated using land-use, spatial consistency, and image quality metrics rather than generic image quality alone, and scenario-based experiments show it can respond to prompts like 'high-density development' to increase density and diversity. However, it is trained only on data from two U.S. cities, which may limit generalizability to other urban contexts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Land-use models traditionally forecast transitions based on numeric data but do not synthesize new maps from text. ControlNet is a neural network structure that adds spatial conditions to diffusion models for precise image generation control. Generative AI in urban systems has been mostly unimodal and weakly controllable, evaluated by generic image quality rather than domain metrics. DualControlNet builds on ControlNet to jointly generate land-use maps and satellite imagery, enabling multimodal and controllable synthesis.

## [AI Assistance Can Hurt When Humans Neglect Signal Correlation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05870) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.05870) develops a behavioral model of AI-assisted binary classification with two primitives—the worker's awareness of evidence overlap and her trust in the AI—and derives a closed-form phase diagram over AI capability and overlap that partitions outcomes into complementarity, impairment, and automation regions. On ChatBench, AI assistance raises accuracy by 21 points, but workers apply only about a quarter of the Bayes-rational weight to AI information; GPT-4o falls in the automation region while Llama-3.1-8b sits at the complementarity boundary. The framework shows that a more capable AI can actually reduce decision accuracy when humans double-count shared evidence, meaning deployment decisions cannot rely on AI accuracy alone. It offers a principled way to diagnose whether accuracy losses stem from correlation neglect on the AI side or undertrust on the human side, which matters for designing and calibrating human-AI decision systems. The model identifies its two parameters empirically by pairing the same workers with different AIs, decomposing accuracy loss into a correlation-neglect cost (reducing overlap on the AI side) and an undertrust cost (calibrating trust on the human side). Calibrating trust recovers about 2.6 points for GPT-4o but almost nothing for Llama-3.1-8b, and undertrust can sometimes compensate for correlation neglect, so the trust level maximizing accuracy depends on deployment parameters.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Correlation neglect is a documented cognitive bias in which people treat redundant or correlated information sources as if they were independent, leading to overconfident, double-counted beliefs. In AI-assisted decision-making, the human's own signal and the AI's recommendation may share overlapping evidence, so a worker who ignores this overlap may over-weight the AI. This paper formalizes that interaction and maps when AI assistance complements, impairs, or automates human judgment.

## [CellCanon: Agentic Cellular World Model Unifies Prediction, Explanation, and Updating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05890) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CellCanon is introduced as the first agentic cellular world model that unifies single-cell expression prediction, evidence-grounded explanation, and observation-driven updating. On 339 held-out SciPlex3 conditions across three cell lines, it improves differential-expression AUPRC and Macro-F1 over the best baseline by 63.3% and 17.3%, respectively, and direction Macro-F1 by 7.2%. This work shows that an agentic world-model framework can support cumulative biological discovery by revising predictions and judgments as new measurements arrive, potentially accelerating hypothesis generation in computational biology. Its dramatic reduction in language-model token use (99.77% for Predict and 98.41% for Explain) also suggests a more efficient alternative to gene-wise reasoning. The predictive core reuses condition-level biological evidence across gene-specific numerical readouts, and a shared world state links predictions, acquired measurements, and source-linked claims. In simulated experimental feedback, Update exceeds the best fine-tuned baselines in differential-expression AUPRC and Macro-F1 using one measured source context per drug without changing model weights.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Single-cell perturbation datasets such as SciPlex3 measure how cells respond to chemical perturbations at the transcriptomic level, and predicting responses to unseen conditions is a central challenge in computational biology. Differential-expression AUPRC and Macro-F1 are metrics for evaluating how well in-silico models rank or classify differentially expressed genes. World models, which maintain an internal state of an environment, have recently been combined with agentic AI to support reasoning and updating in scientific domains.

## [Intervention-blind test measures clinical trial simulators' true skill](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05907) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces an intervention-blind benchmark that quantifies how much of a clinical trial simulator's accuracy depends on knowing which intervention was tested, evaluated on 7,207 registered trials from ClinicalTrials.gov. It constructs 9,996 audited within-trial contrasts and defines interventional skill as the error reduction relative to a reference model trained without intervention-identifying fields. This work provides a measurable, scalable way to assess whether AI models truly learn causal intervention effects rather than relying on shortcuts, which is crucial for trusting patient world models in healthcare. It could influence how clinical trial simulators are validated and ranked, impacting AI/ML in healthcare and causal inference research. On a temporal split, a registry-trained gradient-boosting simulator removes a fifth of the error of a trivial baseline but has interventional skill of -0.12 (95% CI -0.21 to -0.05), meaning withholding the intervention improves it; a frontier language model achieves interventional skill 0.20 [0.08, 0.31] and uses the intervention as such, reversing predictions in 86% of arm swaps. The blind reference is not fully drug-blind (a language model recovers the withheld drug class 45% of the time), so interventional skill is a conservative lower bound.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Patient world models are AI systems proposed as simulators of clinical trials, aiming to predict patient outcomes under different treatments. Their capabilities are often ranked on a ladder from temporal prediction to counterfactual rollouts, but position is typically assigned by reading papers rather than by measurement. This paper turns the counterfactual rung into a measured quantity using data from ClinicalTrials.gov, a public registry of clinical trials.

## [Saliency maps less reliable for dense breasts in mammography AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05909) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv study audited three CNN mammography classifiers on 1,345 malignant CBIS-DDSM views and found that Grad-CAM peaks hit only 44% of dense-breast lesions versus 58% of non-dense lesions. Case mix, mainly more calcifications in dense breasts, explained about half or more of each gap, but occlusion maps retained a significant average gap of -0.56 bits after adjustment. Saliency maps are often presented as evidence that mammography classifiers focus on cancers, so their reduced reliability in dense breasts—where cancers are harder to see—undermines trust in explainable AI for a large patient subgroup. The findings argue for routine reporting of density gaps with case-mix adjustment and controls, which could change how medical AI explanations are validated and regulated. The audit scored Grad-CAM, Integrated Gradients, and occlusion maps using peak-hit accuracy and a chance-normalized lesion-to-background saliency ratio in bits, where +1 bit doubles the ratio. Controls showed unadjusted gaps exceeded those of an image-only conspicuity map and that models relied less on dense-breast lesions, implicating the classifiers themselves rather than only image difficulty.

**Background**: Saliency maps such as Grad-CAM, Integrated Gradients, and occlusion maps are visualization techniques that highlight which parts of an image a deep learning model relies on for its prediction. Dense breast tissue appears white on mammograms and can mask cancers, making detection and localization harder. CBIS-DDSM is a public dataset of curated mammography studies with verified pathology, widely used for computer-aided detection and diagnosis research.

A new arXiv paper introduces IntrAA-SD, a task for attributing generated images to their source fine-tuned Stable Diffusion models that share the same architecture, along with a 140K-image benchmark dataset and a baseline method using a style-conditioned Mixture-of-Experts network. This work addresses a timely problem in AI provenance: as many popular image generators are fine-tunes of the same base model, distinguishing between them is crucial for copyright, safety, and accountability, and the new benchmark could spur further research in this direction. The dataset contains images from different fine-tunes of SDXL (e.g., Juggernaut XL and RealVis XL) with in-domain and out-of-distribution prompts, and the baseline uses a frozen CLIP backbone to capture style and gate a Mixture-of-Experts that processes features from a high-resolution LoRA-adapted DINOv2 network.

Source attribution of AI-generated images aims to identify which model produced a given image, which is important for detecting misuse and enforcing content policies. ## [Confucius4-R2T2: Stable-Prefix Streaming ASR with Longest Stable Prefix](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06043) ⭐️ 7.0/10

NetEase Youdao released Confucius4-R2T2, a stable-prefix streaming ASR model that never revises its earlier output, obtained by post-training Qwen3-ASR-1.7B on 1,500 hours of Chinese and English audio while freezing the pre-trained audio encoder and projector. The adaptation introduces the Longest Stable Prefix (LSP) concept, with proofs that LSP is monotone under prefix order, consistent with full-audio output, and maximal among labels equal to a whole earlier output. Streaming ASR is increasingly consumed by machines such as translators and voice agents that act on each token as it appears, so revised tokens cause wasted work or wrong actions. R2T2 addresses this pain point by delivering stable text with low emission lag while approaching the base model's full-context quality, and its gains extend beyond the post-training languages. The method converts a full-context model into a stable-prefix streaming one without any architectural change, using LSP labels for post-training. Evaluations show streaming recognition close to the base model's full-context quality, and the model weights and code are open-sourced on Hugging Face and GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Qwen3-ASR-1.7B is a flagship multilingual ASR model from the Qwen3-ASR family, supporting streaming and offline inference across 30 languages and 22 Chinese dialects. Traditional streaming ASR often revises earlier tokens as more audio arrives, which is problematic for downstream machine consumers. The Longest Stable Prefix concept defines the longest prefix of output that will not change given future audio, enabling stable streaming without architectural modifications.

## [SysPilot Uses Library Performance Contracts for Cross-Library Autotuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06078) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SysPilot introduces Library Performance Contracts (LPC) and a specification language called LPCL to enable cross-library performance autotuning. The system synthesizes contracts from documentation and code, composes them for an application, derives resource-conflict hypotheses, and selects configurations, improving performance by up to 38% over OpenTuner and SMAC3 while using 3x fewer runs. This work addresses a critical but underexplored problem: resource contention when independently developed libraries run together in scientific and ML applications. By making performance knowledge reusable via contracts, SysPilot could significantly reduce the manual effort needed to tune complex multi-library systems, benefiting performance engineers and application developers. SysPilot matches autotuners that rely on application-specific manual information while retaining cross-application reuse. The LPCL specification captures a library's resource demands, assumptions, controls, and observability signals, and contracts can be reused across many applications that depend on the same library.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Modern scientific and machine-learning applications often compose many independently developed libraries, each with its own runtime that manages resources like CPU cores, GPUs, and memory bandwidth. When these libraries run together, they can oversubscribe shared resources, causing contention and performance degradation. Existing autotuners do not explicitly model cross-library resource sharing, making them inefficient at finding configurations that mitigate contention. SysPilot addresses this by introducing Library Performance Contracts that formalize resource requirements and enable systematic cross-library tuning.

## [Locally Differentially Private Watermarks Reconcile Privacy and Provenance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06100) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces locally differentially private watermarks, specifically the Red-Green Sequence (RGS) watermark, which shifts the goal of language model watermarking from tracking individuals to estimating aggregate usage. The RGS watermark provably limits the evidence any individual text contributes to detection while enabling accurate population frequency estimation, achieving 0.0019 MSE and 0.0038 mean absolute bias at ε=1 on C4 tasks. This work addresses a key tension in AI regulation: users resist fine-grained tracking as a privacy violation and evade watermarks via paraphrasing, undermining their effectiveness. By providing provable local differential privacy guarantees while still enabling accurate aggregate usage estimation, the approach could make watermarking more acceptable for deployment and more useful for regulatory oversight of language models. The RGS watermark is computationally efficient and performs well across various models, sample sizes, and generation entropies in realistic deployment scenarios. In a challenging essay-writing task with only 100 text samples, it still achieves errors on the order of 0.0113 MSE and 0.004 mean absolute bias at ε=2, though performance degrades compared to larger-scale settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Language model watermarking modifies model outputs so they can later be identified as AI-generated, which is important for content ownership, accountability, and regulation. However, traditional watermarking relies on fine-grained tracking of individual texts, raising privacy concerns and prompting users to evade detection through paraphrasing. Local differential privacy (LDP) is a privacy model where each user perturbs their data locally before sharing, ensuring that even an adversary with access to personal responses cannot learn much about any individual. This paper combines LDP with watermarking to enable private aggregate estimation rather than individual tracking.
