## [Text-Only LLM Training Achieves Zero-Shot Video Temporal Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06149) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers trained a text-only LLM (Qwen3-8B with LoRA on CLIP or LLM2CLIP text embeddings) on a 134K-example corpus of textual video descriptions, without any paired video data, and evaluated it on Charades-STA and QV-Highlights for zero-shot temporal grounding. LLM2CLIP achieved 61.54 mtIoU on Temporal-TextVid versus 53.86 for CLIP, and the proposed Nearest Entity Replacement (NER) mapping improved video inference to 14.45 mtIoU on Charades-STA and 20.72 on QV-Highlights. This work shows that temporal grounding can be learned from text alone, removing the need for costly paired video-text annotations and pointing toward annotation-free multimodal training. It also quantifies the modality gap between text and video representations, highlighting that better-aligned encoders are needed for reliable zero-shot video understanding. The model receives only text during training and video at inference; replacing text with frame embeddings at inference yields only 4.99 mtIoU on Charades-STA and 11.12 on QV-Highlights, revealing a large modality gap. The training-free NER mapping represents each video frame by the normalized mean of its nearest text-entity embeddings, outperforming the strongest of four modality-gap baselines by 5.00 and 1.71 points, though absolute performance remains modest.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Video temporal grounding (or localization) is the task of finding the start and end times of a moment in a video that matches a natural language query. Video-LLMs are typically trained on paired video-text data with precise temporal annotations, which are expensive to collect. CLIP is a vision-language model that aligns images and text in a shared embedding space, while LLM2CLIP enhances CLIP's text encoder with a large language model to better handle long, detailed captions. LoRA is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that freezes the base model and injects small trainable matrices, greatly reducing training cost.

## [New Method Unlearns Cross-Modal Associations in Multimodal Reasoning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06168) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.06168) introduces cross-modal association unlearning for multimodal large reasoning models (MLRMs), proposing Visual-Keyed Counterfactual Rebinding (VKCR), a parameter-efficient framework that removes a designated visual-to-knowledge association while preserving access to the same knowledge in permitted contexts. The authors also introduce CLEAR-R and MLLMU-R benchmarks to evaluate target leakage, hallucinated reassociation, reasoning preservation, and modality-specific utility. This work addresses a real gap in multimodal unlearning: existing methods target samples, concepts, or knowledge content but do not separate knowledge from the cross-modal association through which it is accessed, which is critical for privacy when a visual input should no longer unlock sensitive information. It is timely given growing privacy concerns in MLRMs and could influence how model editors handle reasoning traces that may leak forgotten information. VKCR localizes edits to a compact association-conditioned parameter subspace and counterfactually rebinds reasoning behavior by discouraging target-bearing or unsupported continuations while favoring valid non-sensitive alternatives. Experiments show substantial reductions in image-conditioned target leakage and hallucinated reassociation across heterogeneous task formulations, while preserving non-sensitive visual reasoning and permitted text-only knowledge.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Machine unlearning is a technique for removing specific undesired elements—such as private data, outdated information, or harmful content—from a trained model without retraining from scratch. Multimodal large reasoning models (MLRMs) integrate visual inputs with textual and parametric knowledge, enabling cross-modal associations that can inadvertently expose sensitive information during reasoning. Existing multimodal unlearning methods mainly target samples, concepts, or knowledge content, but they do not separate the knowledge from the cross-modal association through which it is accessed, and in reasoning models target information may persist in intermediate reasoning steps even if absent from the final answer.

<a id="item-146"></a>
## [GEdit: Geometry-Aware Regularization Improves LLM Knowledge Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06185) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GEdit introduces a geometry-aware framework built on a Low-Rank Plus Diagonal (LR+D) factor model that captures the intrinsic low-rank structure of Transformer FFN hidden representations. It features two mechanisms: low-rank regularization that protects a core knowledge subspace, and adaptive regularization that adjusts strength based on edit difficulty, outperforming state-of-the-art baselines on GPT2-XL, GPT-J, and LLaMA-3-8B under large-scale editing scenarios with up to 10,000 continuous updates. Knowledge editing is increasingly important for updating LLMs without costly retraining, and GEdit's geometry-aware approach addresses key limitations of existing methods by distinguishing core versus non-core knowledge and hard versus easy edits. This could lead to more reliable and efficient model updates, benefiting researchers and practitioners who need to correct or refresh model knowledge at scale. The LR+D factorization captures the low-rank structure of FFN hidden representations, and the adaptive regularization relaxes constraints for hard edits to ensure success while tightening them for easy edits to preserve pretrained knowledge. Experiments cover both mini-batch and fully sequential editing settings with up to 10,000 continuous updates, though the paper does not specify computational overhead compared to baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Knowledge editing aims to modify specific facts or behaviors in a large language model without full retraining, which is crucial for keeping models up-to-date and correcting errors. Transformer models process information through feedforward networks (FFNs), and recent work has shown that the hidden representations in these layers often exhibit a low-rank structure, meaning they can be approximated by lower-dimensional subspaces. Low-Rank Plus Diagonal (LR+D) factorization is a technique that represents a matrix as the sum of a low-rank component and a diagonal component, often used to capture both shared and individual parameter effects efficiently.

<a id="item-147"></a>
## [Rubric Curriculum RL Tackles Reward Hacking in Open-Ended Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06244) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce Rubric Curriculum RL (RcRL), a method that combines pairwise preferences over rollouts with a non-stationary curriculum over rubric criteria to exploit the generation-verification gap without new data, human annotations, or stronger teacher models. RcRL keeps improving past 1000 steps while baselines plateau within a few hundred steps, achieving a 70.5% human win rate on creative writing and 14.6% (full set) and 25.4% (consensus subset) improvements on HealthBench over the base model. This work addresses the challenge of extending reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) to open-ended domains where ground-truth verification is unavailable and learned reward models are prone to reward hacking. By providing a more discriminative and non-stationary training signal, RcRL could enable more reliable RL fine-tuning for tasks like creative writing and health question answering, impacting AI researchers and practitioners working on alignment and reward modeling. RcRL uses pairwise preferences over rollouts instead of absolute scoring, yielding a more discriminative signal, and employs a curriculum over rubric criteria to create a non-stationary objective that prevents overfitting to static rubrics. On HealthBench, it outperforms all training baselines, including instance-specific rubrics, and preserves output entropy during training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) has driven major gains in math and code generation by using automatically checkable rewards, but extending it to open-ended domains is hard because correctness is subjective and human annotation is costly. Learned reward models often suffer from reward hacking, where the model exploits flaws in the reward function to achieve high scores without genuinely improving. Rubric-based judges, which score outputs against a set of criteria, are a common alternative but are often imprecise and stationary, allowing policies to overfit or hack them. The generation-verification gap refers to the observation that judging the quality of an output is easier than producing a good output, which can be leveraged to create better training signals.

<a id="item-148"></a>
## [Pseudo-Riemannian Attention Network for Heterophilic Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06265) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Graph Signature Index (s_G) to quantify geometric mismatch in heterophilic graphs and proposes a Pseudo-Riemannian Attention Network (PRAT) that uses signed squared pseudo-distances in attention logits, initialized from s_G. Across fifteen node-classification benchmarks, PRAT outperforms or matches the strongest heterophily-adapted models. This work challenges the positive-definite geometric prior common in many GNNs and offers a principled alternative for heterophilic graphs where edges indicate dissimilarity. It could impact the design of attention mechanisms and representation learning on non-homophilous data. The Graph Signature Index is the inertia of a class-compatibility matrix built from label topology, and the authors prove that no edge or node homophily index in (0,1) rules out a negative eigenvalue. A controlled ablation shows that forbidding the metric to be indefinite costs accuracy on graphs marked as indefinite by s_G, tying performance to the sign structure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) often assume homophily, where connected nodes share labels, and many use positive-definite geometric priors such as Riemannian manifolds or dot-product attention. Heterophilic graphs, where edges often connect dissimilar nodes, violate this assumption. Pseudo-Riemannian geometry relaxes positive-definiteness, allowing indefinite metrics that can assign negative squared distances, which may better capture repulsive relationships.

<a id="item-149"></a>
## [NEXTGAN Simplifies and Scales GAN Training for Image and Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06270) ⭐️ 7.0/10

NEXTGAN introduces a gradient-regression-based adversarial training method that trains a scalar critic by regressing its input gradient onto displacements between reference and generated features in frozen representation spaces. It achieves one-step FIDs of 2.87 with pixel-space JiT-B and 1.54 with latent-space DiT-XL/1 on ImageNet 256x256, and scales to 14B parameter models for image and video generation. This method simplifies GAN training by eliminating auxiliary losses, online teacher scores, and trajectories, making it easier to scale adversarial training to large pre-trained models. It enables high-quality one- and few-step generation, which is crucial for efficient image and video synthesis, and sets new state-of-the-art one-step FID scores. The regression is equivalent to an adversarial objective with a gradient-energy penalty for a fixed generator, while the generator retains the standard potential-based GAN update. For text-to-video generation, a single trained Wan2.1-1.3B model supports one-, two-, and four-step sampling, achieving the highest VBench total scores among compared 1.3B methods at one and two steps, with a two-step score of 83.13, exceeding rCM and AnyFlow by 1.25 and 0.47 points respectively.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) train a generator and a critic in opposition, but conventional adversarial losses supervise critic values without directly specifying the gradients used to update the generator. Fréchet Inception Distance (FID) is a metric that measures the similarity between real and generated images by comparing deep feature embeddings, with lower values indicating better quality. Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) are a class of generative models that replace U-Nets with transformers operating on latent patches, known for scalability. NEXTGAN builds on these concepts by using gradient regression to train the critic, simplifying the adversarial training process.

## [OccSplat: Occlusion-Aware Gaussian Densification for Driving Scene Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06273) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OccSplat introduces Occlusion-Aware Gaussian Densification, a feed-forward framework that completes driving-scene reconstructions directly in 3D by predicting new Gaussian primitives for regions lacking support, rather than inpainting rendered pixels. It uses Gaussian Occlusion Mapping to identify unsupported occluded regions and Gaussian Occlusion Completion to generate new primitives, integrated into the original representation without per-scene optimization. This addresses a real limitation of feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting for driving scenes: regions occluded in input views become under-constrained and incomplete from novel viewpoints. By targeting missing 3D geometry instead of image-space inpainting, OccSplat could improve novel-view synthesis quality and coherence for autonomous driving simulation and perception, benefiting the 3D reconstruction and autonomous driving communities. Experiments on the nuScenes benchmark show OccSplat improves over compared methods, with particularly large gains in occluded regions, while retaining feed-forward inference without per-scene optimization. The method explicitly targets missing scene geometry rather than missing pixels, distinguishing it from image-space inpainting approaches.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: 3D Gaussian Splatting is a volume rendering technique that represents scenes as a set of 3D Gaussians and enables real-time novel-view synthesis. Feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting methods can efficiently reconstruct driving scenes from surround-view images in a single pass, but they rely on a single observation, leaving occluded regions under-constrained. Novel-view synthesis generates images from new viewpoints, and missing geometry in occluded areas can cause artifacts when those areas become visible.

<a id="item-151"></a>
## [Reproduction Study Exposes Limits of Risk-Controlled LEO Throughput Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06307) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A reproduction study of BG-CFQS, a state-of-the-art method for risk-budgeted throughput forecasting on LEO satellite links, finds that its risk guarantee is conditional on the calibration split and fails to serve risk budgets below 0.15, with overshoots up to 3.7x. The study evaluates the method on the original three Starlink traces plus an independent fourth dataset. The findings challenge the practical applicability of a state-of-the-art risk-controlled forecasting method for satellite networks, showing that its guarantees may not hold in realistic deployment conditions such as temporal splits. This impacts researchers and practitioners in ML reliability and satellite networking who rely on such methods for safe admission control. Under a random calibration split, the method's risk guarantee is reproduced on all three datasets, but under a contiguous temporal split it holds on only one of three. Additionally, static conformal calibration cannot improve ranking, while online recalibration tracks the budget within 1% from 0.05 to 0.35, but group-conditional conditioning often degrades performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: LEO satellite networks like Starlink provide broadband via low Earth orbit satellites, but their throughput fluctuates rapidly due to satellite mobility and handovers. Risk-budgeted throughput forecasting converts a point forecast into a conservative bound with a user-specified overestimation rate, enabling safe admission control. BG-CFQS is a state-of-the-art method that combines conformal prediction with online recalibration to achieve this. Conformal prediction is a framework for distribution-free uncertainty quantification that uses calibration data to produce prediction intervals or sets with guaranteed coverage.

## [Prism: Bidirectional Self-Teaching Post-Training for Unified Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06343) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Prism introduces a bidirectional self-teaching post-training method that decouples self-generated interleaved text-image rollouts into separate text and image chains, assesses each chain's reliability, and constructs training targets accordingly. Experiments on SenseNova-U1 and BAGEL show gains in both generation (GenEval 85.8→89.2, DPG 86.1→87.3) and understanding (MME 2282.6→2311.6, MMVP 58.0→62.0). This offers a way to improve both understanding and generation in unified multimodal models without an external verifier or ground-truth corrected trajectories, addressing the common problem of mutual degradation when jointly training these capabilities. It could simplify post-training pipelines for UMMs and make self-improvement more practical. Prism derives supervision from the model's own interleaved rollouts by rating the reliability of the text and image chains, and it avoids relying on externally annotated corrected trajectories during target construction. The reported gains are consistent across two different UMMs, SenseNova-U1 and BAGEL, though the work is a single arXiv preprint.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Unified Multimodal Models (UMMs) aim to handle both visual understanding and image generation within a single architecture, such as SenseNova-U1 and BAGEL. Post-training these models is challenging because joint training can cause understanding and generation to degrade each other, while decoupled training misses cross-task benefits. Interleaved text-image rollouts are sequences where text and images alternate, and prior methods either use understanding as a fixed teacher for generation or rely on ground-truth trajectories for supervision.

## [AI Assistance Can Trap Novices and Erode Experts' Skills, Model Shows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06346) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.06346) extends the accept-or-solve model to a sequence of tasks where workers' skills evolve through independent practice and AI reliance. It shows that novices can become trapped at low skill levels and experts may lose skill even when AI-assisted accuracy remains high, while strategic workers may choose independent problem-solving to preserve future competence. This work shifts the focus from whether workers should rely on AI on individual tasks to how organizations can sustain human expertise over time, with implications for workforce design, training incentives, and AI deployment in high-stakes domains. The model compares myopic workers, who maximize immediate utility, with strategic workers, who also value future skills; it characterizes payments needed to encourage skill acquisition and retention, and shows that independent work can become self-sustaining once sufficient skill is acquired, and that supporting a higher skill target can sometimes reduce long-run incentive costs.

## [Traffic-VL: Reinforced Vision-Language Model for End-to-End Traffic Signal Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06353) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SynTra-V, a photorealistic multi-view traffic simulation dataset, and Traffic-VL, an 8-billion-parameter vision-language model that directly maps roadside images and natural-language phase descriptions to traffic signal decisions without intermediate structured representations. Traffic-VL adapts its vision encoder via offline reinforcement learning on SynTra-V and freezes it during online visual stepwise trajectory policy optimization in closed-loop simulation. This work bridges the visual-domain and perception-to-decision gaps in traffic signal control by eliminating dedicated perception pipelines and intermediate representations, enabling direct use of roadside visual observations. It matches structured-state Traffic-R1 and outperforms image-based controllers and general-purpose vision-language models including Gemini 3 Pro, with robust generalization to unseen topologies, degraded sensing, real footage, and a three-week deployment across ten intersections. Traffic-VL builds on Traffic-R1's two-stage reinforced reasoning framework and uses an 8B-parameter vision-language model; its vision encoder is adapted offline on SynTra-V and frozen during online policy optimization. SynTra-V includes automatic semantic auditing to ensure control-critical geometry, vehicle movements, and lane semantics match the simulator state.

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are AI systems that jointly interpret images and text, extending large language models to multimodal tasks. Traditional reinforcement learning for traffic signal control relies on structured traffic states from dedicated perception pipelines, while emerging visual approaches often use idealized simulator images and convert them into intermediate representations before control. SynTra-V converts physics-based traffic simulations into photorealistic multi-view surveillance imagery across diverse road topologies and sensing conditions.

## [Frontier LLM Agents Struggle in Zero-Shot Coordination, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06409) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.06409) empirically evaluates zero-shot coordination (ZSC) among independently developed LLM agents in minimal environments, finding that even frontier models fail to coordinate effectively. The authors attribute this to limited understanding of the coordination problem and weak reasoning about partners' beliefs, and propose Coordination-friendly definitions (CFDs) as a principled fix. As LLM agents are increasingly deployed in multi-agent settings, their inability to coordinate with unseen partners without shared scaffolding poses risks for AI safety and real-world deployment. The findings highlight a fundamental limitation in current LLM reasoning and suggest a path toward more robust multi-agent systems. The study uses simple, general-purpose scaffolds in minimal environments to isolate specific coordination challenges, avoiding the confounds of complex games like Hanabi. It shows that LLMs introduce semantic information as an additional coordination axis but fail to exploit it effectively, and that CFDs can be discovered automatically, removing manual engineering.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Zero-shot coordination (ZSC) refers to the challenge of enabling independently developed agents to collaborate at test time without prior joint training or shared conventions. While ZSC has been extensively studied in reinforcement learning, its application to LLM agents is less understood, and prior work often relies on specialized scaffolding that independently developed agents would not share. Hanabi, a cooperative card game where players cannot see their own cards, is a common benchmark for coordination but makes it hard to pinpoint failure sources.

## [Closed-Loop Control Brings Neurostimulation Principles to LLM Steering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06418) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper formulates LLM steering as a feedback-control problem inspired by closed-loop neurostimulation, where the model's internal state determines how it is perturbed. The authors develop an H∞-based steering controller that improves performance over existing baselines, with particularly strong gains under out-of-distribution conditions, and introduce a pre-deployment measure called robust steerability (S_rob). This cross-disciplinary approach could make LLM steering more reliable when inputs shift away from the training distribution, a key weakness of current activation-steering methods. It also offers a principled way to quantify how much uncertainty a steering intervention can tolerate before deployment, which matters for interpretability and safe model control. The controller explicitly accounts for the mismatch between tractable dynamical models and the nonlinear transformer by using robust control to limit sensitivity to disturbances and linearization errors. The robust steerability measure S_rob shows task-specific structure and varies systematically with model scale across model-task pairs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: LLM steering refers to methods that control model behavior by modifying internal activations during inference, often by adding a steering vector at specific layers. Closed-loop neurostimulation is a clinical technique where stimulation is adjusted based on ongoing neural activity rather than delivered in a fixed open-loop manner. Out-of-distribution robustness measures how well a model performs on inputs that differ from its training distribution, which is critical for real-world deployment.

## [ArcRAG: Agentic Retrieval That Adapts to Any Knowledge Graph Construction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06481) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ArcRAG introduces an agentic retrieval method that induces a query-local graph interface at inference time—capturing node labels, relation descriptors, and property keys reachable from entities named in a question—then plans, executes, and repairs multi-hop traversals as Cypher queries. The authors evaluate it on MuSiQue, HotpotQA, and TriviaQA corpora built as both schema-guided graphs and open-information-extraction graphs, showing that a construction-agnostic retriever can remain effective when the graph construction changes. Most GraphRAG systems co-design retrieval with their own graph construction pipeline, so their effectiveness is rarely tested when the underlying graph is built differently. ArcRAG addresses this cross-construction portability problem, which could make GraphRAG systems more practical and reusable across heterogeneous knowledge graph pipelines. The method is compared against a construction-agnostic Direct-Cypher agent, and each stage is ablated across retry and pass budgets on both graph families. Results show that query-time interface induction keeps a construction-agnostic retriever effective, although the underlying construction still shapes absolute accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: GraphRAG enhances retrieval-augmented generation by building a knowledge graph from a corpus and retrieving over it, but retrieval strategies are typically tailored to the structure produced by a specific construction pipeline. Cypher is a declarative query language for property graphs, commonly used with databases like Neo4j, that allows expressive multi-hop traversals. Open information extraction builds knowledge graphs without a predefined schema, using free-text relations and untyped nodes, which contrasts with schema-guided graphs that have typed nodes and a closed relation set.

## [Model Merging Does Not Require Uniform LoRA Ranks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06486) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper shows that training experts at different LoRA ranks and merging them is as good or better than uniform-rank merging, with up to 1.8 percentage points gain in multi-domain accuracy for averaging and utility-based selection methods. The study trains 36 experts across three domains and three model scales, then merges with six methods, finding that conflict-resolution methods like TIES can lose up to 4 percentage points. This finding frees practitioners from the constraint of choosing a single LoRA rank for all experts, allowing each domain to use its optimal rank and potentially improving multi-domain model performance. It also reveals that the uniform-rank requirement is an artifact of merge methods that select spectral components by magnitude, guiding better merge operator design. The key cause is that merging keeps only a fixed share of pooled spectral components, and methods that select by size discard many loss-reducing components and tend to drop the instruction adapter (smallest singular values), causing IFEval to fall by 16 to 25 percentage points. Choosing components by their effect on loss instead keeps all experts and leaves accuracy nearly flat across an 8x rank range (0.7 pp vs. 6.7 pp for TIES at 4B scale).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that freezes pre-trained weights and injects trainable low-rank matrices, with the rank controlling the number of trainable parameters. Model merging combines separately fine-tuned experts into a single model without retraining, and standard practice trains every expert at the same LoRA rank, coupling the rank choice across experts.

## [NaijaFacts benchmark reveals frontier LLMs know far less about Nigeria than the US/UK](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06504) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced NaijaFacts, a native-speaker-authored benchmark of factual and statistical questions about Nigeria, available in English, Nigerian Pidgin, Yoruba, Igbo, and Hausa. Testing eight frontier and Nigeria-adapted models, they found English-query accuracy of 49–50% for US/UK questions but only 20% for Nigeria, with the knowledge gap persisting across all languages. This work disentangles language competence from factual knowledge, showing that the dominant problem for African contexts is missing knowledge rather than poor translation. It highlights a significant fairness gap in LLMs that could affect users seeking accurate information about Nigeria and, by extension, other African countries. The language effect is smaller and tracks web representation, degrading from English to Yoruba by 9 points on Nigeria and up to 24 points on US/UK questions. Scale does not help: the strongest models reach 66–79% on UK/US but none exceed 31% on Nigerian statistics, and extending to all 54 African countries shows 96% on culture and 98% on politics versus 26% on statistics.

Low-resource languages like Yoruba, Igbo, and Hausa have limited web presence, which can affect both language understanding and factual recall. NaijaFacts uses a country-by-language matrix to separate a language effect (the model knows the fact but degrades in a low-resource language) from a knowledge effect (the fact is absent in every language).

## [EmoReward: Self-Evolving Reward Calibration for Emotional Video Generation RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06518) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EmoReward introduces a self-evolving training mechanism that adaptively calibrates multi-reward weights for emotional video generation using reinforcement learning. It distills evidence reliability from reward traces collected during training to guide weight allocation in subsequent runs, addressing limitations of fixed rewards and RLHF. This work tackles the challenge of subjective reward design and reward hacking in video generation RL, where emotional expression lacks a uniquely correct target. It could benefit researchers in generative models and RL by providing a scalable alternative to RLHF for calibrating aesthetic and emotional cues. EmoReward first plans grounding cues from the emotional prompt, then scores generated videos using heterogeneous signal primitives during the act stage. Reflective Reward Gating calibrates contributions via instance-level gates for phase, face confidence, conflict, and subtlety, while RewardMemory estimates each primitive's reliability from rollout scores and outcomes to fix bounded multipliers for the next training run without updating reward models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) is a reinforcement learning algorithm that optimizes policies by comparing generated responses within groups, reducing computational cost by eliminating a separate value model. Reward hacking occurs when an AI optimizes a reward function's literal specification without achieving the intended outcome, a common issue in RL and RLHF. RLHF trains a reward model from human preferences to align models, but scaling it for subjective tasks like emotional video generation is difficult due to individual aesthetic differences.

## [Signed MCP tools can still leak data; germline execution proposed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06585) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.06585) demonstrates that a cryptographically signed MCP tool can exfiltrate sensitive data to a policy-allowed destination, and that Meta's MCPGuard-Dynamic runtime defense failed to block this attack in every trial. The authors propose 'germline execution,' which runs each tool invocation in ephemeral forked copies from a data-free germline process, and report zero unauthorized disclosures across 153 attack runs. This exposes a fundamental trust gap in AI agent toolchains: authenticity via signatures does not guarantee benign behavior, so runtime policy enforcement alone is insufficient. The findings affect anyone deploying MCP-based agents and suggest that data-isolated execution is needed to bound information disclosure even when malicious code runs. Under germline execution, no unauthorized disclosure was detected across 153 attack runs, compared with 5.9–74.5% of runs under baseline defenses and an ablation; invocations served by a single copy completed in 10–49 ms, 2–28x faster than restarting the tool for every invocation. The security guarantee relies on explicit assumptions about copy confinement and a data-free germline, and the sink noninterference proof holds for every signed release.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an open standard that lets AI agents connect to external tools and data sources via MCP servers, which can be authenticated and restricted by runtime security policies. MCPGuard is a runtime defense and audit layer for MCP that monitors client-server communications to detect malicious activity. This paper shows that such defenses can be bypassed by signed tools that hide data exfiltration within legitimate-looking requests.

## [Hardware Noise as a Regularizer for Quantum Circuit Born Machines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06630) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.06630) proves that for any noise process, the generalization gap of a quantum circuit Born machine (QCBM) trained with maximum mean discrepancy is bounded by the complexity of the distributions the noisy model can reach. It introduces GENIE, a framework that optimally selects the noise level during training under a fixed shot budget with provably bounded regret. This work reframes hardware noise from a pure obstacle into a tunable regularization resource for quantum generative models, which could improve their practical generalization on noisy intermediate-scale quantum (NISQ) devices. It also provides a principled, budget-aware method for noise control that may benefit quantum machine learning practitioners. The analysis shows that scrambled local Pauli noise contracts distribution complexity but biases the model toward the uniform distribution, so an intermediate noise level can be optimal. GENIE realizes a chosen noise level via capped error mitigation and selects it from held-out data within a fixed execution budget; simulations on synthetic and census data show it stays close to the best of four noise-control strategies, whereas any fixed noise level falls short in many settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Quantum circuit Born machines (QCBMs) are quantum generative models that use parameterized quantum circuits to represent and sample from probability distributions via the Born rule, and they are trained by minimizing a loss such as maximum mean discrepancy (MMD). In practice, near-term quantum hardware is noisy, and noise can distort the learned distribution; error mitigation techniques aim to reduce this noise without full error correction. This paper studies how noise affects generalization from finite training data and proposes a method to actively control the noise level.

## [Trick-or-Treat: Adversarial Benchmark Exposes Hallucination in Multimodal Medical AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06631) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Trick-or-Treat, an adversarial multimodal medical benchmark that pairs real chest radiographs with synthetic clinical photographs to test cross-modal reasoning. In an evaluation of three frontier models, they found that models hallucinated clinically significant photograph findings in 45–57.5% of mismatched pairs, compared to 25–37.5% when the same photographs were shown alone. This work addresses a critical gap in medical AI evaluation by exposing a concerning error pattern: models tend to ignore mismatches and hallucinate findings that align with the radiograph. Such failures could lead to misdiagnosis in clinical settings, highlighting the need for more robust adversarial benchmarks before deploying multimodal models in healthcare. The benchmark creates matched and mismatched image pairs sharing a single chest radiograph but differing in synthetic clinical photographs, allowing clinical findings to be varied while keeping the person's features and setting consistent to reduce confounding. Models frequently misread alternative findings (e.g., acne as crusted skin lesions) to justify a unifying diagnosis like varicella pneumonia.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are increasingly applied to medical tasks that require reasoning across different image types, but existing medical datasets rarely pair modalities like radiographs and clinical photographs. Hallucination—where models generate plausible but incorrect findings—is a known challenge in MLLMs, and synthetic medical image generation offers a way to create controlled adversarial test cases.

## [PLUS: A Detector-Agnostic Subspace Signal for Near-OOD Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06688) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces PLUS (Per-class Low-dimensional sUbspace Signal), a near-OOD detection score that selects the m most class-active neurons from a sparse autoencoder, orthonormalizes their decoder directions to form a low-dimensional subspace, and scores inputs by per-class whitened squared Mahalanobis distance. When fused additively with any calibrated base score at a single fixed weight, PLUS improves near-OOD AUROC across all 126 detector-backbone combinations spanning 18 existing detectors. Near-OOD detection remains a weak point for post-hoc OOD detectors, and PLUS offers a simple, detector-agnostic complement that consistently improves performance without per-backbone or per-dataset tuning. This could make existing OOD detection pipelines more robust in high-stakes deployments such as medical imaging or autonomous driving. The method uses only in-distribution validation data for calibration and employs a single global weight, avoiding per-backbone or per-OOD-dataset adjustments. Far-OOD performance is broadly maintained or improved, with only marginal declines where the base score is already near-optimal.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Out-of-distribution (OOD) detection aims to identify inputs that differ from the training distribution. Far-OOD inputs are clearly distinct, while near-OOD inputs are semantically close and harder to separate; post-hoc detectors operate on pre-trained models without retraining. Sparse autoencoders are neural networks with a sparsity constraint that forces only a few neurons to activate, often used to disentangle interpretable features.

## [Rel-Bloom benchmark exposes relational reasoning gaps in multilingual LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06715) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Rel-Bloom, a controlled multilingual benchmark of 450 multiple-choice questions built from 30 Wikidata relations, instantiated at five Bloom-inspired reasoning levels (Remember, Understand, Apply, Analyze, Evaluate) and three veracity conditions (Factual, Counterfactual, Neutralized). Across 43 models, six languages, and 226,800 predictions, open-weight model series show sharp accuracy drops from Apply to Analyze (up to 47.2 percentage points for the Large Granite bucket), while Gemini models do not exhibit this drop. This benchmark isolates evidence-conditioned relational reasoning from memorized associations, which is critical for RAG systems that assume models can reason over supplied facts. The findings show that greater model capability does not guarantee reliable reasoning over provided relational evidence, affecting how developers evaluate and deploy multilingual LLMs in knowledge-intensive tasks. Evaluate items remain a bottleneck even for near-ceiling models: Gemini 3.1 Pro averages 99.5% on the other four levels but only 60.7% on Evaluate. Counterfactual contexts are generally harder than factual ones, and removing entity identities can improve higher-level performance in some model regimes; item- and relation-level difficulty rankings are consistently preserved across languages.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) relies on language models reasoning over relations stated in their context rather than falling back on associations memorized during pretraining. Bloom's taxonomy is a hierarchical classification of cognitive skills, revised in 2001 to include Remember, Understand, Apply, Analyze, Evaluate, and Create, which this benchmark uses to structure reasoning levels. Wikidata is a collaboratively edited knowledge base of structured relations, providing a controlled source for generating multilingual questions.

## [Full-Stack Survey Maps Dexterous Robotic Hands in the Foundation-Model Era](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06724) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv survey (2611.06724) reviews dexterous robotic hand research from January 2024 to early November 2026, organizing the literature into five interdependent layers: hardware, data collection and curation, cross-embodiment transfer, learning and foundation models, and evaluation and applications. It also highlights human hand-object understanding and tactile sensing as two cross-cutting perspectives, and identifies eleven open problems. As dexterous hands become increasingly coupled to large-scale human data and foundation models, this survey provides a system-level architecture and comparative tables that connect design choices across layers, helping researchers and practitioners in robotics and embodied AI see the full stack rather than isolated advances. It also proposes reporting interfaces and controlled comparisons to make progress more measurable. The survey distinguishes within-study experimental comparisons from heterogeneous corpus statistics, vendor specifications, and company-reported results, and concludes that the value of human data depends on kinematic, visual, and contact alignment rather than recording volume alone. It notes that shared action representations and foundation policies do not remove embodiment-specific constraints on force, latency, and contact, and that task completion alone is insufficient to establish physical correctness or deployment readiness.

## [TailSteer: Trajectory-Tail Steering for Drift-Resistant Multi-turn Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06741) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TailSteer is a final-stage training framework that mitigates visual drift in multi-turn image editing by concentrating preservation corrections near the trajectory tail, learned from on-policy denoising rollouts. Experiments across FLUX, SenseNova, and Qwen families show it substantially reduces cumulative drift over 15-20 editing turns while largely preserving base-model performance on text-to-image and single-image editing benchmarks. Multi-turn image editing is increasingly important for interactive creative workflows, but small unintended changes accumulate into severe visual drift as outputs are reused. TailSteer offers a principled, scalable training solution that unlocks long-horizon editing robustness in foundation models without relearning their existing capabilities, potentially benefiting both researchers and practitioners deploying editing models. TailSteer keeps early denoising dynamics close to the base model and applies preservation corrections only near the trajectory tail, using unchanged source content as a reference without curated ground-truth editing pairs. This avoids altering pretrained priors and is designed to scale to foundation models, unlike specialized inference-time mechanisms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Diffusion models generate images through an iterative denoising process, and in multi-turn editing, each edited output becomes the input for the next turn. Small errors can compound over turns, causing visual drift where the image gradually deviates from the intended content. Existing mitigations include training on multi-turn data or cross-turn rollouts, which may alter pretrained priors, or inference-time mechanisms that are hard to scale. On-policy rollouts refer to sampling trajectories from the model's own current policy during training.

The EvoWorld framework combines a vision-language model as the world brain with a segment-autoregressive video renderer, and the benchmark evaluates three aspects: event realization, causal reactions, and revisit consistency. The paper introduces SARBLOH, an open-weight agent that converts integer grids into object inventories and geometric briefings via a zero-parameter perception engine, arguing that spatial perception rather than planning governs sample-efficient reasoning on ARC-AGI-3. It achieved a rubric score of 25/28 (up from 3/28) while cutting token use by 89.4%, and scored 16.53 on the hidden Kaggle evaluation, 13.7x the first Milestone winner. This work challenges the prevailing focus on planning capacity in AI agents, showing that better spatial perception can dramatically improve sample efficiency and performance on interactive reasoning benchmarks. It has implications for designing more efficient agents that can quickly adapt to unfamiliar environments, relevant to AI/ML researchers working on fluid intelligence and embodied AI. The perception engine runs in 35 ms per frame and uses bipartite temporal tracking to maintain object inventories; geometric invariants generalize with 100% accuracy on held-out games, but single-frame affordance guesses fall to 6-50%, necessitating active verification probes. The agent also employs a code-as-action runtime, writing algorithmic hypotheses instead of low-level moves.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: ARC-AGI-3 is a benchmark that evaluates AI agents on their ability to quickly learn and adapt in novel, uninstructed visual games, scored by Relative Human Action Efficiency (RHAE), which penalizes exploratory actions quadratically. The benchmark aims to measure fluid intelligence—the capacity to reason and solve new problems without prior training. SARBLOH addresses the challenge that frontier models, when reading raw integer grids, lose spatial topology due to serialization and waste actions rediscovering basic properties.

## [Message-Passing Graph Persistence: A More Expressive Topological Descriptor for GNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06801) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Message-Passing Graph Persistence (MGP), a family of topological descriptors that integrate message passing into graph filtrations, updating node embeddings at each filtration step and using them to determine connected component representatives. It proves that MGP is invariant under graph isomorphism and strictly more expressive than the persistent homology-based descriptors it extends, and experiments show it outperforms other PH-based descriptors on most graph classification and regression datasets. This work addresses the limited expressivity of message-passing GNNs bounded by the 1-WL test by combining topological data analysis with representation learning, potentially leading to more powerful graph learning models. It is relevant for researchers working on GNN expressivity, graph classification, and topological methods in machine learning. MGP updates node embeddings using the current subgraph at each filtration step and associates them with persistence events, while also using embeddings to determine representatives of connected components during persistence computation. The theoretical guarantees include isomorphism invariance and strictly greater expressivity than prior PH-based descriptors, though the abstract does not specify computational complexity or scalability details.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Message-passing graph neural networks (GNNs) are limited in expressive power by the one-dimensional Weisfeiler-Leman (1-WL) test, meaning they cannot distinguish certain non-isomorphic graphs. Persistent homology (PH) is a method in topological data analysis that computes topological features of a space at different resolutions, often via filtrations (sequences of nested subgraphs). Existing PH-based approaches typically compute persistence separately from the representation learning process, and this paper integrates message passing into the filtration to enhance expressivity.

## [Toolchain Bridges Vitis HLS to AWS F2 FPGAs for ML](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06829) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper presents a complete toolchain and four-level methodology for developing ML models on AWS EC2 F2 cloud FPGAs using high-level Vitis HLS, bridging the gap from HLS to HDK to AFI. The workflow is validated with a TinyBERT model for address Named Entity Recognition, implementing the embedding layer and first encoder layer on FPGA hardware. This work fills a documented eight-year gap in academic literature by providing a reproducible Vitis HLS to AWS F2 FPGA workflow, which can lower the barrier for ML researchers and practitioners to prototype on cloud FPGAs. It also demonstrates how coding agents can implement FPGA designs from user directions, potentially accelerating hardware development. The four-level methodology (FP32 PyTorch, bit-accurate quantized Python, HLS C-simulation, physical FPGA) helps localize errors including FPGA-specific circuit errors. The implementation streams weights from host DRAM, yielding poor performance compared to CPU/GPU, and the paper discusses forward paths and timing closure strategies.

AWS EC2 F2 instances provide FPGA accelerators, but deploying designs requires navigating the HDK (Hardware Development Kit) and creating an AFI (Amazon FPGA Image). ## [Context-Answer Metamodels Forecast LLM Activations Before First Token](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06837) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces context-answer metamodels that map a context activation sequence to point or distribution forecasts of mean-pooled answer activations, predicting LLM behavior under stochastic decoding without expensive autoregressive rollouts. The four metamodel families achieve high fidelity, improve with up to 500,000 training prompts, generalize zero-shot to out-of-domain datasets, and even transfer across models (e.g., Qwen2.5 context activations predicting Qwen3.5 and Gemma 4 answer activations). This work suggests that context activation traces can support scalable forecasts of LLM answer behaviors before the first answer token is generated, potentially reducing the need for many expensive autoregressive rollouts. It could benefit interpretability research, model evaluation, and any application requiring efficient characterization of stochastic decoding variability. The metamodels forecast mean-pooled answer activations with high fidelity, and distribution metamodels also capture rollout variability; forecasts retain behavior-predictive signal and transfer across models, though the approach relies on mean-pooled activations and was evaluated on specific model families (Qwen2.5, Qwen3.5, Gemma 4).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Stochastic decoding methods like top-p (nucleus) sampling introduce randomness so that a single context can yield many different answers from a language model, and characterizing this variation typically requires many expensive autoregressive rollouts, each revealing only one realization. Mean-pooled activations are a way to summarize a sequence of token-level activations into a single vector, often used in interpretability analyses. Metamodels are lightweight models trained to predict the outputs or internal states of a larger model, here mapping context activations to forecasts of answer activations.

## [CounterFactual Retrieval Protocol Exposes Shortcut Learning in Animal Re-ID](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06864) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Counterfactual Foreground Retrieval (CFR), an evidence-aware evaluation protocol for individual animal identification that evaluates the same query-gallery task under full-image, foreground-only, and shortcut-only views. The authors also release around 0.5 million masks for 34 datasets along with code for mask generation and CFR evaluation. This work addresses a critical gap in animal re-identification evaluation: high retrieval accuracy alone does not reveal whether a model relies on animal features or identity-correlated shortcuts such as background or silhouette. By turning a retrieval score into an evidence profile, CFR can improve the trustworthiness of foundation models and shared benchmarks in ecology and conservation. CFR compares full-image retrieval performance with foreground-only retrieval (foreground-supported) and shortcut-only retrieval (identity signal recoverable from background or silhouette when the animal is removed). The released resource includes approximately 0.5M masks across 34 datasets, enabling standardized evidence-aware evaluation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Animal re-identification aims to automatically identify individual animals from images, and is increasingly moving from small curated studies to shared benchmarks and reusable foundation models. Shortcut learning occurs when models rely on easily learnable correlations (e.g., background context) instead of causal features, leading to overestimated performance and poor generalization. Counterfactual evaluation, which tests models under altered conditions to isolate causal factors, has been used in information retrieval but is novel for animal re-ID.

## [Occlusion detection without labels via disagreement between monocular depth cues](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06899) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.06899) shows that heavy occlusion can be detected without training or occlusion labels by comparing apparent height and monocular metric depth cues, achieving AUC 0.909 on KITTI and 0.819 on synthetic CARLA scenes. This label-free approach is valuable for robotics and autonomous systems deployed in settings where occlusion annotations are unavailable, and it outperforms standard architectural responses to occlusion that failed to generalize outside their training distribution. The method relies only on the ordering of apparent height and monocular metric depth, requiring no focal length, stature, or depth scale, provided the depth model's error is close to a pure gain; it holds AUC 0.894–0.925 within fixed distance bands, showing it is not a proxy for range.

AUC (area under the ROC curve) measures how well a score separates occluded from non-occluded cases, with 1.0 being perfect.

## [ToxiMol benchmark evaluates MLLMs on molecular toxicity repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10912) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces ToxiMol, the first benchmark for evaluating general-purpose Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) on molecular toxicity repair, along with ToxiEval, an automated evaluation framework. The benchmark includes 660 toxic molecules across 11 tasks, and the authors systematically assess 43 mainstream MLLMs, finding that while models still face significant challenges, they show promising capabilities in toxicity understanding and structure-aware editing. Toxicity is a leading cause of early-stage drug development failure, and this work defines and benchmarks the previously unformalized task of molecular toxicity repair, which could accelerate AI-driven drug discovery. It provides a standardized evaluation framework that may spur further research into using MLLMs for chemical structure optimization. ToxiEval integrates toxicity endpoint prediction, synthetic accessibility, drug-likeness, and structural similarity into a high-throughput evaluation chain, and the prompt annotation pipeline is informed by expert toxicological knowledge. The study includes ablation studies on evaluation metrics, candidate diversity, and failure attribution, revealing current limitations in MLLM performance.

## [LLMs Simulate Clinical Experts in Borderline Personality Disorder Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19008) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv study (2508.19008v4) evaluated GPT, Gemini, and Claude in supporting qualitative clinical analysis of life-story interviews from inpatients with Borderline Personality Disorder. Using blinded expert judges and neural embedding methods, the study found that Gemini 2.5 Pro's content validity was indistinguishable from human experts, and all models identified themes originally omitted by human researchers. This work suggests LLMs can meaningfully augment qualitative clinical research, potentially reducing human bias and offering scalable analysis in sensitive mental health domains. It also raises important questions about the reliability and interpretability of AI-generated clinical insights, which could influence future adoption in psychology and psychiatry. The study used a mixed-methods approach: Study A involved blinded and non-blinded expert judges rating AI-generated content on credibility, coherence, substantiveness, and grounding; Study B used neural embeddings to compare semantic and stylistic differences; Study C included 115 non-experts to assess the influence of thematic verbosity. AI-human overlap varied widely (0-58%), and external evaluators could not reliably distinguish human-authored themes from AI-generated ones.

## [UpSafe°C: Upcycling Safety Layers into Sparse MoE for Controllable LLM Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.02194) ⭐️ 7.0/10

UpSafe°C introduces a unified framework that identifies safety-critical layers in LLMs and upcycles them into a sparse Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) structure, where the router acts as a soft guardrail that selectively activates original MLPs and added safety experts. It also employs a two-stage supervised fine-tuning (SFT) strategy and a safety temperature mechanism for inference-time control of the safety-utility trade-off. This work offers a new direction for LLM safety by moving from static alignment to dynamic, modular, and inference-aware control, potentially providing robust defense against harmful content and jailbreak attacks while maintaining general capabilities. It could benefit researchers and practitioners seeking flexible safety mechanisms that can be adjusted at deployment time. The approach leverages the existence of safety-critical layers—a small set of contiguous middle layers crucial for distinguishing malicious from normal queries—and upcycles them into a sparse MoE with added safety experts. The safety temperature parameter allows fine-grained adjustment of the safety-utility trade-off at inference time, and experiments across multiple benchmarks, base models, and model scales demonstrate robust safety improvements with competitive general performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Large language models remain vulnerable to jailbreak attacks and harmful content generation despite alignment efforts. Existing safety techniques include external guardrails, inference-time guidance, and post-training alignment, but each faces limitations in balancing safety, utility, and controllability. ## [MinPRO: Prefix Importance Ratio Stabilizes Off-policy LLM Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22718) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper identifies that the exact correction for the prefix-marginal policy gradient in off-policy LLM optimization is the cumulative prefix importance ratio, not the token-level ratio used in most existing methods. It proposes Minimum Prefix Ratio (MinPRO), which replaces the unstable cumulative prefix ratio with a biased, non-cumulative surrogate based on the minimum token-level ratio in the preceding prefix, and shows it substantially improves training stability and peak performance on mathematical reasoning benchmarks for both dense and mixture-of-experts LLMs. This work addresses a real stability issue in RL post-training of LLMs, where off-policy rollouts from older policies are used for efficiency but can cause unstable training under large policy lag. The proposed MinPRO objective offers a simple yet effective fix that could improve the reliability and performance of RLHF and RL-based post-training pipelines. MinPRO introduces a biased but non-cumulative surrogate that uses the minimum token-level importance ratio observed in the preceding prefix, avoiding the high variance of the cumulative product. Experiments are conducted on both dense and mixture-of-experts LLMs across multiple mathematical reasoning benchmarks, demonstrating substantial gains in stability and peak performance under off-policy regimes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning post-training is widely used to elicit reasoning in large language models, but generating rollouts on-policy is expensive. Off-policy methods reuse rollouts from older policies, creating a mismatch between the sampling and target policies that must be corrected via importance sampling. Traditional importance sampling uses token-level ratios, but in sequence generation the correct correction for the prefix-marginal gradient involves cumulative prefix ratios, which can be unstable when the policy lag is large.

## [IPG: Training-free attribution traces LLM reasoning mechanisms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02313) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Integrated Policy Gradient (IPG), a training-free framework that backpropagates outcome-level, non-differentiable behavioral signals (e.g., reasoning correctness) through entire inference trajectories using path integration to isolate outcome-relevant mediators at the component level. It provides a linear approximation of Causal Mediation Analysis for reasoning and empirically identifies sparse, concentrated subsets of internal components associated with specific reasoning behaviors. This work addresses a key challenge in mechanistic interpretability: tracing long-horizon, outcome-driven influence of internal components in multi-step LLM reasoning, which existing methods struggle with. By enabling precise localization and modulation of reasoning capabilities, IPG offers a powerful tool for understanding and potentially improving LLM reasoning reliability. IPG is training-free and grounded in outcome-oriented and sequential-influence-aware principles, using path integration to attribute non-differentiable signals like correctness. It yields a linear approximation of Causal Mediation Analysis and identifies sparse component subsets, though the approximation may have limitations in capturing nonlinear interactions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer the internal computations of LLMs, often by identifying circuits or features responsible for specific behaviors. Policy gradient methods are reinforcement learning techniques that optimize a policy directly via gradient ascent on expected reward. Path integration attribution methods, such as Integrated Gradients, attribute a model's output to input features by integrating gradients along a path from a baseline to the input, and have been extended to local path integration for better performance.

## [E-SPL jointly evolves LLM weights and system prompts via RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14697) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Evolutionary System Prompt Learning (E-SPL), a method that in each reinforcement learning iteration samples trajectories under multiple system prompts in parallel, then jointly updates model weights via policy gradients and evolves the system prompts through self-reflection. On an easy-to-hard generalization setting (AIME to BeyondAIME), E-SPL improves RL success rate from 38.8% to 45.1%, and also boosts performance on AIME 2025 (56.3% to 60.6%), HMMT 2025 (50.0% to 52.7%), and agentic search (44.2% to 48.6% on gpt-oss-120b). This work demonstrates that weight updates and context updates are deeply synergistic, yielding generalization gains that neither reinforcement learning alone nor prompt evolution alone achieves. It offers a practical path toward self-improving LLM agents that can autonomously refine both their procedural knowledge and interpretable declarative knowledge. E-SPL encourages a natural division of labor: procedural knowledge is encoded in the weights, while interpretable declarative knowledge is captured in the system prompts. Across all evaluated settings, E-SPL outperforms both RL-only and evolution-only baselines, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Large language models today primarily self-improve through two mechanisms: self-reflection for context updates (e.g., refining prompts) and reinforcement learning for weight updates. System prompts are initial instructions that set an LLM's role, rules, and output style, while benchmarks like AIME and BeyondAIME test mathematical reasoning at olympiad-level difficulty. E-SPL combines these two self-improvement pathways by evolving system prompts alongside RL weight updates.

## [Certified Circuits: Stability Guarantees for Mechanistic Circuits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22968) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Certified Circuits, a framework that wraps any black-box circuit discovery algorithm with randomized data subsampling to certify that inclusion decisions over circuit components are invariant to bounded edit-distance perturbations of the concept dataset. Unstable components are abstained from, yielding circuits that are more compact and more accurate across ResNet, ViT, and GPT-2 on vision and language tasks. This addresses a known brittleness problem in mechanistic interpretability, where discovered circuits often fail to transfer out-of-distribution, by providing provable stability guarantees. It could increase trust in circuit-based explanations for debugging, auditing, and deploying neural networks, impacting researchers in interpretability and trustworthy ML. Certified circuits achieve up to 56% higher accuracy and up to 80% fewer components than baselines, and remain reliable where baselines degrade. The framework is validated on ImageNet and four OOD datasets for vision, and on IOI, IOI-Hard, and Greater-Than tasks for language, with code available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer neural networks by identifying circuits—minimal subnetworks responsible for specific behaviors. Circuit discovery algorithms, such as those based on activation patching, automatically find these components but are known to be brittle: the discovered circuits depend heavily on the chosen concept dataset and often fail to generalize. Certified Circuits introduces a certification step using randomized subsampling to ensure that only components stable under dataset perturbations are included.

<a id="item-183"></a>
## [AS2: Fully Differentiable Neuro-Soft-Symbolic Reasoning Without External Solvers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18436) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AS2 introduces a fully differentiable neuro-symbolic architecture that replaces discrete Answer Set Programming (ASP) solving with a continuous approximation of the immediate-consequence operator, maintaining probability distributions over symbols and learning by minimizing the fixed-point residual. It achieves 99.89% cell accuracy and 100% constraint satisfaction on Visual Sudoku, and 99.94% on CLEVR-Hans3 and 87.77% on CLEVR-Hans7. This work enables end-to-end gradient-based training of neuro-symbolic systems without relying on external solvers during training or inference, potentially simplifying deployment and improving integration with deep learning pipelines. It shows that differentiable fixed-point reasoning can match or outperform solver-based approaches, which could impact constraint satisfaction, visual reasoning, and other structured tasks. AS2 replaces positional embeddings with constraint-group membership embeddings derived from declarative ASP specifications, making the model independent of arbitrary position indexing. The approach is evaluated on Visual Sudoku and CLEVR-Hans, but the paper does not discuss scalability to large-scale problems or potential limitations of the continuous approximation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Answer Set Programming (ASP) is a declarative programming paradigm for hard search problems, based on stable model semantics; its immediate-consequence operator computes the least model of a positive program. Neuro-symbolic AI combines neural perception with symbolic reasoning, but typically uses discrete solvers that block end-to-end gradient-based optimization. Visual Sudoku is a benchmark where MNIST digits are placed in Sudoku grids, requiring both perception and constraint satisfaction.

## [First Survey Maps Reinforcement Learning for GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.27955) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2604.27955v2) presents the first comprehensive survey of reinforcement learning (RL) methods for graphical user interface (GUI) agents, proposing a taxonomy of Offline RL, Online RL, and Hybrid Strategies. It also analyzes reward engineering, data efficiency, and emerging trends such as world-model-based training and System-2-style deliberation. As GUI agents move from supervised fine-tuning toward RL to handle long-horizon tasks and distribution shifts, this survey provides a structured entry point for researchers and practitioners. It could help shape the next generation of robust GUI automation and agent-native infrastructure. The survey highlights a tension between reliability and scalability driving composite multi-tier reward architectures, notes that GUI I/O latency bottlenecks are accelerating world-model-based training, and suggests explicit reasoning supervision may be unnecessary when reward signals are rich enough. It distills a roadmap covering process rewards, continual RL, cognitive architectures, and safe deployment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: GUI agents are AI systems that perceive and interact with graphical interfaces visually, much like a human clicking and typing. Supervised fine-tuning alone struggles with long-horizon credit assignment (figuring out which earlier action led to a later outcome), distribution shifts, and safe exploration in irreversible environments, which is why reinforcement learning has become central. Offline RL learns from fixed datasets without online interaction, online RL learns by interacting with the environment, and hybrid strategies combine both.

## [Alice Learns Executable World Models Online via Preservation Conflicts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16725) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Alice, a closed-loop system that learns executable world models online by treating failed candidate updates as structural signals, and evaluated it on Baba in Wonderland, a prior-misaligned variant of Baba Is You where rule-property labels are replaced with unrelated words. Experiments show that Alice substantially improves executable world-model learning under prior misalignment, with five-run ablations supporting the benefit of class structure and exploration signals. This work addresses a key challenge in AI planning: learning executable world models that capture true transition laws rather than superficial patterns, even when prior knowledge is misleading. The approach could enable more robust agents that adapt to novel environments without reward signals or trustworthy lexical priors, impacting areas like program induction and autonomous exploration. Alice refines preservation conflicts into hypothesis classes, providing one broken example per affected class for update and guiding exploration toward novel and underrepresented transitions. The evaluation uses a variant of Baba Is You that preserves simulator dynamics but replaces semantically meaningful labels, isolating the challenge of prior misalignment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Executable world models are programs that represent an environment's transition rules and can be executed, edited, and reused for planning. Unlike latent neural world models, they offer interpretability and verifiability. Baba Is You is a puzzle game where rules are represented as text blocks that can be rearranged, making it a testbed for dynamics discovery. Prior misalignment occurs when an agent's initial knowledge (e.g., word meanings) does not match the environment, requiring learning from interaction alone.

## [AutoMMemo Automatically Designs Memory for Multimodal Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16857) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AutoMMemo is a framework that represents each memory mechanism as an executable memo program and automatically discovers effective programs through iterative update-then-retrieve evaluation, reflection-guided mutation, and budget-aware tree search. Experiments on GUI/Web navigation and multimodal visual reasoning benchmarks show consistent improvements over no-memory and fixed-memory baselines. This work addresses a real gap in experience-driven learning for multimodal agents, moving beyond text-only settings and manually designed memory schemas. It highlights adaptive memory design as a key optimization target, potentially enabling agents to learn reusable experience from rich perception-reasoning-action trajectories. The approach maintains reasonable token and interaction costs, and transfer and search-process analyses demonstrate that learned memory designs capture reusable experience structures and progressively refine retrieval relevance. Code is available on GitHub, though the work is an arXiv preprint without community discussion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Multimodal agents interact with environments and generate trajectories that include perception, reasoning, and action. Experience-driven learning aims to turn these raw trajectories into reusable knowledge, but existing methods are mostly text-based and rely on manually designed memory schemas, which struggle with heterogeneous multimodal signals across modalities, temporal scales, and abstraction levels.

## [EvoOptiGraph: Weakness-Driven Coevolution for Optimization Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26578) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EvoOptiGraph introduces a framework where data and models co-evolve by representing mixed-integer linear programs (MILPs) as attributed bipartite graphs and applying validity-preserving evolutionary operators to generate structurally diverse instances. Training uses supervised fine-tuning followed by reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR), where graph-derived weakness signals guide the generation of new instances targeting model failures. This approach addresses the lack of structural diversity in training corpora and the static nature of data generation pipelines, which are key bottlenecks for automating optimization modeling with LLMs. It could significantly improve LLMs' accuracy, executability, and generalization on optimization tasks, benefiting practitioners who rely on MILP solvers. The evolved graphs are converted into solver code and natural language via deterministic compilation and verified back-translation, forming a closed loop that continuously updates the training distribution. Empirical results on six public datasets show that EvoOptiGraph significantly outperforms larger generalist models, agentic methods, and specialized baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Mixed-integer linear programming (MILP) is a widely used optimization technique where some variables are constrained to be integers, with applications in scheduling, logistics, and production planning. Automating the translation of natural language problem descriptions into MILP formulations using large language models (LLMs) is challenging due to the need for structural diversity in training data and the static nature of existing data generation methods. Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) is a training paradigm that uses objective, programmatically verifiable rewards (e.g., unit tests) to improve model reasoning and reliability.

## [KV-PRM Cuts Process Reward Model Scoring Cost from Quadratic to Linear via KV-Cache Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09153) ⭐️ 7.0/10

KV-PRM introduces a process reward model that reads the KV cache generated during LLM inference instead of re-encoding the full trajectory text, reducing scoring cost from O(L^2) to O(L). The authors formally prove that the KV cache has strictly greater information capacity than text, and empirically show up to 5,000x fewer scoring FLOPs, 37x lower latency, and 34x smaller per-sequence memory footprint compared to text-based PRMs on MATH, GSM8K, and AIME benchmarks. This work removes a major computational bottleneck in process reward models, making them practical for long-context multi-agent test-time scaling where scoring cost previously grew quadratically with sequence length. It could significantly improve the efficiency of LLM-based multi-agent systems that rely on PRMs for guiding search and voting methods. KV-PRM processes a single "verify token" against the pre-existing KV cache, and matches or strictly outperforms text-PRMs under Beam Search, MCTS, and Weighted Voting. The approach relies on access to the KV cache produced during generation, which may introduce engineering constraints in distributed or batched inference settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Process Reward Models (PRMs) evaluate intermediate reasoning steps in LLM outputs and are widely used to guide test-time scaling methods like beam search and MCTS. KV caching is a standard transformer inference optimization that stores key and value tensors from previous tokens to avoid recomputation. Existing PRMs are text-based and re-encode the entire trajectory from scratch, which becomes prohibitively expensive for long multi-agent rollouts.
