## [MvBody: Multi-View Transformer Predicts Cesarean Risk from 3D Body Scans](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.03212) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose MvBody, a multi-view Transformer network that predicts cesarean section risk using only self-reported medical data and 3D optical body scans taken between the 31st and 38th weeks of gestation, achieving 84.62% accuracy and an AUC-ROC of 0.724 on an independent test set. This pilot study demonstrates the feasibility of using affordable 3D body scanning for cesarean risk assessment in resource-limited or home-based settings, potentially enabling earlier prenatal care decisions and improving maternal and neonatal outcomes where in-hospital predictive models are unavailable. The model incorporates a metric learning loss to enhance training efficiency and generalizability in data-scarce environments, and uses Integrated Gradients to provide explanations, identifying pre-pregnancy weight, maternal age, obstetric history, previous CS history, and body shape (especially head and shoulders) as key contributors.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Cesarean section risk is typically assessed in hospitals during labor using medical parameters that may not be available in low-resource settings. 3D optical body scanning is a non-invasive technology that captures body dimensions and shapes, and multi-view Transformers are neural networks designed to integrate information from multiple views or data sources. Explainable AI techniques like Integrated Gradients help make model predictions transparent by attributing importance to input features.

## [Study Finds SAM 3D Body Smooths Out Anthropometric Deviations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.06035) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2601.06035) evaluates SAM 3D Body and finds it consistently fails to reconstruct detailed anthropometric deviations such as geriatric muscle atrophy, scoliosis, and pregnancy, even when these features are prominent in the input image. The authors attribute this not to model capacity but to a 'perception-distortion trade-off' arising from the low-dimensional MHR parametric representation, semantic-invariant DINOv3 conditioning, and annotation-based alignment, which together cause a 'regression to the mean' effect. This work pinpoints a systematic limitation in a state-of-the-art human mesh recovery model, showing that high perceptual quality and topological coherence can come at the cost of anatomical precision. It matters for researchers and practitioners who might otherwise assume SAM 3D Body is suitable for medical or clinical body-shape analysis, and it proposes concrete directions such as hybrid representations and Medical-in-the-Loop alignment. The analysis links the smoothing effect to three architectural factors: the low-dimensional MHR identity parameterization (45 shape parameters), semantic-invariant conditioning via DINOv3, and annotation-based alignment, all of which bias outputs toward typical bodies. The paper is a focused technical critique rather than a new model, and it offers constructive pathways—implicit-explicit hybrid representations and Medical-in-the-Loop alignment—to extend SAM 3D Body into high-precision medical domains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: SAM 3D Body (3DB) is Meta Superintelligence Labs' promptable model for single-image full-body 3D human mesh recovery, built on the Momentum Human Rig (MHR) parametric body model, which uses 45 shape parameters to control identity and 204 parameters for full-body articulation. Human mesh recovery aims to reconstruct a 3D mesh of a person from a single image, and parametric models like MHR constrain outputs to a low-dimensional space of plausible bodies. The perception-distortion trade-off, formalized by Blau and Michaeli in 2018, states that perceptual quality and distortion are fundamentally at odds in image restoration, and this paper applies that lens to body reconstruction.

## [PEEK: Training-Free Sampler Plugin Corrects Exposure Bias in Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05407) ⭐️ 6.0/10

PEEK introduces a training-free deterministic sampler plugin that performs a provisional one-step update, evaluates the denoiser at the look-ahead state, and interpolates it with the one-step-later prediction to form the next noisy state. Across 8 pixel-space and latent-space diffusion models with unconditional, label-guided, and text-guided settings, PEEK consistently improves FID over NFE-matched baselines by up to 49.3% with minimal extra computation. Exposure bias is a fundamental train-test mismatch that caps diffusion image quality, and PEEK addresses it without retraining or architectural changes, making it broadly applicable to existing samplers. Its minimal overhead and consistent gains across diverse models and settings suggest it could become a practical drop-in enhancement for diffusion-based image generation pipelines. PEEK uses an interpolation schedule search made efficient by prefix and baseline noise-schedule-rebasing caches, and the one-time-per-model calibration takes at most 2.2 minutes across experiments. The method is deterministic and training-free, but requires a calibration step per model and is evaluated primarily on FID as the quality metric.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Diffusion models generate images by iteratively denoising random noise, and the denoiser network is trained on real data but during sampling it receives inputs that differ from training data, creating exposure bias. This mismatch often leads to blurry, detail-lacking, or awkwardly shaped outputs. Deterministic samplers like DDIM are popular for fast generation but can exacerbate such errors, and prior work has tried to mitigate exposure bias through shifted time steps or spectral priors.

## [CAST-Bench: A New Benchmark for Sound-Source Tracing in Multi-Shot Audio-Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05422) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced CAST-Bench, a benchmark of 900 prompts (439 single-shot and 461 multi-shot) organized into 9 sound-source categories, designed to trace each generated sound back to its intended source in text-to-audio-video (T2AV) generation. The benchmark formalizes correctness as an 'audiovisual contract' and provides an 18-metric evaluation suite across Visual Performance, Content Consistency, and Audio & Semantic Alignment, benchmarking 12 representative generators. Existing T2AV benchmarks mainly evaluate perceptual quality and audiovisual alignment, but lack balanced sound-source sampling and separate reporting by shot structure, making it hard to diagnose whether a sound is missing, misattributed, or lost across shots. CAST-Bench fills this gap, offering a more granular evaluation tool that could guide improvements in multi-shot audio-video generation, especially for challenges like multi-person speech and cross-shot event retention. The benchmark reveals that while visual quality is nearly saturated across leading systems, audio semantic scores still differ markedly, and multi-shot prompts receive lower aggregate scores under subset-specific scoring rules, yielding different model rankings. Multi-person speech, consistent speaker identity across shots, and cross-shot event retention remain the main unresolved challenges.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Text-to-audio-video (T2AV) generation is an emerging multimodal task where models generate both video and synchronized audio from text prompts. Recent systems like Google Veo 3 and MiniMax H3 support native audio co-generation, and multi-shot video generation tools (e.g., Wan 3, Seedance 2.0) enable creating sequences of connected shots. Benchmarks are essential for evaluating these models, but existing ones like AMusE focus on agentic tasks, leaving a gap in fine-grained sound-source attribution across shots.

## [Laplacian-Constrained Stiefel Flow Matching for Spectral Graph Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05436) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Laplacian-Constrained Stiefel Flow Matching (LaSFM), a flow matching method on a constrained Stiefel manifold that preserves the Laplacian nullspace, eigenvector orthonormality, and positive eigenvalues during spectral graph generation. It further extends LaSFM to property-guided generation, steering samples toward desired graph properties while maintaining these constraints. This work addresses a real gap in spectral graph generation by enforcing the Laplacian nullspace constraint, which existing methods overlook, potentially leading to more structurally consistent generated graphs. It could benefit researchers working on generative models for graphs, especially those focusing on spectral methods and manifold learning. LaSFM characterizes the underlying constrained Stiefel manifold and formulates flow matching on it, preserving the nullspace condition alongside eigenvector orthonormality and positive eigenvalues. Experiments show improved performance on spectral and graph-level metrics, as well as better alignment with target properties in property-guided generation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Spectral graph generation models graphs through the eigenvalues and eigenvectors of their Laplacians, providing a compact and continuous alternative to direct graph construction. The Stiefel manifold is the set of orthonormal k-frames in R^n, and flow matching is a generative modeling framework that learns to transform noise into data by modeling continuous flows. Graph Laplacians are symmetric and singular, with row sums zero, implying a nullspace constraint that must be preserved for valid eigensystems.

## [SurgTransfer learns transferable latent actions from unlabeled surgical videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05443) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes SurgTransfer, a multi-step latent action learning framework that learns context-invariant actions from unlabeled surgical videos to enable action transfer in surgical world models without relying on ground-truth kinematics or expert annotations. It is evaluated on the public SurgToolLoc-2022 and PitVis-2023 datasets with quantitative metrics and qualitative review by surgical experts, and the code is released on GitHub. This addresses a genuine bottleneck in surgical AI: most world-model approaches require ground-truth kinematics or task-specific expert annotations, which are scarce and expensive. By learning transferable latent actions from unlabeled video, the work could broaden intra-operative guidance, risk-free training, and transfer of expert surgical maneuvers to a much wider range of procedures. The authors argue that directly applying natural-domain latent action learning to surgery is short-sighted because it fails to capture the hierarchical, goal-directed structure of surgical procedures, motivating a multi-step formulation. Evaluation uses SurgToolLoc-2022 and PitVis-2023, combining quantitative metrics with qualitative expert assessment, though the contribution remains specialized to surgical AI research.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Latent action models (LAMs) are self-supervised methods that infer a compact, low-dimensional action representation from observation-only video, typically by compressing frame-to-frame changes into latents, and then reuse that representation as a control interface for world models, robot policies, or cross-embodiment transfer. Surgical world models aim to simulate and predict surgical scenes to provide intra-operative guidance and training, but prior work such as the Surgical Vision World Model has relied on labeled kinematics or task-specific annotations. SurgTransfer extends the LAM paradigm to surgery by learning multi-step, context-invariant latent actions from unlabeled surgical video.

## [Modular Framework Benchmarks 15 Multimodal Fusion Methods for IBD Remission Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05579) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper presents a modular framework that systematically compares 15 multimodal fusion methods combining whole-slide image embeddings and per-visit clinical data to predict STRIDE-II-aligned remission outcomes in a prospective cohort of newly diagnosed inflammatory bowel disease patients. The best fusion method achieved macro-F1 of 0.57–0.75 and outperformed both single-modality models in four of six scenarios. This work addresses a key gap in computational pathology by evaluating fusion methods under identical conditions across different clinical endpoints, providing a fair benchmark that could guide method selection for multimodal medical AI. The finding that methods with similar accuracy rely on the slide to very different degrees has implications for model interpretability and trust in clinical deployment. Slide-level embeddings were obtained by training an attention-based aggregator over patch embeddings from a frozen foundation model, and all fusion methods shared a common output dimension and prediction head except decision-level methods. Image reliance, measured as the drop in balanced accuracy when the slide embedding is removed, was highest for histological remission and lowest for endoscopic remission, showing that fusion performance depends on outcome type and input modality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Whole-slide images are gigapixel-resolution digitized pathology slides that are central to computational pathology, but analyzing them requires aggregating thousands of patch-level features into a slide-level representation. Multimodal fusion combines such image representations with other data sources, such as clinical records, and can occur at different levels (e.g., feature-level or decision-level). STRIDE-II is an international consensus that defines treatment targets for inflammatory bowel disease, including clinical remission, endoscopic healing, and histological remission, which serve as the endpoints predicted in this study.

## [VideoMogaNet: Efficient Multi-Order Spatio-Temporal Interaction for Video Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05633) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces VideoMogaNet, which replaces quadratic-complexity cross-channel aggregation in temporal modeling with parallel multi-order processing using depthwise-only convolutions and implicit gating, achieving linear complexity in the number of channels. It also proposes a dual spatial-temporal interaction that improves representation without increasing computational cost. This work reduces the computational overhead of the temporal branch in video recognition models, making efficient video understanding more feasible. It achieves performance on par with or better than state-of-the-art action recognition methods on benchmarks like UCF101, HMDB51, Kinetics-400, and Diving48. The method builds on MogaNet's multi-order spatial representation and uses depthwise-only convolutions with implicit gating for temporal aggregation, which is linear in channels. The dual spatial-temporal interaction is designed to balance local and global order interactions without extra cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Video recognition models often use 3D CNNs or transformers, which have high computational costs due to quadratic complexity in channel aggregation or attention. MogaNet is an efficient ConvNet architecture that uses multi-order gated aggregation to capture informative spatial context with favorable complexity-performance trade-offs. Depthwise convolutions are a technique that reduces parameters and computation by applying filters per channel.

## [TACT: AI Council for Scalable Qualitative Thematic Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05702) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers present TACT (Traceable AI Council for Thematic analysis), a multi-model procedure for qualitative coding that keeps teams in control of interpretation. Evaluated on 256 Stack Overflow threads, TACT achieved F1 scores of 0.71–0.77 against existing labels and Cohen's κ of 0.79–0.85 against manual coding, at a cost of $13.77–$33.59 per 100 threads. Software teams often face a false choice between leaving thousands of qualitative responses unanalyzed or fully delegating interpretation to a single LLM. TACT offers a traceable, economical middle ground that could make rigorous qualitative analysis practical for resource-constrained teams while preserving human judgment. TACT comes in two variants: TACT-Lite, the core AI council procedure, and TACT-Full, which adds adversary models from other model families to critique outputs and a decision model to flag high-uncertainty code assignments. The paper also identifies pitfalls in AI-assisted qualitative analysis and offers safeguards and practical guidance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Thematic analysis is a widely used qualitative research method for identifying and interpreting patterns of meaning (themes) in data such as interviews or forum posts. An LLM council is a multi-agent workflow where several models answer independently, critique each other's responses, and a judge or chairman synthesizes a final answer. TACT applies this council idea to qualitative coding, the process of assigning descriptive labels (codes) to text segments.

## [Text-Only Domain Adaptation for Hausa Medical ASR via Shallow Fusion and Density Ratio Rescoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05744) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper investigates text-only domain adaptation for Hausa medical ASR using a fixed MMS-1B-FL102 acoustic model and two decoding-time language model fusion methods: Shallow Fusion (SF) and the Density Ratio Approach (DRA). Under prompt-disjoint five-fold cross-validation, SF and DRA reduce word error rate (WER) from 29.56% to 23.55% and 23.33%, respectively, both significantly outperforming greedy decoding (p < 0.001). This work demonstrates that lightweight, text-only language model fusion can meaningfully improve medical ASR for a low-resource language like Hausa without collecting additional domain-specific speech data, which is often expensive and difficult to obtain. It offers a practical adaptation strategy for similar low-resource medical speech applications. A 4-gram KenLM trained on 19,421 Hausa health sentences served as the medical target model, while a 75,372-sentence general-domain model provided the source prior for DRA. However, general-LM-only SF achieved 23.96% WER, indicating that the medical LM's additional improvement over a general LM is modest, and the difference between SF and DRA was not statistically significant (p = 0.423).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Automatic speech recognition (ASR) systems often struggle with specialized medical vocabulary, especially in low-resource languages where domain-specific speech data are scarce. Shallow fusion is a simple and widely used technique that combines an external language model with an ASR system during decoding, while the density ratio approach adjusts the language model scores by subtracting a general-domain prior to avoid over-penalizing common words. The MMS-1B-FL102 is a massively multilingual speech model from Meta's Massively Multilingual Speech project, used here as a fixed acoustic model for Hausa.

## [BobFlow: Dataflow Abstraction for Human-Agent Collaborative Data Science](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05761) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduce BobFlow, a system that combines dataflow abstraction with the ReAct paradigm to let LLM agents iteratively construct and execute dataflows for data science tasks while human analysts inspect and provide feedback. A user study shows BobFlow helps analysts understand, inspect, and verify agent-generated analyses, and experiments across proprietary and open-weight LLMs report accuracy gains of up to 18.6 points at just over one-third of a script-based ReAct agent's inference cost. Effective human-agent collaboration in data science requires a shared computational abstraction that both agents and analysts can reason about, and BobFlow's dataflow approach offers a more interpretable alternative to Python or SQL scripts. If validated, this could lower the cost and improve the reliability of LLM-driven data analysis workflows, benefiting analysts who need to trust and refine agent outputs. The system leverages the ReAct paradigm, where agents alternate between reasoning and acting, to iteratively build dataflows, and the evaluation spans both proprietary and open-weight LLMs. The reported 18.6-point accuracy gain comes at just over one-third of the inference cost of a script-based ReAct agent, though the work is an arXiv preprint with no peer review or community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Dataflows are a computational abstraction that represents processing logic as a graph of operations, making it easier to visualize and modify than code in Python or SQL. The ReAct paradigm is a popular approach for LLM agents that interleaves reasoning traces with actions, allowing the agent to plan and execute steps iteratively. BobFlow aims to combine these ideas so that agents can construct dataflows while humans can inspect and guide the analysis, addressing the challenge that script-based outputs are often hard for analysts to understand and modify.

## [DARE: Predicting Repair Task Difficulty for LLM Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05797) ⭐️ 6.0/10

DARE introduces a framework that defines continuous repair-task difficulty as the observed failure rate across multiple coding agents, predicts it using 151 static features extracted from benchmark artifacts, and analyzes the contributions of seven Repair-Task Feature Groups to select task-specific agent support. Evaluated on four benchmark partitions totaling 2,594 tasks, the difficulty model achieves Spearman's ρ = .476–.621 on held-out partitions, and coding-agent experiments show improved repair success when support is matched to each task's salient Feature Group. This work moves beyond simple solve rates by enabling difficulty-stratified evaluation, cross-benchmark comparisons, and targeted support for LLM-based coding agents, which could lead to more reliable automated program repair and better identification of capability gaps. It affects researchers and practitioners working on automated program repair and LLM evaluation. The difficulty model uses 151 static features from benchmark artifacts and achieves moderate predictive correlation (Spearman's ρ = .476–.621), with the most salient Feature Groups varying across tasks and benchmarks. The approach relies on observed failure rates across multiple agents to define difficulty, which may be costly to obtain for new benchmarks.

The paper introduces GREP, a three-tier evaluation philosophy for LLM-generated furnished rooms that checks physical validity, visual coherence, and human-use logic, using an autonomous agent to identify issues and produce scores. Experiments show GREP achieves 87% agreement with human majority preferences, outperforming baseline evaluators. This work addresses the lack of comprehensive evaluation methods for LLM-based 3D scene generation, providing a principled framework that could standardize quality assessment and guide future model improvements. It matters for researchers and practitioners developing and benchmarking room layout generation systems. GREP's agent autonomously gathers evidence from scene context to produce scores and reports, and these reports improve scene quality through refinement, whereas score-only and no-feedback baselines show slight declines. The paper also notes that stronger models like GPT-5.6 Sol can challenge pipeline guidance but still lack the ability to resolve resulting conflicts.

## [MoE Expert Deactivation Must Match Training-Time Weight Rule](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05804) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper compares four inference-time expert deactivation strategies—removal, freezing, remaining-expert renormalization, and unnormalized re-routing—across five instruction-tuned checkpoints from four model families, plus a base OLMoE checkpoint. It introduces a weight-sum model showing that performance depends on whether the deactivation rule matches the weight rule used during training. This work provides a principled explanation for why ad-hoc expert pruning can catastrophically degrade MoE model quality, guiding practitioners to choose deactivation methods that align with training-time routing. It could lead to more reliable and efficient inference-time compression for large MoE language models. With half the experts deactivated, renormalization yields the highest perplexity in models trained with raw router probabilities, while unnormalized re-routing is worst for models trained with normalized weights; perplexity ranges from 1.4× to 73× baseline when the training rule is retained, but 14× to 28,740× under mismatch. On OLMoE, GSM8K strict-match accuracy falls to zero under all four implementations, and the model predicts that the perplexity penalty from extra renormalization grows as fewer experts are active.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models route each token through a small subset of specialized feed-forward networks called experts, enabling large parameter counts with sparse computation. At inference, deactivating some experts can reduce memory and compute, but it changes both token routing and expert weights. The paper studies how different deactivation implementations interact with the weight normalization scheme used during training, such as raw router probabilities versus normalized weights.

## [RACE: Rollout-Free Checkpoint Selection for Robot Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05838) ⭐️ 6.0/10

RACE introduces a rollout-free metric that calibrates action deviations against state-conditional thresholds derived from held-out successful trajectories, then aggregates scores via a running product to rank robot policy checkpoints during training. The method outperforms baselines in simulation and aligns with real-robot deployment success across different policy backbones and task difficulties. Selecting the best checkpoint during robot policy training typically requires costly real-world rollouts, so a reliable rollout-free metric could significantly reduce evaluation cost and accelerate development cycles for robotics and reinforcement learning practitioners. RACE uses a fixed set of held-out successful trajectories to derive state-conditional thresholds, and its running-product aggregation downweights later reference predictions after a large deviation, addressing two limitations of averaged validation loss. Perturbation analyses show it distinguishes deviations with markedly different effects on task success, though the approach assumes access to successful reference trajectories.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: In robot policy training, checkpoints are saved periodically, and choosing which one to deploy is critical. Directly measuring task success requires real-world trials for each candidate, which is expensive and slow. Averaged validation loss is a rollout-free alternative but its rankings can be inconsistent with actual deployment performance. RACE is a new metric that aims to better correlate with deployment success without running the policy.

## [Autonomous ETL Framework for Salesforce M&A Data Consolidation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05918) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes a reference framework for autonomous ETL pipelines that consolidate Salesforce customer data during mergers and acquisitions, mapping each stage to Jitterbit Integration Studio constructs. The framework was implemented as an open prototype and evaluated on synthetic two-organization scenarios ranging from 32,040 to 383,973 source records, achieving pairwise F1 scores of 0.918 for accounts and 0.885 for contacts at a preset threshold. Merging companies that both run Salesforce face complex data consolidation challenges, and this framework offers a structured, reproducible approach that data engineers can adapt. It demonstrates how quality gates and change data capture can prevent load failures and silent data loss, which are critical risks in M&A integration projects. The hybrid matching approach combines deterministic and blocked fuzzy matching, and calibrating the threshold per object would raise F1 scores to 0.938 for accounts and 0.963 for contacts. Quality gates prevented all load failures and silent loss of unmapped picklist values without false positives, whereas removing them produced about 1,000 load failures per run; validation on a production deployment remains future work.

**Background**: Salesforce is a widely used CRM platform, and when companies merge, they must combine customer data from two separate Salesforce organizations into one while both remain operational. ## [FuseAMC: CPU-Efficient Decision Fusion for Modulation Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05937) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose FuseAMC, a decision-fusion model that combines a compact 198k-parameter residual CNN with a gradient-boosted-tree cumulant classifier via a logistic-regression meta-combiner. On RadioML 2016.10a, it achieves 55.8% overall accuracy, improving over the best cumulant baseline by 5.1 points and over the CNN alone by 3.2 points, while running entirely on one CPU core. This work shows that classical statistical features and deep learning are complementary across SNR regimes, enabling accurate automatic modulation classification on general-purpose and embedded processors without hardware acceleration. It offers a practical path for spectrum sensing and non-cooperative signal identification in cognitive radio and resource-constrained devices. The fusion is trained on a validation split disjoint from the test set, and the model footprint is a few megabytes with roughly 3 MFLOPs per frame. Evaluation uses 5 seeds on a 44k-frame held-out test set, with paired-bootstrap 95% confidence-interval lower bounds of 5.0 and 3.1 points confirming the gains are positive.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Automatic modulation classification (AMC) is the task of blindly identifying the modulation scheme of a received signal from its in-phase and quadrature (I/Q) samples, which is essential for spectrum sensing and cognitive radio. Two common CPU-feasible approaches are compact convolutional neural networks (CNNs) on raw I/Q data and classical classifiers using higher-order cumulant features, but they are rarely compared conditioned on signal-to-noise ratio (SNR). Decision fusion combines the outputs of multiple classifiers, often via a meta-combiner such as logistic regression, to improve overall accuracy.

## [BAM: Bivariate Additive Models for Interpretable Tabular Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05940) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose Bivariate Additive Models (BAMs), a class of GA²Ms where each pairwise shape function is constrained to an asymmetric bivariate Gaussian, making every component visualizable as a unimodal contour map. Experiments on six tabular benchmarks show BAM attains accuracy comparable to standard GAMs and approaching that of GA²Ms. This work addresses the auditability gap in interpretable machine learning: while GA²Ms are considered glass-box models, their learned shape functions are often too complex for practitioners to meaningfully interpret or check against domain knowledge. By enforcing simpler, unimodal pairwise shapes, BAM makes it easier to audit models in high-stakes domains such as healthcare and finance. Each pairwise component in BAM is an asymmetric bivariate Gaussian selected automatically during training, and the paper also analyzes the trade-off between predictive accuracy and the number of components a practitioner must inspect. The approach is evaluated on six tabular benchmarks, showing accuracy comparable to standard GAMs and approaching GA²Ms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Generalized Additive Models (GAMs) are interpretable models that sum univariate shape functions, and GA²Ms extend them with a small number of pairwise interaction terms, as introduced by Lou et al. in 2013. Methods like the Explainable Boosting Machine (EBM) and NODE-GAM learn such shape functions from data, but the resulting contours can be irregular and hard to interpret. BAM constrains these pairwise functions to a simple parametric form to improve auditability.

## [Ray-Tracing and Transformer-Based CSI Prediction for Dynamic Wi-Fi Sounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05970) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes a dynamic Wi-Fi channel sounding framework that combines Sionna-based ray tracing with a rotary-position-embedding Transformer to autoregressively predict CSI across subcarriers under device mobility, and selects sounding intervals via SINR-constrained throughput maximization. In Wi-Fi 7 link-level simulations, predicted-CSI beamforming improved mean goodput by 67.3%, 112.7%, and 119.1% over stale CSI for linear, circular, and cosine trajectories, respectively. This work addresses the trade-off between channel aging due to mobility and the overhead of frequent CSI sounding in MIMO Wi-Fi, showing that learned prediction can substantially boost throughput without proportionally increasing sounding overhead. It is relevant to wireless researchers and engineers working on physical-layer AI and next-generation Wi-Fi beamforming. The sounding-interval selection is formulated as a predicted average-throughput maximization problem under per-stream SINR lower-bound constraints, including CSI acquisition and processing overhead, and is globally solved via cumulative-sum enumeration for the finite single-variable problem. A prediction-error perturbation analysis provides the SINR bounds, and actual SINR feasibility holds whenever prediction errors satisfy these bounds; results are based on ray-tracing experiments and link-level simulations rather than real-world testbeds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: In MIMO Wi-Fi, channel state information (CSI) describes the wireless channel and is used for beamforming, but it must be acquired through sounding, which consumes airtime. Device mobility causes the channel to age, so CSI becomes stale between soundings; predicting future CSI with autoregressive models can help, but errors accumulate over time. Sionna is an open-source GPU-accelerated library for differentiable wireless simulation and ray tracing, and rotary position embedding (RoPE) is a technique for encoding position information in Transformers that captures relative positions.

Clips2Story introduces a training-free, LLM-based pipeline that takes a pool of unordered video clips and a user-specified keyword, retrieves relevant clips via multimodal descriptors, and uses an LLM to arrange them into an editable storyboard with connective narrations. The pipeline extracts text-based multimodal descriptors for each clip, retrieves candidates relevant to the keyword, and passes their textual descriptors to an LLM for selection and arrangement; accompanying visuals for narrations are then retrieved to compose the final video. Evaluation used scene-change detection to segment videos and shuffling to remove temporal order, but the abstract is truncated and the work is an arXiv preprint without peer review.

## [Simulated Annealing and Branch-and-Bound Advance Latin Rectangle Imbalance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06003) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper studies the minimum imbalance of Latin rectangles, improving 33 of 51 reference values (by up to 39.4%) using a simulated annealing algorithm and proving the exact minimum imbalance for 3×9 rectangles is 56 via branch and bound. The work also provides asymptotic bounds and formal verification in Lean 4. This result advances combinatorial optimization by combining heuristic search with exact methods, and the Lean 4 verification ensures correctness, which is valuable for both mathematical research and algorithm design. It also provides new insights into the structure of Latin rectangles and their imbalance. The simulated annealing moves symbols within rows and penalizes column clashes, while a row group invariant certifies that new rectangles are not isotopic to group tables. A sharper pruning bound derived from I=2∑(d-t)^+ reduces the search tree by a factor of 2.4 to 3.3, and the asymptotic minimum imbalance is Θ(n^3) for fixed k.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: A Latin rectangle is a k×n array filled with symbols such that each row and column contains no repeated symbol. The imbalance measures how far the rectangle is from being perfectly balanced, where balance means the points corresponding to symbols are pairwise equidistant in the ℓ1 metric. Simulated annealing is a probabilistic optimization technique inspired by metallurgical annealing, and branch and bound is an exact algorithm that systematically explores solution space with pruning.

## [Estimating Bayes Optimal Fairness-Accuracy Tradeoff with Soft Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06027) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper derives an explicit expression for the fair Bayes error rate—the best achievable error subject to fairness constraints—and constructs consistent, instance-free estimators that use only soft labels and group information. It also provides provable bounds on bias and sample complexity, and empirically shows robust, low-variance estimates of the optimal fairness-accuracy tradeoff curve even with noisy soft labels. This work extends state-of-the-art Bayes error estimation to fairness-constrained classification, providing a benchmark for the fundamental limit of fair models and a tool to compare fair classifiers against the Pareto frontier. It could help practitioners detect overfitting in fair models and set realistic performance expectations. The estimators require only soft labels (positive class probabilities) and group information, with no input features or auxiliary training, and the method is proven consistent with bounded bias and sample complexity. Empirically, it produces low-variance estimates even when soft labels are noisy and derived from a classification model on hard labels.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: The Bayes error rate is the lowest possible error rate for any classifier on a given data distribution, serving as a benchmark and overfitting detector. Recent techniques estimate it for binary classification using only soft labels, without features or training. Fair classification imposes constraints such as equalized odds, requiring similar true positive rates across demographic groups, which creates a fairness-accuracy tradeoff or Pareto frontier. This paper extends Bayes error estimation to that constrained setting.

TouchéWorld is a new framework that integrates gesture-driven hand interaction into a camera-controlled video world model, generating video conditioned on temporally aligned camera and hand controls from an initial image and scene-description prompt. It builds joint camera–hand training data from heterogeneous egocentric videos via annotation completion, consistency-based filtering, and temporal alignment, and introduces TouchéBench to evaluate hand-motion following, camera–hand coordination, online interaction, and object response. ## [Drift-Adaptive Representation Preservation for Domain-Incremental Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06093) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces a drift-adaptive representation preservation method for domain-incremental learning that uses measured representation drift to allocate preservation effort and down-weight drifted domains in prototype construction. It repurposes a Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture (JEPA) to measure drift without storing past raw data, storing only compact context summaries. This work addresses non-uniform forgetting in domain-incremental learning by providing a measurable surrogate for forgetting risk, potentially improving accuracy-forgetting trade-offs across benchmarks. It offers a memory-efficient solution that could benefit applications requiring continual adaptation to new domains without access to past data. The method uses a consolidation loss that assigns stronger preservation to high-drift pairs and prototype construction that down-weights highly drifted domains. Evaluated on DomainNet, Office-Home, CORe50, and CDDB-Hard, it achieves a strong accuracy-forgetting balance with substantially less training memory and the smallest storage among compared methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Domain-incremental learning is a continual learning scenario where a model sequentially adapts to new input distributions while the label space remains fixed. Catastrophic forgetting, the tendency of neural networks to forget previously learned information upon learning new data, is a key challenge. Prototype-based methods use compact feature vectors to represent classes and mitigate forgetting, and pretrained vision models provide strong general-purpose representations that can be adapted to new domains.

The paper introduces AssameseLM-E2B and its instruction-tuned variant AssameseLM-E2B-it, a 2-billion-parameter open-weight model built on the Gemma-4 architecture and adapted for the low-resource Assamese language. It uses an expanded byte-level BPE vocabulary that reduces tokenization overhead by nearly 500% compared with multilingual 2B–3B baselines, and is trained via a Hybrid LoRA continual pre-training pipeline followed by supervised fine-tuning. This work shows that parameter-efficient adaptation can produce capable, locally deployable language models for low-resource languages under constrained compute, which matters for communities and developers who lack access to large-scale GPU resources. It also highlights specific failure modes of general-purpose multilingual models, such as generative degradation on Romanized Assamese and unintended Bengali script assimilation. Evaluation reports a Bits-per-Byte (BPB) score of 0.7488 on FLORES-200, an overall RAG score of 96.7%, and 100% refusal accuracy on adversarial unanswerable prompts. The model is a single-language 2B model, so its broader impact is limited, and the reported gains are relative to 2B–3B multilingual baselines rather than larger models.

**Background**: Byte-level BPE tokenization converts text into UTF-8 bytes and learns merges over that byte stream, which lets a tokenizer handle any script but can be inefficient for languages underrepresented in the training corpus. Continual pre-training adapts an already-trained model to a new language or domain, and LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) makes this cheaper by training small adapter matrices instead of all weights. FLORES-200 is a multilingual benchmark with human-reviewed translations for over 200 languages, commonly used to evaluate low-resource language models.

## [CoSafe: Cooperative Runtime Safety for Mixed-Traffic Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06204) ⭐️ 6.0/10

CoSafe introduces a modular, ADS-agnostic cooperative runtime safety assurance framework that uses V2X communication and roadside units (RSUs) to monitor scene-level hazards and jointly mitigate violations involving multiple vehicles in mixed traffic. Integrated with seven state-of-the-art ADSes and evaluated on the InterDrive benchmark, CoSafe improves driving score by 70.03% on average, reduces safety violations by 87.80%, and identifies unsafe AVs with an F3 score of 0.930 while meeting real-time constraints. Existing ego-centric assurance approaches fail in mixed traffic because violations often stem from locally reasonable but jointly incompatible decisions among multiple vehicles, which cannot be resolved by independent takeover. CoSafe extends runtime safety assurance from ego-centric takeover to scene-level cooperative intervention, potentially improving safety for AVs, human-driven vehicles, cyclists, and pedestrians sharing the road. CoSafe leverages vehicle-to-everything (V2X) communication to enable roadside units to monitor scene-level hazards and jointly mitigate hazards involving multiple vehicles, and it is designed to be modular and ADS-agnostic so it can integrate with different autonomous driving systems. The evaluation on the InterDrive benchmark shows significant improvements in driving score and safety violations while satisfying real-time constraints, though the paper is an arXiv preprint without peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Autonomous driving systems (ADSes) are increasingly deployed on public roads, creating mixed-traffic environments where autonomous vehicles (AVs) interact with human-driven vehicles (HDVs), cyclists, and pedestrians. Runtime safety assurance frameworks continuously monitor for hazards and intervene to prevent violations, but traditional approaches are ego-centric—they focus on a single vehicle and rely on independent takeover. In mixed traffic, safety violations often arise from joint decisions among multiple vehicles that are locally reasonable but globally incompatible, motivating cooperative approaches that use V2X communication and roadside infrastructure to coordinate safety interventions.

## [MonoLaDN Distills VLM Reasoning into Monocular 3D Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06282) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MonoLaDN introduces a language-guided denoising framework that transfers a frozen vision-language model's geometric reasoning into a purely vision-based monocular 3D object detector during training. The VLM generates structured reasoning about occlusion, depth, dimension, and angle, which is encoded into queries, perturbed with noise, and reconstructed by the detector, with the reasoning path discarded at inference. This approach improves monocular 3D detection accuracy without adding inference cost or parameters, making it attractive for low-cost autonomous driving perception. It demonstrates a practical way to leverage large VLM priors in latency-sensitive deployment scenarios. Experiments on KITTI 3D and nuScenes show consistent outperformance over state-of-the-art detectors and good generalization to new domains. The frozen VLM acts as a teacher only during training, so the deployed model remains purely vision-based with no extra latency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Monocular 3D object detection aims to infer 3D bounding boxes from a single image, which is fundamentally ill-posed because depth must be estimated from one view. Transformer-based detectors learn geometry from image features and 3D box annotations but lack high-level semantic priors. Vision-language models acquire such priors from large-scale pretraining, but deploying them directly as detectors is impractical due to heavy inference cost. Knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a large teacher model to a smaller student model, often using a frozen teacher.

## [Bounded Halting Problem Gets an Engineering-Focused Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06303) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper formalizes the bounded halting problem with a step budget T and uses a diagonal argument to prove that no fixed deterministic checker can decide all instances within any fixed fraction of T below one, for large T. It also applies bounded-halting instances as adversarial tests for finitary universality, separating different models of resource growth. This work bridges computability theory and engineering practice by providing a rigorous framework for certifying bounded halting, which is directly relevant to resource-bounded verification and the evaluation of machine-learning systems' universality claims. It clarifies the resource growth assumptions underlying universality and offers a way to test whether deployed models meet them. The diagonal argument includes explicit additive overhead and places the simulated program and checker on the same unit-cost timescale, enabling the checker to be embedded in an adversarial program. In the nondeterministic case, T bounds the branch depth for a stable observable decision, and the tests check resource growth with T, absence of a fixed deliberation bound, and later access to intermediate data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: The halting problem, formulated by Alan Turing in 1936, asks whether a program eventually halts with unrestricted resources and is undecidable. The bounded halting problem restricts the program to a fixed number of steps T, making it decidable by simulation. Diagonalization is a proof technique, originally from Cantor, used to show undecidability and separate complexity classes. Finitary universality refers to a machine's ability to simulate any finite computation, a notion relevant to universal Turing machines and, by extension, to general-purpose machine-learning models.

A new paper (arXiv:2611.06320) proposes a conformal prediction framework for distribution-to-distribution regression that operates in an orthogonal basis space to produce interpretable prediction sets while maintaining coverage guarantees. It also introduces an adaptive variant with asymptotic conditional coverage under mild assumptions, and code is publicly available on GitHub. This work addresses a key limitation of naive conformal prediction in distribution regression, where prediction sets are marginally valid but non-interpretable, and could enable reliable uncertainty quantification in applications like functional data analysis and high-dimensional output settings. The proposed method constructs prediction sets in an orthogonal basis space, ensuring they have well-defined volume and tractable sampling, unlike naive split conformal prediction. Empirical results on synthetic and real-world datasets validate the approach, though theoretical guarantees rely on mild assumptions for the adaptive variant.

## [Biased Gradients in Iterative Feedback Tuning for Throttle Valve Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06338) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper analyzes biased gradient estimates in iterative feedback tuning (IFT) for model-free control of automotive electronic throttle valves, showing that stiction-induced state mismatch between the two closed-loop experiments introduces a bias inversely proportional to the amplitude of the second experiment. On digital twins of ten valves, the IFT gradient is a descent direction in only 69% of the low-cost region, and all update rules plateau and then drift. This work highlights a fundamental limitation of IFT when applied to real-world systems with nonlinear friction, showing that the common assumption of unbiased gradients can fail and lead to controller degradation. It provides valuable insights for practitioners tuning model-free controllers in automotive and industrial control systems where stiction is prevalent. The study uses an intelligent proportional (iP) controller, which is an incremental PI controller, and evaluates on digital twins and two hardware valves. On hardware, the best update rule depends on the noise structure: Adam outperforms Gauss-Newton when one gradient component is buried in noise, but on the second valve where both components are consistent, Adam's normalization amplifies a direction that Gauss-Newton discounts, causing Adam to drift to a worse controller.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Iterative feedback tuning (IFT) is a data-driven method that tunes fixed-structure controller parameters using gradient estimates obtained from closed-loop experiments, without requiring a plant model. Model-free control, such as the intelligent proportional (iP) controller, relies on online estimation of unmodeled dynamics and is popular in automotive applications. Electronic throttle valves exhibit friction and stiction, which are nonlinear effects that can cause state mismatch between experiments and bias gradient estimates.

## [IDSISA: Influence-Distilled Sharded Training for Efficient Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06519) ⭐️ 6.0/10

IDSISA (Influence-Distilled SISA) uses influence functions to concentrate forget-prone samples into fewer shards and applies knowledge distillation from unaffected shards to recover utility lost due to isolation. It inherits SISA's exact deletion guarantee and introduces a closure-based cascading retraining procedure to handle sequences of deletion requests, with experiments showing 14–91% better selective forgetting and up to 50% better utility retention relative to SISA. This work addresses a key inefficiency in certified machine unlearning: random sharding scatters forget requests across many shards, increasing retraining cost. By reducing the number of shards that need retraining while maintaining formal deletion guarantees, IDSISA could make certified unlearning more practical for large-scale models subject to privacy regulations like GDPR. The deletion guarantee from SISA's retraining step remains exact and requires no probabilistic qualification, while the distillation-based utility recovery is a separately quantified approximation with ε_cert ≤ 0.015 in expectation. Experiments on ResNet-18/CIFAR-10, ResNet-18/CIFAR-100, BiLSTM/IMDB, TCN/IMDB, and a fully connected network on Fashion-MNIST show unlearning-time changes ranging from -6.7% to +6.4% depending on architecture.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Machine unlearning aims to remove the influence of specific training examples from a trained model to comply with privacy regulations. SISA (Sharded, Isolated, Sliced, and Aggregated training) is a framework that achieves certified deletion by splitting data into shards, training independent models on each shard, and aggregating their outputs, so that deleting a record only requires retraining the shard that contained it. Influence functions, a classic technique from robust statistics, measure how a model's prediction changes if a training point is upweighted or removed, and are used here to identify forget-prone samples. Knowledge distillation is a model compression technique where a smaller student model is trained to mimic a larger teacher model, and in IDSISA it is used to transfer knowledge from unaffected shards to recover utility lost due to isolation.

## [Pre-reproduction signals predict archive entry and retention oppositely in QD search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06522) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.06522) studies credit assignment in unstructured Quality-Diversity archives, testing whether signals available before reproduction can distinguish offspring that merely enter the archive from those retained generations later. Across a planar-arm benchmark and two robotic-locomotion tasks, the same signals often predict entry and persistence in opposite directions, with lineage depth being the clearest such signal. This work challenges the intuition that signals correlated with archive entry can be used to guide parent selection for long-term retention, showing that biasing toward survival-associated signals does not improve search. It has implications for automated failure discovery and evolutionary robotics, where allocating search effort efficiently is critical. Biasing parent selection toward the signals with the strongest measured association to survival does not improve search: moderate bias matches uniform sampling, while strong bias reduces QD-score. The persistence discrimination of lineage depth grows with population capacity, which is associated with lower turnover.

## [Test-time optimization turns frozen reconstructors into time-series imputers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06612) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes converting a frozen sequence reconstructor into an imputer at inference time by treating missing entries as optimization variables and minimizing reconstruction error only on observed coordinates. The method, called observed-self-consistency, requires no artificial mask augmentation during training and is analyzed via the cross-Jacobian from missing inputs to observed residuals. This approach offers a lightweight way to adapt existing reconstruction models to sensor-channel failures without retraining, which could be valuable in real-world deployments where failure patterns are unpredictable. It also provides theoretical insight into when test-time refinement is informative, bridging test-time optimization and missing-data imputation. Across five real-world datasets and four reconstruction architectures, no-prior test-time refinement reduced MAE in 19 of 20 dataset-backbone combinations compared to one-pass reconstruction. However, the best refined reconstructor achieved the lowest MAE on only two datasets and trailed the strongest mask-aware baseline on three, with gains requiring substantially more inference time and being sensitive to cross-channel coupling.

The cross-Jaacobian matrix measures how changes in missing inputs affect observed residuals, helping determine when refinement is useful.

## [Study reframes AI ethics 'gap' as situated epistemic and ethical practice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06709) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.06709) draws on 14 semi-structured interviews with designers and governors of AI-enabled anti-trafficking systems across law enforcement, commercial, research, and survivor-support contexts to argue that the principle-practice gap in Responsible AI is not merely an implementation or compliance deficit. Instead, it proposes that the gap emerges from varied institutional and professional conditions that shape how actors recognize certain issues as ethical problems, tracing four interconnected moments: perception, judgement, situated action, and socio-technical materialisation. This reframing matters for AI ethics and governance researchers because it shifts the focus from individual awareness or compliance to the institutional and epistemic infrastructures that determine which concerns become actionable in design. It suggests that responsible AI governance requires building conditions—not just principles—that make the knowledge of affected communities and potential harms receivable and actionable, which could influence how organizations structure ethics review and stakeholder engagement. The study is qualitative and based on a small sample of 14 interviews, so its findings are context-specific and not statistically generalizable. It introduces the concept of 'epistemic infrastructures' as a governance requirement and shows that ethical agency is exercised within competing constraints of profession, role, prior experience, and institutional settings, with some concerns materialised while others are narrowed, deferred, or left unrealised.

Common explanations frame this as a problem of implementation, awareness, or compliance, implying a deficit in systems or individual behaviour. ## [Smartphone-Powered VR Exergame Gamifies HIIT with AI Pose Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06734) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers developed a VR exergame system that uses a standard smartphone camera on a tripod to capture full-body movement during High-Intensity Interval Training (HIIT) workouts, with pose estimation and a backend built on Anthropic's Model Context Protocol (MCP) and GPT-4o to generate personalized feedback. A study with 20 participants found significantly higher enjoyment, immersion, and likelihood to recommend in the VR condition compared to non-VR, and the MCP system showed 87.5% agreement with expert evaluation. This approach offers a cost-effective and scalable alternative to wearable motion capture suits or headset-based sensors, making immersive fitness more accessible for home users. It also demonstrates a practical integration of large language models and open standards like MCP into real-time health and fitness applications. The system supports 11 predefined combo moves (e.g., squat-punch, bilateral arm strikes, diagonal stretches) and analyzes biomechanical data for form accuracy, movement efficiency, intensity, and injury risk. The study was limited to 20 participants aged 18-25 and expert validation involved only one certified trainer across 10 sessions, so broader generalizability remains to be tested.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: HIIT (High-Intensity Interval Training) alternates short bursts of intense exercise with recovery periods, and VR exergames combine virtual reality with physical exercise to make workouts more engaging. Pose estimation algorithms detect body keypoints from images or video, and Anthropic's Model Context Protocol (MCP), introduced in November 2024, is an open standard that connects AI models like GPT-4o to external data sources and tools.

## [ZETA decouples routing policy from action execution in scientific vision agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06760) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ZETA introduces an extensible architecture that separates intent routing from visual tool execution in scientific image-analysis agents, enabling independent evaluation of routing and perception failures. On a zero-shot benchmark of 51 scientific-intent queries, explicit routing-policy specification improved segmentation routing accuracy for Qwen 3.8 from 6.2% to 75.0% and detection routing accuracy from 60.0% to 100%. This decoupling exposes the 'black box' of agent decision-making, allowing routing correctness and downstream execution to be audited as separate failure modes, which is critical for deploying reliable agents in high-stakes scientific automation. It also provides the first controlled study isolating routing-policy specification while holding the legal action interface fixed. ZETA treats measurement as a deterministic operation grounded in explicit geometry rather than generative output, and its execution traces make routing decisions and downstream artifacts separately observable. However, the improvements vary across models, and the evaluation is limited to a zero-shot benchmark of 51 queries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Scientific image-analysis workflows require agents to choose among capabilities like description, detection, counting, and segmentation. Current multi-tool architectures conflate intent interpretation with visual execution, making it hard to tell whether a failure comes from misrouting or from failed perception. ZETA addresses this by resolving scientific intent before invoking specialized visual tools.

## [FedFiOT: Fisher-Weighted Optimal Transport for Federated Model Merging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06783) ⭐️ 6.0/10

FedFiOT introduces a Fisher-weighted optimal transport framework for one-shot and few-round federated learning that jointly optimizes neuron correspondences and global model parameters via alternating minimization of a shared Fisher-weighted objective. The method leverages Fisher information to quantify parameter importance and guide neuron alignment, and the authors theoretically prove monotonic decrease of the layer-wise merging objective and establish a stationarity bound. One-shot and few-round federated learning drastically reduce communication costs but suffer from client drift and performance degradation compared to multi-round FL. FedFiOT addresses this by combining optimal transport for neuron alignment with Fisher information for importance weighting, potentially enabling more efficient and accurate federated model merging in bandwidth-constrained or privacy-sensitive settings. The framework uses alternating minimization to jointly optimize neuron correspondences and global parameters, and the theoretical analysis provides a stationarity bound that quantifies the effects of client heterogeneity and nonuniform Fisher weighting. Experiments across multiple datasets show consistent improvements over competing baselines in both one-shot and few-round settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Federated learning trains a shared model across many clients without centralizing data, but conventional multi-round approaches require frequent communication. One-shot and few-round FL reduce communication by constructing a global model in one or a few rounds, yet client models may drift apart, causing neurons that learn similar features to occupy different positions. Optimal transport has been used to align neurons across models before averaging, and Fisher information is a standard tool for measuring parameter importance in neural networks.

## [AdaMamba: Adaptive Frequency-Gated Mamba for Long-Term Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23239) ⭐️ 6.0/10

AdaMamba introduces an adaptive frequency-gated Mamba architecture that internalizes input-dependent frequency information into state-space modeling for long-term time series forecasting. It combines an interaction learning module for cross-variable dependencies with an adaptive frequency-gated state space module that unifies temporal forgetting into a single time-frequency gate. This work addresses the limitation of heuristic frequency methods that ignore time-frequency heterogeneity, where variables synchronized in time can differ in frequency. By integrating adaptive frequency analysis directly into Mamba's state transitions, it could improve forecasting accuracy in web-scale applications like recommendations, energy scheduling, and capacity planning. AdaMamba generates context-dependent frequency bases and modulates state evolution through a single time-frequency gate, avoiding predefined periods or top-k frequency selection. Experiments on seven public LTSF benchmarks and four domain-specific datasets show consistent outperformance over state-of-the-art baselines with competitive computational efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Long-term time series forecasting (LTSF) aims to predict future values over extended horizons, requiring models to capture long-range dependencies and periodic patterns. Mamba is a state space model that uses input-dependent selective parameterization for linear-time sequence modeling, offering efficiency for long sequences. Frequency-aware methods provide a global view of temporal dynamics but often rely on inflexible heuristics like predefined period lengths or manually selected frequency components.

## [Paper Proposes Work-Centered Framework for AI Agent Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23262) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.23262v3) proposes a work-centered representation for AI agent benchmarks of knowledge work, deriving four implicit design choices from studies of professions, situated action, boundary objects, and validity theory. It introduces reporting practices for four missing fields—represented activity, tested setting, required work product, and evaluated result—and clusters O*NET task statements into 18 shared work activities to support reporting. This framework addresses a key gap in agent evaluation: benchmarks for knowledge work are typically described only by tasks, environments, and metrics, making comparison difficult. By providing a common description, it enables side-by-side comparison of benchmarks designed in disparate ways and could improve the validity and interpretability of AI agent evaluations. The authors analyzed three popular knowledge work benchmarks—GDPval, OfficeQA Pro, and APEX-SWE—and found that across all three, the scope covered by the evaluated result is narrower than that of the required work product. An audit of all 25 public APEX-SWE Integration tasks revealed that in 13 tasks, a deliverable can alter established content from the source data and still pass all tests.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Agent benchmarks evaluate AI agents on tasks that simulate real-world work, but current benchmarks often lack a standardized way to describe what work is actually being performed. The paper draws on sociological concepts: studies of professions examine how expert work is organized, situated action theory emphasizes that actions are shaped by context, boundary objects are artifacts that enable coordination across different groups, and validity theory concerns whether a test measures what it claims to measure. O*NET is a database of occupational tasks that the authors use to categorize work activities.

## [TraceCAD: Trace-Guided Repair for Agentic CAD Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03062) ⭐️ 6.0/10

TraceCAD introduces a persistent trace-based recovery layer for LLM-based CAD agents that links requested features, modeling steps, failure evidence, and candidate outcomes as persistent state. It diagnoses faulty operations, searches bounded edits in dependency regions, and stores successful and failed repair outcomes in reusable skill memory. This work shows that preserving repair evidence and localizing edits can significantly improve the reliability of LLM-generated CAD programs, with ablations showing that removing persistent state nearly halves recovery score and removing localized search more than doubles geometric regression and code-agent invocations. It suggests a practical path for making agentic design tools more robust without retraining the underlying model. On DeepCAD-derived benchmarks with 200-model ablations and a 1K-model comparison, TraceCAD achieves competitive geometric quality in IoU, Chamfer distance, and Hausdorff distance. Initializing the skill store on disjoint training models further reduces retries, token cost, and latency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: LLM-based CAD agents generate executable parametric programs, but their correction loops often lose track of which requirements were satisfied, which operations failed, and what repairs were previously attempted. DeepCAD is a widely used dataset and benchmark for parametric CAD sequence generation, and Chamfer distance and Hausdorff distance are common metrics for comparing 3D shapes. TraceCAD addresses the evidence-loss problem by maintaining a persistent trace and reusing repair outcomes as skills.

## [STRIDE: Automated Evaluation of Text-to-Trajectory Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34799) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduce STRIDE, the first framework for automatically evaluating context alignment between scenario descriptions and generated pedestrian trajectories, using a sociologically derived VRDST evaluation protocol instead of costly real-world data collection. They instantiate it as STRIDE-Bench, comprising 1K scenarios, 6K behavioral questions, and 11K measurements across 30 real-world maps, achieving 80% human agreement. This addresses a real gap in scalable evaluation for language-conditioned trajectory generation, where existing metrics rely on comparing against real human data that is infeasible to collect for every scenario. It could enable more reliable benchmarking and drive progress in context-aware pedestrian behavior modeling for robotics and autonomous systems. STRIDE decomposes high-level context into scenario-adaptive behavioral questions resolved against a deterministic measurement tool library for reproducible answers, and evaluation of several text-to-trajectory models reveals limited context-alignment capability and persistent challenges in fine-grained context conditioning. The framework is currently instantiated only in the crowd domain as STRIDE-Bench.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Text-to-trajectory generation is an emerging task where models produce motion paths for agents (such as pedestrians) conditioned on natural language descriptions. Traditional pedestrian trajectory prediction is evaluated with metrics like Average Displacement Error (ADE) and Final Displacement Error (FDE), which compare predicted coordinates to ground-truth trajectories. However, these metrics do not scale to text-conditioned generation across diverse contexts, because pedestrian behavior is heterogeneous and context-dependent, and collecting human trajectories for every scenario is costly.

## [Isharah-Selfie: A New Dataset for One-Handed Arabic Sign Language Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33200) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Isharah-Selfie, a large-scale Arabic Sign Language dataset of 8,997 selfie videos recorded by 16 signers across 1,493 unique sentences using front-facing smartphone cameras under unconstrained conditions. Each video is annotated with gloss sequences for continuous sign language recognition (CSLR) and Arabic sentence translations for sign language translation (SLT), and the authors define signer-independent and unseen-sentence evaluation protocols. This dataset addresses a gap in sign language recognition benchmarks, which are typically collected in controlled studio settings with fixed cameras and two-handed signing, and thus fail to reflect everyday mobile communication. By focusing on realistic selfie conditions, it supports research toward robust, user-centered sign language technologies that can operate reliably on smartphones, benefiting the accessibility community and Arabic Sign Language users. The dataset captures one-handed signing, close-range viewpoints, partial body visibility, camera motion, unconstrained environments, and heterogeneous device resolutions, which differ from conventional fixed-camera, two-handed datasets. Extensive benchmarks using RGB-based and pose-based methods show that current CSLR and SLT approaches remain challenged by selfie-style signing, particularly under unseen-sentence evaluation where compositional generalization is weak.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Continuous sign language recognition (CSLR) is the task of recognizing sequences of signs from video without prior knowledge of temporal boundaries between signs, and it typically requires annotated RGB data. Arabic Sign Language (ArSL) is a primary sign language used across Arab countries, but it has been underrepresented in sign language datasets. Most existing benchmarks use studio-style cameras and two-handed signing, which do not reflect how signers communicate in mobile scenarios. One-handed signing is common in everyday communication, especially when using a smartphone, but it is rarely captured in existing datasets.

## [Entropy-Based RelPPR Metric Improves Black-Box LLM Text Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33767) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.33767v3) proposes the Relative Peak Prominence Rate (RelPPR), a metric that captures localized entropy fluctuations to detect LLM-generated text in a black-box, zero-shot setting. The authors show that existing state-of-the-art detectors, such as dual-model and resampling-based methods, catastrophically fail on instruction-conditioned text, and that RelPPR achieves state-of-the-art performance across diverse model families and datasets. Reliable detection of machine-generated text is crucial for mitigating LLM misuse, especially when the source model is unknown (black-box) and text is produced under instruction prompts. This work addresses a real failure mode of current detectors and offers a more robust signal, which could benefit content moderation, academic integrity, and AI safety applications. The method traces the failure of existing detectors to poor prompt conditioning for resampling-based methods and diminished cross-model discrepancy for dual-model detectors. RelPPR leverages the observation that human writing maintains a steady information flow, whereas machine-generated text exhibits sharp, localized entropy fluctuations, and the code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Zero-shot detection of LLM-generated text aims to identify machine-written content without training on labeled data from the specific model. Black-box settings mean the detector cannot access the source model's internals or sampling process, which is common for proprietary APIs. Entropy, a measure of uncertainty in token predictions, tends to fluctuate differently in human versus machine text, and peak prominence quantifies how much a local maximum stands out relative to surrounding values.

## [BCSM: Efficient Score Matching for Discrete Diffusion Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04279) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes Bidirectional Centered Score Matching (BCSM), a within-position distillation objective for discrete diffusion models that reduces pairwise complexity from O(|V|^2) to O(K^2) and factorizes to O(K), along with cross-timestep transition alignment. Experiments show up to 6x NFE reduction with a 16.9% relative HumanEval gain and a 15.1% relative MMMU gain at single-step inference. This work addresses the inefficiency of discrete diffusion distillation by providing a more scalable objective, which could enable faster and more accurate few-step inference for language modeling and other discrete data generation tasks. The significant gains in benchmarks like HumanEval and MMMU suggest practical impact for deploying diffusion-based models in resource-constrained settings. BCSM constructs an O(K) bidirectional active set to reduce pairwise comparisons to O(K^2) and factorizes them into an O(K) objective, avoiding the O(|V|^2) complexity of full Concrete Score Matching. The method also introduces cross-timestep transition alignment to improve supervision under aggressive few-step distillation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Discrete diffusion models are generative models that operate on discrete state spaces (e.g., tokens) via a forward jump diffusion process and a reverse process, and are often used for language modeling. Knowledge distillation compresses a large teacher model into a smaller student model by mimicking outputs, and in diffusion models it aims to reduce the number of function evaluations (NFE) during sampling. Token-wise KL divergence is a common distillation objective but may provide insufficient supervision under few-step settings, while Concrete Score Matching offers an alternative but with quadratic complexity in vocabulary size.
