## [Bandit Framework for Incentivizing Perfect-Knowledge Agents with Unobserved Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.07407) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a repeated principal-agent game within a multi-armed bandit framework where the principal offers arm-specific incentives but cannot observe the agent's rewards, and designs a policy that achieves square-root regret up to a logarithmic factor when the agent has perfect knowledge of its expected rewards. The policy relies on a novel estimator for the agent's expected reward built from the sequence of offered incentives and chosen arms, analogous to online inverse optimization. This work opens a new direction in bandit literature by combining incentive design with unobserved rewards, which is relevant to real-world applications such as personalized healthcare adherence programs and sustainable transportation. It provides a theoretically grounded approach for principals who must learn agent preferences indirectly through incentive responses. The policy achieves low regret by deriving finite-sample concentration bounds for the proposed estimator, and the framework is validated through numerical simulations on personalized adherence-improving incentive design for long-term statin treatments. The analysis assumes the agent has perfect knowledge of its own expected rewards, which may be a limitation in settings with agent uncertainty.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: The multi-armed bandit problem models sequential decision-making under uncertainty, where a learner chooses among arms to maximize cumulative reward while balancing exploration and exploitation. The principal-agent problem describes a conflict of interest when an agent acts on behalf of a principal under asymmetric information. Regret minimization is a standard objective in online learning, measuring the gap between the learner's performance and the best fixed strategy in hindsight. This paper merges these concepts to study incentive design when the principal cannot directly observe the agent's rewards.

## [TWISTER: Structure-Aware Review Imputation for Sparse Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.01128) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces TWISTER (ToWards Imputation on Sparsity with Textual Edge Graph Representation), a framework that synthesizes missing reviews in sparse recommendation settings by jointly modeling textual semantics and user-item graph structure. It represents interactions as a Textual-Edge Graph (TEG) with reviews as edge attributes, constructs line-graph views, and uses an LLM as a graph-aware aggregator to generate personalized reviews for interactions lacking text. Review sparsity severely limits the effectiveness of review-aware recommender systems, and TWISTER offers a drop-in, structure-aware imputation method that outperforms traditional numeric, graph-based, and LLM baselines, potentially improving recommendation quality and explainability in real-world sparse settings. TWISTER treats reviews as edge attributes in a Textual-Edge Graph and leverages line-graph transformations to capture relational context, using an LLM to aggregate natural-language representations from neighboring interactions. Experiments on multiple datasets show consistent improvements in both imputed review quality and downstream recommendation performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Recommender systems often use textual reviews to capture fine-grained user preferences and improve explainability, but in practice users rarely write reviews, leading to severe sparsity. Traditional imputation methods like matrix completion operate on numeric ratings and lose contextual semantics, while LLM-based augmentation ignores structural dependencies in the user-item graph. Textual-Edge Graphs (TEGs) are a graph format where edges are annotated with free-form text, enabling rich relational modeling, and line-graph transformations convert edges into nodes to capture higher-order relationships.

## [DUP: Unified Detection and Unlearning Framework Purifies Backdoored Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.01647) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose DUP (Detection-guided Unlearning for Purification), a unified framework that detects backdoor-poisoned inputs via feature-level anomalies and then purifies the model using parameter-efficient unlearning guided by knowledge distillation, without full retraining or external clean models. Experiments across diverse attacks and architectures show superior detection accuracy and purification efficacy. Backdoor attacks pose a serious security threat to language models, and existing defenses often require costly retraining or clean reference models. DUP offers a more practical, unified defense that could be deployed in real-world model supply chains to detect and remove backdoors efficiently. The detector combines class-agnostic distances and inter-layer transitions with a weighted scheme to identify poisoned inputs at a fine-grained level. Purification repurposes knowledge distillation to increase output divergence between student and teacher on detected poisoned samples, forcing the model to unlearn the backdoor behavior; code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Backdoor attacks inject hidden triggers into training data or model weights so that the model behaves maliciously when the trigger appears, while performing normally otherwise. Machine unlearning aims to remove specific information or behaviors from a trained model without full retraining, and knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a teacher model to a student model. DUP combines these ideas to detect and unlearn backdoor behavior efficiently.

## [PointRAFT uses height embedding to predict potato tuber weight from partial point clouds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.24193) ⭐️ 6.0/10

PointRAFT is a point cloud regression network that incorporates a dedicated height embedding to predict potato tuber weight from incomplete point clouds. On a test set of 5,254 point clouds, it achieved an MAE of 11.7 g and RMSE of 17.1 g, outperforming six point cloud regression networks without height embedding. This work shows that incorporating geometric priors, such as height from a fixed camera setup, can significantly improve trait estimation from incomplete point clouds, enabling high-throughput phenotyping on commercial harvesters. It has implications for precision agriculture and 3D robotic perception. PointRAFT requires only 6.3 ms per point cloud for preprocessing and inference, allowing processing rates up to 150 tubers per second. The dataset includes 26,688 point clouds from 859 tubers across four cultivars and three growing seasons, and code, weights, and a subset are publicly available.

In agriculture, they enable non-destructive estimation of crop traits like size and weight, but self-occlusion makes them incomplete, causing errors. Deep learning regression networks can predict traits directly from partial point clouds, but typically ignore geometric cues like object height, which can be derived in structured settings such as a harvester with a fixed camera-to-conveyor distance.

The paper introduces COL-Tree (Compacted Object-Landmark Tree), a novel hierarchical data structure that uses landmark-based heuristics instead of Euclidean-based ones to enable efficient graph traversal for queries like Aggregate kNN and k Farthest Neighbors in spatial networks. Experiments on real-world and synthetic datasets show up to three orders of magnitude performance improvement over existing approaches with only small pre-processing overhead. This work addresses a key limitation of existing spatial network search techniques, which rely on Euclidean heuristics that perform poorly on road networks and other graph-based spatial data. The significant speedup could benefit map-based services and other applications requiring efficient POI search for multiple agents or farthest-neighbor queries. The COL-Tree enables hierarchical graph traversal using a more accurate landmark-based heuristic, and the paper presents query algorithms for AkNN, kFN, and other queries. Evaluations cover road networks of varying sizes and edge weights, terrain networks, and 2D physics meshes, demonstrating versatility across different spatial network types.

The paper introduces ALD-Font, an architecture-level disentangled diffusion model with an asymmetric U-Net design that separates content and style at the architectural level, using SimSun-style glyphs as content templates and injecting style features as modulation signals. It also adopts a parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) strategy that updates only style-related modules, and introduces a Background Noise Removal (BNR) module plus new Grey and OCR metrics for evaluation. Most existing few-shot font generation methods achieve only feature-level disentanglement, allowing the generator to re-entangle content and style and causing content distortion or degraded style fidelity. ALD-Font's architecture-level separation could improve style fidelity while maintaining content accuracy, benefiting designers and developers who need to generate custom Chinese fonts from very few reference images. The method is grounded in a theoretical validation of disentanglement effectiveness, and the PEFT strategy is designed to avoid overfitting to the reference images' content under the few-shot setting. Experimental results show significantly higher style fidelity with comparable content accuracy to state-of-the-art methods, and the implementation is available on GitHub.

## [PREBA: Training-Free LLM Framework Predicts Surgical Duration with PCA-Weighted Retrieval and Bayesian Averaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13275) ⭐️ 6.0/10

PREBA is a retrieval-augmented framework that combines PCA-weighted retrieval with Bayesian averaging to ground LLM reasoning in institution-specific evidence for surgical duration prediction, without task-specific fine-tuning. Evaluated on four real-world datasets with three LLMs (Qwen3, DeepSeek-R1, HuatuoGPT-o1), it reduces MAE by up to 64.9% compared to zero-shot inference and achieves R² up to 0.63, matching or surpassing fully supervised ML methods on several datasets. This approach eliminates the need for costly labeled data and computationally intensive fine-tuning, making accurate surgical duration prediction more accessible for hospital resource management. It also provides greater interpretability through retrieved exemplars and explicit priors, which is crucial for clinical adoption. PREBA encodes heterogeneous clinical features into a unified representation, weights each dimension by its contribution to variation across institutional cases via PCA, and consolidates multiple LLM-generated predictions using Bayesian averaging with population-level statistical priors. The code is available at https://github.com/kanxueli/PREBA.

**Background**: Surgical duration prediction is important for hospital resource management, but traditional supervised learning methods require high-quality labeled data and computationally intensive training. Zero-shot LLM inference avoids training but lacks grounding in institution-specific clinical evidence, leading to unstable and clinically misaligned predictions. Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) grounds LLM outputs by retrieving relevant external knowledge, while PCA (Principal Component Analysis) is a dimensionality reduction technique that identifies the most informative dimensions in data. ## [Manifold Matching Regularization Imposes Chosen Geometry on Autoencoder Latent Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16568) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Manifold Matching, a regularization method that imposes a chosen geometric structure on an autoencoder's latent space by minimizing the batch-wise mean squared error between pairwise distances in a reference representation R and those in the latent space Z. The resulting Manifold-Matching Autoencoder (MMAE) matches the global-structure quality of persistence-based autoencoders across fifteen datasets, statistically tying RTD-AE on distance correlation and triplet accuracy, while using only a batched distance loss with no persistence computation. This work provides a simple, label-free regularizer that decouples the dimensionality of the reference representation from the latent bottleneck, allowing any reference to guide any bottleneck and turning fixed embeddings into reusable out-of-sample encoders. It offers an efficient and controllable alternative to geometric or topological regularizers, with potential impact on representation learning where latent space structure is critical. The method requires only reference distances (scalars), so the dimensionality of R is decoupled from that of Z; all experiments use a two-dimensional bottleneck. MMAE stays competitive on local and topological metrics that other methods lead, at the computational cost of a plain autoencoder, and the authors frame it not as a universal winner but as an efficient and controllable regularizer whose reference selects which structure the latent inherits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Autoencoders are neural networks trained to reconstruct their input through a low-dimensional bottleneck, and the geometry of that latent space strongly affects downstream tasks. Manifold learning is based on the manifold hypothesis, which posits that high-dimensional data lie near low-dimensional nonlinear manifolds. Prior work has used geometric or topological regularizers, such as persistent homology, to shape latent spaces, but these can be computationally expensive. Manifold Matching instead matches pairwise distances to a reference representation, a technique inspired by multidimensional scaling.

## [ReMoA: Recursive Modular Adaptation for Multimodal Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25299) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ReMoA introduces a recursive modular adaptation framework that integrates into joint attention layers of diffusion models, iteratively refining visual tokens over multiple recursion steps while sharing parameters via sparse selection of neural modules. It improves DiT-XL recall on ImageNet from 0.57 to 0.62 and boosts SD3-medium's GenEval score from 68.33% to 71.18% without adding parameters through recursion. This work addresses the limited structured reasoning and text-following capabilities of diffusion models by borrowing recursive latent reasoning ideas from language models, potentially advancing text-to-image generation quality and alignment. The parameter-free recursion and sparse module sharing offer an efficient path to richer object details and better-aligned outputs, relevant to researchers and practitioners in multimodal generation. ReMoA is evaluated on class-conditioned ImageNet generation and text-to-image generation using GenEval and DPG benchmarks, with visual latent trajectory and routing analyses showing that modules specialize across diffusion timesteps and diverge at later low-noise steps. The approach achieves gains without introducing additional parameters through recursion, though the abstract does not detail computational overhead or training complexity.

The paper introduces feedback-informed in-context black-box optimization (FICBO), which pretrains a transformer with structured priors to leverage auxiliary feedback of varying reliability for cross-task adaptation. At test time, the model estimates source reliability by comparing observed objective values with auxiliary signals, improving query selection. This approach addresses a key limitation of existing feedback-aware Bayesian optimization methods, which handle tasks independently and cannot adapt across feedback sources. By enabling cross-task adaptation while remaining robust to misleading feedback, FICBO could improve optimization efficiency in real-world scenarios where expert or simulator feedback is imperfect. The model is pretrained across regimes ranging from highly informative to misleading feedback, and empirical investigations illustrate how it perceives test-time sources, offering insights into interpretability and decision-making. The approach is evaluated on both synthetic and real-world tasks.

## [D-RPC compresses reasoning paths for consistent LLM distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07139) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Distillation through Reasoning Path Compression (D-RPC), which constrains a teacher LLM to follow a compact, dynamically maintained bank of reusable high-level reasoning paths when generating rationales for distillation. It also provides a PAC-Bayes analysis of the trade-off between bank size and coverage, and ablations show an intermediate bank size works best. Teacher rationales for similar problems often vary in structure, creating noisy supervision that hinders student learning; D-RPC addresses this by producing consistent yet diverse rationales. Across five math and commonsense reasoning benchmarks with two student models, D-RPC achieves the highest average accuracy among evaluated distillation methods while using fewer tokens than template-heavy alternatives. The method retrieves the most relevant reasoning path for each training question and conditions the teacher to follow it, balancing consistency and diversity. The PAC-Bayes analysis formalizes that smaller banks limit path diversity and risk coverage gaps, and ablations confirm an intermediate bank size is optimal.

As LLM-generated text becomes more sophisticated and diverse, detectors must scale across writing styles, domains, languages, and generator models, and this work offers a practical encoder-only approach that explicitly models within-class variation. It could benefit platforms and researchers needing deployable, input-only detection systems rather than costly multi-model ensembles. The key technical contribution is the Prototype Positioning loss, which separates class-level structure from the within-class variation that differentiates individual prototypes, since adding multiple prototypes alone does not determine which variation each should represent. The method is evaluated on domain, generator, language, and adversarial variation settings, though the abstract does not report absolute performance numbers or computational overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Prototype learning is a neural network approach where models learn reference vectors (prototypes) in an embedding space, often used for interpretable classification such as ProtoPNet. Text embedding models are typically transformer-based encoders that map input text into a vector space where classification can be performed. Standard binary classifiers for LLM-generated text detection output only a label and do not explicitly organize the substantial variation within the human-written or machine-generated class.

## [I/O-Aware Reformulation Makes Wavelet Convolutions Faster and More Memory-Efficient](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10805) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.10805v3) develops an I/O model for wavelet convolutions (WTConv) and uses three algebraic reformulations to create a fused implementation that reduces modeled HBM traffic by about 2.55×, yielding up to 4.35× training speedup and roughly halving peak memory usage compared to the reference WTConv. WTConv is a drop-in replacement for depthwise convolutions that exponentially expands receptive fields with linear parameters, but its reference implementation is severely memory-bound; this work removes that systems overhead, making WTConv practical for training large models without sacrificing its benefits. The three reformulations are: (1) recomputing the inexpensive Haar analysis butterfly on chip, (2) collapsing the multi-level synthesis cascade into a single closed-form pass indexed by output-coordinate bits, and (3) folding learned per-channel scales into the convolution weights. Evaluated on WTConvNeXt across decomposition levels and tensor shapes, the approach performs comparable arithmetic but is substantially faster than the reference.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Wavelet convolution (WTConv) uses the discrete wavelet transform, implemented via analysis and synthesis filter banks, to decompose inputs into frequency sub-bands and expand a network's receptive field exponentially with decomposition levels while keeping parameters linear. High-bandwidth memory (HBM) is the main GPU memory where activations and weights reside; it is fast but still much slower than on-chip SRAM, so excessive data movement between HBM and compute units becomes a bottleneck for memory-bound kernels.

## [MAIA uses multi-agent Socratic inquiry to turn vague artistic intent into verifiable briefs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12097) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers present MAIA (Multi-Agent Intent Articulation), a multi-agent system that scaffolds requirement discovery for bespoke art commissions through Socratic inquiry under a 'Verification over Invention' rule, converting vague affect into a text-only brief of visual terms the user must ratify. In a within-subjects study (N=16), the full configuration produced a large, significant gain in Cognitive Support over a minimal baseline (r=0.97, p_FDR=0.003; LMM p_FDR<0.001), and an exploratory blind review by three professional concept artists found the rewritten briefs more complete and executable. This work targets requirement discovery, an under-served upstream stage that precedes any artist or image generator, and shows that multi-agent scaffolding can help laypeople constitute intent rather than merely refine it. It suggests a practical HCI role for multi-agent LLM systems in creative and client-facing workflows, though the niche domain and small sample limit generalization. The system enforces a validator gate that structurally blocks unratified content from entering the brief, and the evidence includes a within-subjects study with N=16 plus an exploratory blind review by three professional concept artists. The small sample and single niche domain mean the results are descriptive and should be validated in larger, broader studies.

**Background**: In bespoke art commissions, clients often know what they feel but lack the words to specify medium, scale, and palette, creating an articulation bottleneck before any artist or image generator can act. MAIA addresses this with Socratic inquiry, a dialogue method of continual probing questions that helps people examine and clarify their own beliefs, implemented as a multi-agent LLM system in which several agents collaborate on a task. The evaluation uses a within-subjects (repeated-measures) design, where the same participants test all conditions, which reduces random noise and requires fewer participants.

## [SPARK Decouples Knowledge Retention from Privacy Correction in Continual LLM Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29711) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes SPARK, a post-task self-distillation replay method that separates knowledge retention from privacy correction in privacy-preserving continual learning for LLMs. It first freezes the learned post-task distribution and then applies selective correction around this stable reference to reduce annotated PII likelihood while preserving current- and old-task non-PII behavior. This addresses a real tension in continual learning: models must retain broad knowledge across sequential tasks while selectively suppressing sensitive content, and joint optimization can destabilize current-task preservation. SPARK's decomposition offers a practical recipe for deploying continually updated LLMs in privacy-sensitive domains without sacrificing utility. SPARK combines Self-Distillation Replay, which learns the current task while distilling behavior from previous tasks, with Post-Task Privacy Correction, which reduces annotated-PII likelihood while anchoring non-PII behavior to the resulting checkpoint. The work focuses on operational output control rather than formal differential privacy guarantees, and code and data are promised upon publication.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Continual learning aims to train models on a sequence of tasks without catastrophic forgetting, where learning new tasks overwrites previously acquired knowledge. Privacy-preserving continual learning adds the requirement that models not reproduce sensitive content such as personally identifiable information (PII). Self-distillation replay mitigates forgetting by having the model imitate its own earlier outputs, while privacy correction selectively lowers the likelihood of sensitive tokens.

## [Typological Features Predict Cross-Lingual Transfer Without Compute](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39640) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper shows that a random forest trained only on freely available typological features can predict entries of a 24-language cross-lingual transfer matrix with leave-one-language-out Spearman ρ=0.705 and R²=0.49, versus ρ=0.62 for a tuned non-typological control. The authors note that a paired bootstrap cannot separate this improvement at the current scale, and performance drops to ρ≈0.41 when both languages of a held-out pair are unseen. This work suggests that typological databases can serve as a zero-compute screening tool for cross-lingual transfer, potentially replacing hundreds of expensive multilingual training runs with a single model fit. It could help NLP practitioners prioritize which source languages to use, though the modest and statistically inseparable gains limit immediate practical impact. The signal survives leave-one-script-out and leave-one-family-out protocols, indicating that script and language-family confounding do not explain the effect. By decomposing transfer into a typology term and a resource-and-script bias term, the authors find that best-source rankings are sensitive to this bias, which typological features never observe.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Cross-lingual transfer refers to how knowledge learned in a source language benefits performance in a target language, and it is typically measured by training multilingual models and building transfer matrices across many language pairs. Typological features are structural properties of languages, such as word order or morphological complexity, cataloged in databases like WALS. Prior work has argued that such features can inform NLP, but whether they can predict transfer without running any training has been an open question.

## [DTA-GT: Direction- and Topology-Aware Graph Transformer for Neural Architecture Representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16822) ⭐️ 6.0/10

DTA-GT introduces encoder-wide directional consistency, a design principle that coordinates directed computational semantics across node initialization, global spectral encoding, pairwise interaction, and feature updates. The model integrates direction- and topology-aware node initialization, Magnetic Laplacian spectral encoding, direction-dual structural attention, and a Direction-Sensitive MoE-FFN, outperforming representative predictors on NAS-Bench-101 and NAS-Bench-201 under limited-budget settings. Architecture attribute predictors are key to reducing the cost of neural architecture search, and DTA-GT's coordinated direction-aware design offers a more principled way to capture the directed acyclic graph semantics of neural architectures. This could lead to more accurate and efficient NAS predictors, benefiting researchers and practitioners who rely on NAS for model design. The approach is validated on NAS-Bench-101 and NAS-Bench-201 for both accuracy and latency prediction, with ablations and Magnetic spectral controls supporting the contribution of direction-aware stages. However, the evaluation is limited to these benchmarks and limited-budget settings, and the work is an incremental technical contribution to NAS representation learning.

**Background**: Neural architecture search (NAS) automates the design of neural networks, but evaluating many candidate architectures is expensive. Predictors estimate architecture accuracy or latency from a small set of evaluated candidates, and neural architectures are naturally represented as operation-labeled directed acyclic graphs (DAGs) where edge directions encode computational roles and dependencies. Graph Transformers combine attention mechanisms with graph structure, and recent work uses them as NAS predictors; the Magnetic Laplacian is a spectral tool for directed graphs that encodes edge directionality into the graph Laplacian.

## [Synthetic chest X-rays can bypass dataset intake quality checks, study finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29152) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.29152v2) evaluates whether generated chest radiographs submitted as real clinical data can pass automated quality checks and origin classifiers. Using Qwen3.8-27B for candidate selection, MedGemma 1.5 for quality assessment, and a linear probe on frozen DINOv3 features for origin detection, the study finds that while quality selection improves pass rates, zero Cheff-identified images pass both gates and 152 SynthCheX images still pass both pixel-level checks on unprocessed test cohorts. This work exposes a critical vulnerability in medical dataset intake pipelines: pixel-level screening alone cannot verify that an image truly comes from a clinical acquisition. As synthetic medical images become more realistic, hospitals, registries, and AI developers risk training on fabricated evidence, undermining the integrity of clinical AI systems and regulatory trust. The origin classifier rejected 4,677 of 5,000 SynthCheX images at a nominal 5% false-alarm rate but still flagged 110 of 2,297 real NIH radiographs, and its performance degraded under JPEG compression (AUROC dropped from 0.987 to 0.841). Applying the NIH-calibrated threshold to an external genuine cohort (Open-I) triggered false alarms on 568 of 957 authentic radiographs (59.4%), showing fragility to routine distribution shifts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Chest radiographs are among the most common medical images used to train diagnostic AI, and public datasets like NIH ChestX-ray14 and CheXpert have enabled major advances. ## [Meta-Diff adapts diffusion models via learned task-space transport maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31323) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Meta-Diff embeds source and target distributions in a shared low-dimensional task space, learns an affine transport map across paired training tasks, and generates from the transported source coordinate using a shared diffusion backbone. At deployment, it requires only source examples and the identity of a previously learned relation, avoiding target images or target-specific optimization. This approach could enable reliable few-shot domain adaptation for diffusion models without any target data, which is valuable when target images are scarce or private. It also introduces a reusable task-space relation concept that may generalize to other transfer learning scenarios. On held-out CIFAR-100 validation tasks, correct-source transport achieves 13.9% lower mean sliced Wasserstein distance than wrong-source transport, and in a one-target final-test comparison it achieves 14.7% lower distance than target-only adaptation and 32.7% lower than full fine-tuning. However, directly applying the known corruption to clean-source generations performs better on CIFAR, and requested-class identity remains weak; Office-Home shows domain alignment without reliable object identity, and clinical stress tests show no resolved source-specific advantage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are generative models that learn to denoise data gradually and have achieved strong results in image synthesis. Few-shot domain adaptation aims to adapt a model trained on a source domain to a target domain using only a few target examples, but often suffers from overfitting or requires target-specific optimization. Optimal transport provides a mathematical framework for mapping between distributions, and affine transport maps are a simple yet expressive family of such mappings.

## [Entity-Aware Evaluation Reveals OCR Rankings Shift for Historical Documents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33004) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.33004v3) proposes entity-aware evaluation of OCR systems for historical document transcription, using a benchmark of 175 documents from the Berrutti Archive with 5,209 manually annotated entity mentions. The study compares sequence-recognition OCR systems, OCR-specialized vision-language models, and general-purpose vision-language models, finding that OCR rankings depend on the evaluation criterion: Qwen 3.6 achieves the highest entity coverage across all tolerances, while some systems with lower exact-match coverage recover substantially when small errors are tolerated. This work shows that standard aggregate metrics like Character Error Rate (CER) do not fully capture whether meaningful information such as names, dates, and identifiers is preserved, which is critical for digitizing historical archives. It could influence how cultural heritage institutions and researchers select OCR systems, emphasizing entity-level evaluation over raw transcription accuracy. The benchmark includes 175 documents from the Berrutti Archive with 5,209 manually annotated entity mentions, to be released upon acceptance. The analysis examines entity preservation and recoverability under varying character-level edit tolerances, revealing that some systems' errors are localized character deviations rather than fundamentally incorrect entity transcriptions, while others remain unable to recover many entities even under relaxed matching.

## [Tensor-Structured Neural Network Reconstructs 3D Ocean Acoustic Fields from Sparse Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01680) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose TIDAL, a neural reconstructor that uses a tensor-structured representation derived from the Helmholtz equation and normal mode propagation to reconstruct 3D ocean acoustic fields from sparse measurements without requiring fully specified environmental parameters. Experiments across three structured sampling geometries show TIDAL achieves the best reconstruction performance and fastest inference among baselines. This approach bridges physics-informed learning and data-driven methods by embedding wave physics as an inductive bias rather than a hard constraint, potentially enabling practical ocean acoustic field reconstruction in scenarios where environmental parameters are uncertain or unavailable. It could benefit applications such as underwater sensing, sonar, and ocean monitoring. The representation, termed the S/c representation, takes the form of a rank-(L,L,1) block-term tensor decomposition under a low-rank specialization, which connects structured sampling patterns to sufficient conditions for field recovery. The neural learner removes the need for known model parameters required by conventional physics-based recovery methods, and benefits are shown under limited data and model capacity.

Tensor decompositions, such as CANDECOMP/PARAFAC and Tucker, are widely used in machine learning to represent high-dimensional data compactly and have been combined with implicit neural representations for efficient function modeling.

## [TeSCo Uses Textual Semantics for Robust Cross-View Object Correspondence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02356) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduce TeSCo, a framework that leverages textual descriptions of objects to improve cross-view object correspondence (CVOC) between videos from different viewpoints. TeSCo generates a textual expression of the target using a vision-language model and fuses it with visual features via a text-conditioned cross-modal fusion module and an iterative textual feature refinement module, achieving state-of-the-art results on Ego-Exo4D and HANDAL-X benchmarks. This work addresses a key challenge in computer vision—matching objects across drastically different viewpoints and appearances—by incorporating viewpoint-invariant textual semantics, which could enhance applications like surveillance, robotics, and augmented reality. It demonstrates the potential of multimodal (vision-language) approaches for robust correspondence in complex real-world scenarios. TeSCo introduces a text-conditioned cross-modal fusion (TCF) module that modulates visual features with text-guided information, and an iterative textual feature refinement (ITFR) module that progressively adjusts textual features using search-view context to ensure compatibility. The code is released at https://github.com/VAIL-Fan/TeSCo.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 5, 05:00

**Background**: Cross-view object correspondence (CVOC) is the task of associating the same object across videos captured from different viewpoints, such as ego-centric and exo-centric views. Traditional methods rely on visual cues, but they struggle when viewpoint changes and appearance variations are large. Textual descriptions can capture attributes like category, texture, and color that are invariant to viewpoint, offering complementary information to visual features.
